# iHYPE — Claude Code Instructions
# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# Claude Code reads this file on every turn — these rules are always active.
# Sessions start cold with no memory of prior work. This file is the memory.

---

## CRITICAL: Never replace — only update the UI layer

**Every session starts with zero memory of previous work.**
The backend wiring, auth logic, DB queries, and API calls in each file were
built across many sessions. They must NEVER be lost.

### Rule: When a .dc.html arrives, do a surgical UI update — not a rewrite

**Before editing any file**, state out loud:
> "Update the UI layer in `[file]` to match the new design — preserve all backend wiring."

Then follow this sequence:

1. **Read the existing `.tsx` file first** — understand what backend logic is already there
2. **State explicitly what will be preserved** — list auth gates, API calls, DB queries present
3. **Identify only what changed visually** — layout, copy, colors, new components
4. **Edit only those parts** — use the Edit tool, not Write/overwrite
5. **Preserve everything else** — API fetches, auth checks, state management, error handling, DB queries

If a page needs a full rewrite because the design is fundamentally different,
explicitly list every piece of backend logic being preserved before touching anything.

### Files with critical backend wiring — never overwrite blindly

| File | What's wired |
|---|---|
| `src/app/globals.css` (`:root` + the six theme blocks), `src/app/mmm.css` (dock · tabs · mini · pane wash · plate · strips · shelf), `src/app/mmm-console.css`, `scripts/measure-chrome.mjs` (was `measure-dock.mjs` until the dock went, 2026-09-22), `scripts/measure-datepicker.mjs` | **THE APPLE MUSIC SKIN (2026-09-05, DESIGN_SYNC row 346).** A value-only ground move plus one structural change: the console's MATERIALS became tokens. Three things to carry. **(1) `mmm-console.css` loads LAST and used to re-declare the ground, the accent, both radii and all four fonts as literals**, so a token change in globals.css reached nothing inside a pane — its token block is deleted and must not come back; the file keeps three paper rules that READ tokens. **(2) Both measurement harnesses hand-restated a token table and drifted**: `measure:dock` (now `measure:chrome`) reported the app broken (52px bar vs 55px table) because a NEW token was absent from its private `:root` and an invalid `border-top` has no width. They slice globals.css's real `:root` by landmark now; a harness that copies tokens is the same defect as one that copies geometry. **(3) The accent is `#e0263e`, not Apple's `#fa2d48`**, because white must clear 4.5:1 on it and the audit checks — `--ink-on-accent` is WHITE for the first time. `--ink-3` is `#545458`, not systemGray, for the same reason on `--bg-4`. Tab labels stay 11px tracked mono: 15px is the body floor and the eyebrow exemption is the only way under it. The launch colour lives in FOUR places outside the stylesheet — `capacitor.config.ts` (twice), the Android launcher XML, `layout.tsx` `themeColor`, `manifest.json` — and two guard tests refuse a ground move that leaves them behind. **A FIFTH was found on 2026-09-14 (DESIGN_SYNC row 416): the service worker's hand-written offline page in `public/sw.js`, still on the DS8 navy gradient two conversions later, because it is a `<style>` block inside a template literal inside a worker and under no audit. Its values are literals by necessity (a worker cannot read `globals.css`) and each carries a `/* --token */` label; `wiring-guards.test.ts` holds every labelled value to `:root` and refuses an unlabelled hex. Paint a colour there only with its token named.**  **AMENDED the same day — the chrome is a MATERIAL and the console's control vocabulary is TOKENS (DESIGN_SYNC row 347; owner: "Keep redesigning for iOS transparency and better visibility").** `--material-thick/-regular/-thin` + `--material-blur` are UIKit's materials and the dock, the map's segmented control and search field, pin labels, the results list, the bottom sheet and the date popover paint from them — **except `--material-thin`, which is defined in all six theme blocks and read by nothing (measured 2026-09-15, DESIGN_SYNC row 486).** It is KEPT rather than deleted, because the trio mirrors a platform vocabulary and a two-thirds trio is the more confusing artefact; this sentence is corrected instead, so nobody goes looking for the surface that paints from it. Every literal the console wrote into mmm.css for a control — the 1.5px ink rule, the `2px 3px 0` pressed offset, the brass edge and lit top on a key, the dashed serif empty plate, the walnut mini-art, the full-bleed brass plate — is a `--key-*`/`--cta-*`/`--paper-*`/`--seg-*`/`--tile-*`/`--empty-*` token: iOS values in `:root`, the console's own literals restored in the four character themes. **Paint no material and no control finish into mmm.css again; set the token.** Three measured facts worth keeping: `--mmm-tab-h` is 50px (iOS's 49pt) so the chrome is 59/115 and nothing was re-measured by hand; `mmm-primitives.css`'s migrated-surface input rule is (0,2,1) and drew a second box inside the search field, so the field's input is reset at (0,3,0); and on a `--bg-2` card every `--bg-2` key disappears, which is why the profile card's ground is `--card-bg` (the page on iOS, a plate on the console).  **AMENDED again the same day — EVERY PAGE, and three real bugs (DESIGN_SYNC row 348; owner: "Keep working through all pages to align with this iOS theme").** `mmm-primitives.css` — the skin of the 40-odd migrated surfaces inside the shell — was still the console written as literals (brass edge, 5px, plate gradient, brass ring on every card, stat card and row); it reads the same `--plate-*`/`--key-*`/`--empty-*` tokens as `mmm.css` now. `--back-*` makes the sub-page back link iOS's chevron-and-word (the console's mono pill on its themes); `--site-orb-opacity`/`--site-grid-opacity`/`--auth-wash` turn the marketing ground and the auth wash off on iOS. **The bugs the walk found**: `TrustPolicyPage` called `useI18n()` from a server component, so `/delete-account` (the Play Store's deletion URL), `/copyright`, `/ticket-policy` and `/community-rules` all answered the error page in production — it is a client component now; a section-head link rule in `mmm-primitives.css` painted the venue dashboard's filled "+ Create Event" key teal on teal (label invisible, measured); `.mmm-advertiser a` painted the advertiser's two primary keys red on red. **A rule that colours every anchor in a container will eventually colour a filled button; exclude the keys.** Admin: the rail is 84px (15px labels clipped in 56), and an h1's `.meta` count takes the body face. |
| **Retired:** `RadioShowCreator.tsx` | Radio show creation is out as a product decision. What replaced its ad wiring is the always-on station: `resolveWeightedAdBreakClips()` mixes all four reach scopes, weighted local-most (`src/lib/ad-scope-mix.ts`, unit-tested), and `interleaveStationAds` places a break every 15 minutes of music — after the track that crosses the mark, so a song is never cut. |
| `src/lib/show-composer.ts` | `buildResolvedSequence()` is the real ad-interjection engine — resolves a `Show.productionPlan` into a playable sequence, auto-injecting AD breaks every `advertising.frequency` tracks. `ResolvedSequenceItem.adClipId` carries the underlying `ShowAdClip.clipId` through so a player can tell a real marketplace ad (`mkt_<Ad.id>`) apart from a placeholder (`0x...`, from `builtInAdClips`). **`advertising.clips` is now auto-filled server-side** (`src/lib/ad-clip-selection.ts`'s `resolveAdBreakClips()`, called from `POST /api/shows` and `PATCH /api/shows/[showId]`) whenever `advertising.enabled` is true but a DJ never manually confirmed an ad break in the timeline — previously "advertising enabled" with an empty `clips` array meant zero interjections ever actually happened, silently. |
| **Deleted:** `src/app/api/radio/ad-clips/route.ts` | **The route is GONE and this row used to describe it as live (corrected 2026-08-31).** It mapped `Ad` marketplace rows into `ShowAdClip` shape to feed `RadioShowCreator`'s ad-break picker, and it was removed with that component — correctly, since nothing could author a show any more. The mapping itself survives as `src/lib/ad-clip-selection.ts`'s `resolveWeightedAdBreakClips()`, which is what the always-on station calls. **CORRECTED 2026-09-03: that was the only path, and NOTHING CALLED IT.** This row used to say ads reach a listener through `getStationState()` (`src/lib/radioStation.ts`), served by `GET /api/radio/station` — "the path to test, and the only one". The route is real and still works; the problem is that `src/app/radio` was deleted with the show creator and the MMM shell has always read **`GET /api/stations/[slug]/tracks`**, which returned music and nothing else. So an advertiser could pass vetting, be charged, and be heard by nobody — measured by the acceptance walk, which had an APPROVED campaign and a station serving 0 breaks. That endpoint now mixes breaks in through the SAME `interleaveStationAds()` placement rules (never first, never last, never zero on a rotation of two or more), and **only `mkt_` clips**: `resolveWeightedAdBreakClips()` falls back to the built-in placeholder catalogue when no campaign has inventory, which is right for a fixture and wrong for a listener — a member would hear "Local spot A" and nobody would be billed. The lookup is caught to `[]`, so an ad failure can never take a station down. `StationTrack.adClipId` carries it to the client, `toQueue()` carries it into the player (with `mediaId: null`, so an ad can never write a `MediaListen` against an artist who did not perform it), and `GlobalMediaPlayer` fires `POST /api/ads/impression` once per clip when the break starts — the station counterpart of the same call in `ShowSequencePlayer`. Walk item 33 is the durable proof and it seeds its own two-track rotation, because a break has nowhere to go on a one-track station and the check would otherwise pass while measuring nothing. A stale comment in `src/lib/show-composer.ts` still names the old route as the source of the `mkt_` prefix; the prefix is real, the route is not. |

| `src/components/PagesHome.tsx` + `PageEditor.tsx` | **The profile editor, at `/app/me/profiles` — `/pages` no longer exists (2026-09-01, DESIGN_SYNC row 326).** **THERE IS NO AI IN THIS EDITOR AND THE GENERATOR IS NOT COMING BACK.** Whole-page generation went 2026-08-11 (DS8 replaced `templates/page-creator/` with a fixed per-type schema: it cost tokens on every page and still produced pages that had to be hand-edited into a common shape). The two survivors — refine, and import-from-website — went 2026-09-01 by owner instruction ("ditch AI page editor for artists and venues"), with `/api/page-builder/refine`, `/api/page-builder/import-website` and `src/lib/page-refine.ts`. This line used to say those two were "deliberately kept"; they are gone. `/api/page-builder` and `/api/page-builder/publish` were deleted the same day (see the "Deleted 2026-09-01" row above — this sentence used to say they "still exist, called by nothing", which contradicted that row; only `/api/page-builder/ad-recs` remains, and `PageRoleModules` calls it). **Four sections, and WHICH four depends on the profile type**: artist = About · Media · Press kit · Stats, venue = About · Event Info · Contact · Stats, fan = About · Stats. Do not re-add a shared section list filtered per type — that is exactly what grew to eight sections, because nothing in it said what a section was FOR. A section outside the type's set falls back to the first rather than rendering blank. **`PagesHome` draws NO tab control**: it registers its sections with `MmmStationsProvider` and the dock's dial tunes them (one dial per screen — it drew its own directly above the dock's until this date). Both registered values must be reference-stable or the registration loops; `t` is redeclared every render, so memoise on a label digest, never on `t`. **The tour calendar is `AvailabilityDate` rows with `kind`** (`AVAILABLE` | `TOUR`), not a second model and no longer the free-text `tourContent`. Booking availability is the same calendar, which is why it survived a spec that had nowhere to put it. **Albums are BUILT as the folder version (2026-09-02, DESIGN_SYNC row 333; owner: "Artists can have a graphic per track or one for an entire album it's their choice")**: `/api/albums` (list/create), `/api/albums/[albumId]` (rename/date/order, delete — deleting the folder leaves its tracks up as singles, `albumId` is `SetNull`), `/api/albums/[albumId]/artwork` (one cover, same magic-byte + image-vetting + public-prefix gates as track art), and `albumId` on `PATCH /api/artist-media/[hexId]` (must be the same profile's album). The Media section manages folders and files each track into one; the artist page's Albums tab groups tracks under their album with singles below; every playback surface (stations, deck, recommend, the artist page, `MmmPlayHere`) shows a track's own cover and falls back to the album's. NOT a release unit — no album page or hype count; those are deferred past alpha. **Release scheduling and removal (same day, DESIGN_SYNC row 334; owner: "Artists can prep an album or single release for a future date or launch it immediately totally up to them. They can remove tracks or albums or graphics as well or make changes.")**: `src/lib/release-schedule.ts` is the one place the arithmetic lives — a scheduled release is `{ isPublished: false, publishAt: <future> }` (the existing `publish-scheduled` cron flips it live and tells the artist), an immediate one `{ isPublished: true, publishAt: null }`, and **a HELD track is `isPublished: false` with NO `publishAt`** and is never touched by a release write: the artist cannot lift a copyright hold with a date picker (409 from the route, skipped by the album cascade). Upload takes `publishAt` and `albumId`; `PATCH /api/artist-media/[hexId]` takes `release` (`'now'` | ISO), `artworkUrl: null` (removes and deletes the stored cover) and `albumId` (filing into a dated folder takes the folder's date); `POST /api/artist-media/[hexId]/artwork` adds or replaces a cover. An album's `releasedOn` (date or ISO instant, or `'now'`) IS its tracks' release moment and cascades to every non-held track in the folder. **Track DELETE now removes the R2 object and the cover too** — before this the row went and the bytes stayed. Profile graphics have a Remove button (`set(field, null)`, saved with the form). **The Stats section is a fixed board for artists and venues, not a picker (2026-09-01, DESIGN_SYNC row 327)** — `src/lib/profile-stat-board.ts` via `GET /api/profile/stats` (owner-gated, `private, no-store`), one tile per line the owner specified: Listens · Completed listens · Follows · Hypes · Past events · Total tickets sold · Recommendations · Requests. Every figure is a count the schema stores, each query independently caught, and **`null` renders as a dash, never 0**. "Requests" is fan requests (`VenueConnectionRequest`) naming the artist, every status, and "Recommendations" is the distinct venues whose demand radar currently ranks them — the owner's product model, see the `fan-demand.ts` row (an earlier draft of this line the same day had the two the other way round and counted `BookingRequest`, which is now its own tile on the venue board only). The "pin up to 4" picker survives for FANS only, because `/app/fans/[slug]` is the one page that renders `pinnedStats` — the artist page never did, so an artist's picker chose for nobody. `pastShowWhere()` in `profile-detail.ts` is the complement of `upcomingShowWhere()` over the public statuses and is tested to partition them. **The tour calendar renders on `/app/artists/[slug]`'s Tour tab as of the same date** — it did not before: an artist could enter dates and no page read `AvailabilityDate`. Both kinds render on one list under the ticketed shows (`/artists/[slug]` is a redirect to the shell page, so that is the only artist page). Dates are UTC-midnight days and are formatted with `timeZone: 'UTC'`; a local-time read shifts every date to the evening before for anyone west of Greenwich. |

Driving eleven shell screens on the built worker at 393 and 1280 counted **54 controls under the floor before and 4 after** — and the 4 are the dock's own dial tab (design hardware, deliberately untouched) and a checkbox. The floors are now unconditional on `.mmm-chip`, `.mmm-row`, `.mmm-btn-ghost`, `.mmm-btn-primary`, `.mmm-search-input`, `.mmm-datepick-trigger`, `.mmm-search-result`, `.mmm-settings-pill`, `.mmm-deck-play` (38→44, centre held), `.mmm-deck-artist`, the Settings buttons, `.sub-tab`, `.button.small` and every control in the upload form; `mobile-fit.css` keeps its coarse-pointer set as the second copy of the same number. (3) `FollowButton` is painted by `.follow-btn` in `globals.css` with `data-variant`/`data-following`, so themes reach it and a row of equal actions can size it — it carried ~20 inline properties and rendered as a bare 634px label beside Hype until the profile card overrode it. | `src/app/mmm.css` (`.mmm-pane` padding reads `--mmm-chrome-top`) + `MmmSeedDeck.tsx` (`measure()` reads the DOCK ELEMENT) + the unconditional floor block at the end of `src/app/globals.css` + `scripts/audit-mobile.mjs` (`smallTaps` budget 28) | **THE VISUAL AUDIT (2026-09-10, DESIGN_SYNC row 375; owner: "Audit for visual improvements") — read that row for the ten defects and the numbers; carry these four rules.** **(1) Anything that must clear the chrome reads `--mmm-chrome-top`, never `--mmm-chrome-size`**: the dock rides `--mmm-dock-lift` on the cookie banner and a clearance that does not ride with it leaves the last ~170px of every list under the dock and the banner for as long as the banner is up — which every list did, measured. **(2) Never `parseFloat` a custom property that is a `calc()`.** `getComputedStyle(...).getPropertyValue('--mmm-chrome-top')` returns the SUBSTITUTED STRING, `"calc(calc(50px + 3px * 2 + 3px + 0px) + 237px)"`, not a length; `parseFloat` makes it NaN, `|| 0` makes it nothing, and the deck's reservation had been 20px since the day its read moved onto `.mmm-frame` — the comment recording that move as a fix was the trap. Measure the element: `innerHeight − dock.getBoundingClientRect().top` is "how much of the bottom is not mine" with lift and safe area included, and it needs re-measuring when the banner writes or removes `--consent-inset`, which is not a resize. **(3) A floor in a shared block is a proposal; the rule that owns the size decides.** The 44px floor is unconditional for the public and migrated surfaces now (row 338 did the shell; `mobile-fit.css`'s coarse-pointer set was the ONLY copy for everything else, so a mouse measured 17–42px controls on every public page), but three controls kept their size until the floor went where the size lived: `.nav-join-button.button` set `min-height: 0` to match a template's anchor exactly and its two classes beat everything, `.fan-entry-nav a` set 42, and `.nav-logo` already owned `display`/`align-items` so `audit:css` rightly refused a re-declaration. `audit:mobile` had read 45 against its budget of 39 on every deploy — under `continue-on-error`, so the ratchet slipped by six unnoticed; it reads 23 against 28 now. **(4) An unstyled class is a defect the type system cannot see**: `audit:unstyled` found 33 classes rendered by markup and defined by nothing, 32 with no JS reference either, and three of them were 25px browser-default buttons on the TICKET page (`btn`, whose `.btn-primary` exists only inside the event creator's own `<style>` block) and two more on the believer and promote share controls. When a surface needs a button, it wears `.mmm-btn-primary`/`.mmm-btn-ghost`; a status wears `.mmm-profile-badge`. **The instrument that found all of it is `scratchpad`-only and its false positives are recorded in the row** — `sr-only` text reads as clipped, an offscreen honeypot reads as a 25px input, a decorative element under `overflow: hidden` reads as a spill, and `wrangler dev`'s esbuild service deadlocks under ~100 sequential page loads. `measure:layout` and `audit:mobile` remain the durable instruments; a census probe that counts controls under 44px signed IN, across the shell, is the one this repository still lacks. |

run 34914298500 charged a real test-mode sponsorship (`pi_3UFkUiLpb7XqNjgc1lhA6hht`, 2500c) and created `acct_1UFkUnLuDggaJeBJ`, which the key alone cannot do — the campaign went APPROVED off a webhook envelope signed with the webhook secret, so both are proved by the same run. **The four BROKEN were the nightly's OWN env block**, unreachable until then: `FEATURE_ENABLE_TICKET_PAYMENTS` was pinned `'false'` (production carries `"true"`), so the moment the ticket items stopped blocking they ran into `getPaymentProcessingReadiness()`'s 503; Set it and `EMAIL_FROM` on BOTH the worker and the walk (the nightly's one env block does; the harness forwards them). **(2) The admin step-up gate is satisfied the way the session is**: seeded, not performed. `requireRecentAdminReauth` reads `admin_reauth:<userId>` from KV; the walk writes that key with `wrangler kv key put --local` into the harness's persist dir, published in a sidecar file because it is a random temp path — to BOTH namespaces (the binding has `id` and `preview_id`), awaited rather than `spawnSync` (a blocked loop misses the worker closing an idle keep-alive socket, and undici will not retry a PATCH on a dead one: "fetch failed"), and asserting the 401 first so the gate is what is measured. Outside the harness the item BLOCKS with the reason. **(3) `freeUseEnabled` is promoter consent, not visibility** — it defaults off, the upload form leaves it off, and search had required it on every track since April, so a fan searching for a song found almost nothing. Gone from search and from `/api/profile/[slug]`'s track list; `releasedMediaWhere()` + `discoverable` is the whole test. **Do not gate a public listing on it again.** **(4) `POST /api/content-reports` files `media`; the moderation switch knew `track`** — approve marked the report ACTIONED and touched nothing. Both names resolve now, by hexId or id. **(5) The offline wallet still did not work on first view, and the spec is what found it.** `e2e/offline-ticket.spec.ts` (Playwright, not the walk — it needs a registered worker and a network that can be cut) warms from the wallet on a SECOND load (the warmer posts to a controller that is null on the first), cuts the network and opens a ticket never rendered before. First run: the HTML was in the cache with the code inside it, and the page read "Loading…" — the shell's shared chunks were cached from earlier visits, so React hydrated, could not load this route's own chunk, and replaced the server-rendered ticket with the fallback. **The 09-03 fix cached the page; the page is not the ticket without its scripts.** `WARM_TICKETS` now warms every `/_next/static` script and stylesheet the HTML names into the static cache too (`warmPageAssets` in `public/sw.js`). The script now separates the two states it was conflating: **ABSENT exits 2** (honest degradation, links open in the browser) and **MALFORMED still exits 1** (both platforms cache the rejection for days). The nightly treats 2 as a reported gap. **A check that cannot pass is not a gate, it is a wall in front of the instruments.** **(2) The walk step was missing `PLAYWRIGHT_AUTH_COOKIE_SECURE`** — workerd serves a production build, so the session cookie is `__Secure-` prefixed; without it every authenticated request answers 401 and the walk reports **22 failures that are all one variable**. `e2e-workerd.mjs` sets it for the Playwright child it spawns, which is why the browser suites never needed it; in `--serve` mode the walk is a separate process and inherits nothing. **(3) A placeholder Stripe key ran the money items instead of blocking them** — the preflight checked the `sk_test_` prefix only, and this sandbox's placeholder is literally `sk_test_` plus a non-ASCII ellipsis, nine characters, prefix intact. So the key read as configured, Stripe refused everything, and two money items reported FAIL beside nine siblings reporting BLOCK for the same condition. **That is the trap that let earlier sessions believe they had rehearsed against Stripe.** Now refused loudly at preflight, naming the length and the non-ASCII character. Also: a **429 from `/api/register` reports BLOCKED, not FAIL** — the limiter is keyed per client IP and the walk arrives from loopback, so a second run against the same worker is one caller hammering signup, landing on the four items that open the walk and making a re-run look catastrophic. |
| `scripts/measure-layout.mts` (`--taps`) + `npm run measure:taps` (`--max=9`) + `src/lib/__tests__/tap-census-probe.test.ts` | **THE SIGNED-IN TAP-TARGET CENSUS — the instrument row 375 closes by saying this repository lacks (2026-09-12, DESIGN_SYNC row 400).** `audit:mobile` measures SIGNED-OUT pages at 393; the 44px floors rows 338 and 375 added live inside the shell, so MOBILE.md's plainest rule had nothing measuring it where it mostly applies. **Its first run found 20 controls under 44x44, every one at 860 or 1280 and none at 375 or 393.** The dock was the finding: `.site-dock-play` carried its floor inside the `(pointer: coarse)` block while the dock's centre and right groups are hidden below 768px — **a floor written for touch, on controls only a mouse can reach**, which is row 375's own sentence surviving the pass that wrote it. **20 → 9, and the nine are a decision**: flooring both dimensions gave 980px of row in an 860px window, and four measured variants also showed the bar has ALWAYS overflowed at 860 with the height-only floor the one that fits. Height is unconditional on all twelve; the nine narrow ones stay counted, because **a number reachable by breaking a layout is one people learn to game**. **AMENDED 2026-09-14 (DESIGN_SYNC row 466) — THE ZERO ABOVE IS EARNED ON THE NUMERATOR ONLY, AND THAT WAS THE WRONG AXIS.** An UNMEASURED route/width pair adds no key to the capture, so both refusals were ABSOLUTE and could not tell a census of the whole app from a census of whatever loaded: a run came back with **41 of 92 pairs refused by the connection and still printed a score**. It names its denominator now — `pairsPlanned`, from the `--only`-FILTERED list so a scoped run is judged against its own plan, counting pairs **PLANNED not attempted** (a browser that dies mid-width never tries the rest, which is exactly the run to refuse), printed on **every** run because the 90% line cannot see three or four quietly missing surfaces. Quote `N of M` alongside the score, never the score alone. **AMENDED 2026-09-14 (DESIGN_SYNC row 465) — A HEIGHT-ONLY FLOOR CANNOT SEE A CONTROL THAT WENT NARROW.** The census read 10 against the budget of 9, and the tenth was the show page's “Edit event →” organiser link at **43.3×72** at 375 and 393px: four organiser links in a flex row with no `flexWrap` (while its own parent one line above wraps), so at phone width each item shrinks to min-content and wraps INTERNALLY — and a control that got TALLER because it got too thin satisfies `min-height` perfectly. The shortest of the four labels is the one that crossed; the others are saved only by being longer. **Do not read “row 400 floors the dock's height only” as a rule about floors** — it is a record of that row's measured constraint (a width floor gave 980px of row in an 860px window), and a column that wraps has no such conflict, so the floor there is square. Back to **9/9, 1,712 sightings, tightest clearing control exactly 44px**. **THE TRAP TO KNOW: the probe is a TEMPLATE LITERAL, so JS resolves every escape before the browser sees it** — `/inset\(\s*50%/` arrives as `/inset(s*50%/` (a syntax error) and `/\s+/` arrives as `/s+/`, which is NOT an error and splits class names on the letter s forever; `'\n'` in a string becomes a real newline and terminates it. It is written with **no backslashes at all**, whitespace built from char codes, and a test asserts that. A zero is earned rather than assumed: it counts every control CONSIDERED (1,383), refuses a figure when that is implausibly low, and names the tightest control that clears the floor. One browser per WIDTH, because the first full run lost its Chromium two thirds in and discarded the whole hour. An inline link in a sentence is counted separately, never as a defect; a checkbox is measured by its LABEL; sr-only and far-offscreen are excluded but **anything merely scrolled out of view is NOT** — the strips and shelves scroll sideways and their later items are reachable. Not in CI, same reason as `measure:layout` and `audit:mobile`. |
| `tsconfig.scripts.json` + `npm run typecheck:scripts` (CI and nightly) | **THE `.mts` INSTRUMENTS ARE TYPECHECKED BY THIS CONFIG AND BY NOTHING ELSE (2026-09-14, DESIGN_SYNC row 428).** `tsconfig.json` includes `**/*.ts`, which never matched `scripts/*.mts` — the acceptance walk, `measure-layout`, `feature-health` — and `tsx` strips types without checking them, so four errors sat in the instruments unread (a payable's `role`, a column that has never existed; two `null`-narrowed readouts typed `never`). Keep the scripts OUT of `tsconfig.json`'s include: `next build` typechecks that config, and an instrument must not be able to fail a production deploy. A new `.mts` script is covered automatically; a `let x` declared as nullable `T`, initialised to `null` and assigned inside a callback reads as `never` later — initialise it with `null as` nullable `T` instead. |

This classifies every `page.tsx` three ways: **MMM** (`/app/*`, 9), **legacy app shell** (per `SHELL_ROUTES`, **50 — the retirement target**) and **outside both** (58: marketing, legal, auth, admin). That third group is not work-in-progress and is deliberately excluded from the ratchet — a logged-out landing page is not the app, and wrapping it in MMM would be a mistake rather than progress. Conflating them is what makes "108 pages to port" sound impossible when the real figure is 51. `--max-legacy=N` is a ratchet: **that number must go DOWN, never up.** It parses `SHELL_ROUTES` out of `app-nav.ts` rather than duplicating it, and **exits 1 if it parses zero entries** — a format change must break the script rather than silently reclassify every route as OUTSIDE and report a triumphant drop. **CORRECTED 2026-09-14 (DESIGN_SYNC row 433): that refusal no longer exists.** `app-nav.ts` went with the legacy shell and the retired prefixes are a constant in the script, so the only collection left to refuse is the route tree itself — and over an empty `src/app` the script inventoried 0 routes and PASSED. It exits 2 on zero pages now, as do `audit:css`, `audit:retro` and `audit:spacing`, and `empty-scan-refusal.test.ts` drives all four from an empty tree. "No inbound link" (22 routes) is a question, not a verdict: nearly all are deliberate redirect aliases for URLs already in sent email, app-store listings and bookmarks. |
| `src/components/ds/` + `scripts/vendor-ds-components.mjs` (`npm run vendor:ds` / `guard:ds`) | **The design system's components, generated rather than ported (2026-08-21, DESIGN_SYNC row 288).** The handoff README's one instruction is "**do not port the components by hand**", and this repo is why: the shipped `TunerDial` and the design system's are two different controls — three stations on the face vs one, step keys inside the lit face vs flanking hardware — both written from the same description months apart. So the seven console components are GENERATED from `design/handoff-console-2026-08-21/component-source/`, and `guard:ds` (a mandatory CI step) fails when a checked-in file stops matching that source. **An edit in `src/components/ds/` is reverted by the next run — that is the mechanism, not a bug.** A deliberate change goes into Claude Design and comes back in a re-vendored bundle. **Do not try to load `_ds_bundle.js` instead**: it is an IIFE reading `React`/`ReactDOM` off `window` (this app is RSC in a Worker — no global React, no `window` on the server), it **executes** its UI kits on load and mounts an `ops` kit into `#root`, and it is 731KB against a budgeted Worker bundle. Three components carry warnings in their own file headers and each is load-bearing: **`TicketQR` must never render a real ticket** (its own docstring: a QR-*shaped* matrix from the code string, "a representation of the door credential, not an encoder" — a scanner cannot read it); **`RotaryNav` is not adoptable** (8.5px cap readout, which the design system's own floor forbids, and at 15px "MUSIC" does not fit a 74px knob's cap); **`HypeButton` paints its active state as accent copy**, 2.48:1 on cream, against the bundle's own rule 2. Everything the generator changes is listed in `VENDOR_REPORT.md` — 21 px→rem conversions, 21 floor raises, 42 hardcoded colour literals, and **4 glyphs given U+FE0E** (`\u25c0`/`\u25b6` carry an emoji variant and iOS serves the colour glyph, so the joystick drew three blue rounded squares in a row on a real iPhone while the two triangles with no emoji variant came through correctly — FE0E requests TEXT presentation and is the opposite of the FE0F that DS8's no-emoji rule is about) — which is also why `src/components/ds/` is exempt from `audit:shell`'s colour check: the fix lives in another repository, exactly as with `audit:design`, and the exemption is a record rather than a silence. **Adopt by wrapping, never by forking.** The shipped `TunerDial` carries three fixes found by driving the built page, not by reading — a non-passive `wheel` listener (React's synthetic one is passive, so the design system's own `preventDefault` cannot fire and the page scrolls out from under the gesture), roving-tabindex focus restored after the re-render, and a caller-supplied `aria-label` — and those belong in a wrapper around the vendored component. |
| `scripts/audit-retro-coverage.mjs` + `npm run audit:retro` (**`--max=0`**) + `npm run app-links:write` / `check:app-links` | **The two ratchets that reached zero, and the files that must not be faked (2026-08-22, DESIGN_SYNC row 290).** `audit:retro` counts member-facing routes that paint a raw colour literal — **89 of 89 now paint only from tokens**, so `--max=0` is a gate rather than a countdown, and it is in CI's fast lane along with `audit:routes --max-legacy=0` (the legacy app shell has no rendered pages left). **Its `design-exempt` markers were silently useless until this date**: `strip()` collapsed block comments to a single space, so the line numbering the exemption was decided against no longer matched the source and a marker written ABOVE the line it excuses was deleted before it could be read. Comments now collapse to newlines. Fixing it unmasked `/aux-queue/[slug]` painting its own `#0a0a0a` ground — the one member-facing page still dark after the console conversion, and one people share. **CORRECTED 2026-09-04 — both association files ARE in the repository, as ROUTE HANDLERS, and have been for weeks.** This line said they were "deliberately NOT" here and generated by `npm run app-links:write`; `src/app/.well-known/apple-app-site-association/route.ts` and `src/app/.well-known/assetlinks.json/route.ts` read `APPLE_TEAM_ID` / `ANDROID_CERT_SHA256_FINGERPRINTS` through `readRuntimeEnv`, so both turn on with a `wrangler secret put` and no deploy — which is strictly better than a generated file, and is what to use. **The DOCTRINE below is right and the routes were violating it**: a placeholder is worse than an absent file, because with no file links simply open in the browser while a malformed one is cached as a verification FAILURE by both platforms for days. `assetlinks.json` was answering **HTTP 200 with an empty `sha256_cert_fingerprints` array** whenever the secret was unset — which Google fetches, matches against nothing, and caches as a failure. Both routes now **404 when their variable is unset**. **And neither could be read at all**: `apple-app-site-association` has no file extension, `isStaticAsset()` keys on a dot in the last segment, so production answered **307 to /login** and Apple's CDN — which does not follow redirects — could only ever have recorded the domain as unverified. `.well-known` is a public prefix by name now, with a test covering the extensionless case (the old test used `security.txt`, which has a dot, and therefore proved nothing). The generator refuses anything whose shape it cannot verify — a SHA-1 fingerprint pasted from the same Play Console screen is rejected by length — and `check:app-links` reads back what is on disk. |
| `scripts/measure-layout.mts` + `npm run measure:layout` | **The instrument that makes deleting retired CSS safe (2026-08-25).** Every other check here asks whether the code is *named* right or paints from tokens; none can answer "did this deletion move anything", and that is the only question that matters when removing old designs. The cascade means a rule you never touched can start winning, so the effect of a CSS deletion is not readable off the diff — both times this repo was caught, it was found by measuring: `.mmm-music-controls > .mmm-search-wrap` never applied because a comment sat inside its own selector (273px of empty cream), and the canonical `.authcard-*` block sat dead above a later one for months. Captures every visible element's box and paint across 19 routes × 4 widths (375 / 393 / 860 = `--mmm-frame-max` / 1280), signed IN via the e2e session fixture — which is the point, since `audit:mobile` covers only signed-out pages and the shells are what accumulated the retired CSS. Workflow is `--write=<file>`, delete, `--compare=<file> --strict`. **The baseline is deliberately NOT committed**: a checked-in geometry fixture churns on every legitimate design change, and a fixture that churns is one people regenerate without reading. **Verified in both directions, which is the whole of its value** — a null run (same code twice) reports 0 differences over 2,272 boxes, and three changes injected into a baseline are all caught with the right addresses. Getting the null run to zero took four fixes and each is a false positive the tool would otherwise have taught people to ignore: the cookie banner renders from an effect and so is up in one run and not the next (**and its height feeds `--mmm-dock-lift`, so it moves the dock**); an error page is a *successful* response measuring one `<pre>`, so a box-count floor rejects it rather than recording a perfect capture of nothing; the probe is passed as **source text** because esbuild's `keepNames` wraps local functions in a `__name()` helper that does not exist in the browser; and the tabs that fetch rows need `networkidle`, not a fixed delay, or one surface is measured at two different moments — that alone accounted for all 153 "changes" reported on 7 of 60 pairs. **THE "0 DIFFERENCES" CLAIM ABOVE STOPPED BEING TRUE WHEN THE ROUTE LIST GREW, AND NOTHING SAID SO (corrected 2026-09-13, DESIGN_SYNC row 403).** It was measured over 2,272 boxes and 15 routes; the list is 19 routes and ~9,100 boxes now, three of them authenticated surfaces added without re-verifying the guarantee everything downstream quotes. **(The list is 23 routes and 11,364 boxes as of 2026-09-14, DESIGN_SYNC row 414 — the 70/20/10 payout breakdown, the ticket page the offline wallet caches and the event creator joined it; a scoped null run over the nine ME routes read 0 differences over 5,288 boxes, and the tap census read 15/9 before the payout breakdown's six inline-styled controls were floored by class and 9/9 after. `--only=<substring>` scopes both instruments to the shared list, and a scoped `--taps` run that considers too few controls refuses to score — quote the full-list figure.)** Re-measured: the same build against itself reported **277 changes and 132 elements present in one run and not the other**. Four causes were found and removed — **(1)** the seeded show's start time was `Date.now() + 7 days` and the page renders `{date} · {time}`, so two runs measured two different strings; it is pinned to UTC 20:00. **(2)** the seeded ORDER migrates through the demand sparkline, which buckets 12 hours into 8 bars measured back from now, so every 90 minutes two bars trade `--heat-fire` for `--ink-a35`; `seedShowWithTicket` takes `orderCreatedAt` and the harness pins it 13 hours back, outside the window. **(3)** `networkidle` + a flat 300ms is not a settled page — `.site-shell` was captured at HEIGHT 0 — so the wait is now for STILLNESS: element count, document height and text length, three identical readings 350ms apart, and text is not redundant because a MUSIC tab draws the same `.mmm-empty` element for "loading" and for "nothing here". **(4)** four of the twenty routes had no page at all (`/tickets`, `/settings`, `/payouts`, `/search` are `redirects()` lines) and `/settings` was a straight DUPLICATE of `/app/me/settings`, 218 boxes measured twice at every width; the destinations are named now. **That residual is CLOSED as of 2026-09-13 (DESIGN_SYNC row 408)**: it was the Playlists tab reading `?? []` on all four of its endpoints, so a failed `/api/stations` removed the whole shelf (`MmmShelf` returns null on zero tiles) and a failed library read rendered the empty plate's "Nothing saved yet" — a claim about the member on top of a fetch that never landed. All three states are distinguished now and two e2e tests fault the network to prove it. **The rule it leaves: an empty state is a CLAIM, so it is only ever rendered over reads that actually succeeded.** **The server panes were held to the same rule on 2026-09-14 (DESIGN_SYNC row 448)**: six pages read their lists with `.catch(() => [])` and rendered "has not published any releases yet" / "You're all caught up" over a failed query; `src/lib/read-list.ts`'s `readList()` keeps the failure as `null` (the counters' convention), `ProfilePanel` draws an `unavailable` sentence in place of the empty claim, and `server-page-empty-claims.test.ts` refuses the idiom in any member-facing server page. `.catch(() => null)` on a single-object read that only sets a control's default state is not the defect. **The operator console was the same defect on a larger scale (row 449)**: `/admin` caught eleven counts to `0` and seventeen lists to `[]`, so a dead database read as 0 users, "$0.00" captured and "No reports yet" — against a rule five of its own sibling pages state; `readValue()` keeps a count's failure as `null` too, the guard refuses `.catch(() => 0)` beside `.catch(() => [])`, and admin is no longer skipped. **And the client half (row 450)**: seven client components swallowed a fetch failure and drew their empty sentence — `PagesHome` MANUFACTURED `{ myProfiles: [] }` on failure and read "No pages yet" about profiles the artist has; Settings rendered every default with Save enabled, which would have written those defaults over the member's preferences — and three more hung on "Loading…" for ever. Each carries a load-failure state now; `client-empty-claims.test.ts` refuses the manufactured-empty shape outright and, as a heuristic with a reasoned allowlist, requires a failure state wherever a swallowed fetch sits beside an empty sentence. A loading state with no failure exit is a claim deferred indefinitely. **And the API half (row 451)**: four read routes caught their PRIMARY read to `[]` and answered 200 — `{ rows: [] }`, `{ ready: true, tracks: [] }`, three empty venue groups, a sparkline of zeros — so no client could branch; each answers 503 `READ_UNAVAILABLE` with `Retry-After` from `src/lib/read-unavailable.ts` now. The read a response IS fails loudly; a read that only shapes it (a ranking signal, a picker's genre list) may still degrade to empty, with a comment saying so. **Read a single clean run as evidence about that run, not as a property of the tool**, and when a comparison reports a whole SECTION appearing or disappearing rather than boxes moving, suspect the capture before the edit. `/` and `/login` are **not** in the route list: both resolve to `WORKBENCH_PATH` under a session, so measuring them authenticated measures a redirect. **`/app/me/advertising` joined the list on 2026-09-13 (DESIGN_SYNC row 409) and needed a fixture to be worth measuring**: nothing in this repository had ever seeded an `AdvertiserAccount`, so the route would have measured its "no account yet" state — four sentences, no dashboard. `seedSessionCookie` takes an `advertiser` now, with **every campaign date pinned to an absolute instant and no `AdImpression` rows at all** (the chart buckets the last 14 days against `Date.now()`, and with no rows every bar sits at its 2px floor, which is the only height independent of when the capture ran). The residual is the page's own: its two axis labels turn over at UTC midnight. Not in CI, for the same reason `audit:mobile` is not — it needs a built worker and a database, and the browser stages are gated on cost. |
| `scripts/audit-spacing.mjs` + `npm run audit:spacing` | **There are two spacing scales and only one is written down (2026-08-22).** The design system publishes a 4px scale (`--space-1`…`--space-16`), `globals.css` mirrors it, and **`src/` reads not one rung** — every spacing value in the app is a literal. The interesting part is that the literals are not random: **90% land on a 2px step and only 57% on the published 4px step**, with 6, 10, 14, 18, 22, 26, 30, 34, 38, 42 and 46 all in live use. So the app runs a coherent, undocumented 2px grid beside the documented 4px one; this is not 941 typos. **941 near-miss literals** (within 2px of a rung, competing with it) across 89 files — 8px×279 against 10px×185, 12px×237 against 14px×197, 16px×202 against 18px×112 — and half of them sit in five files (`mmm.css` 159 of its 283, `AdvertisePage.tsx` 127, `globals.css` 102, `PagesHome.tsx` 50, `PageEditor.tsx` 35). It also reports **one visual role spaced many ways** — chips 9 ways, buttons 9, sheets 8, fields and rows 7, cards 5 — which is the rhythm version of the problem `shell-surfaces.css` exists to fix for paint, and which nothing else checks. **Advisory and NOT in CI on purpose:** a gate at 941 locks in a number nobody has agreed to pay down; add `--max=941` to the fast lane once a first tranche is converted, then lower it. **CORRECTED 2026-09-14 (DESIGN_SYNC row 433): it IS in CI, as a CEILING — `--max=900` since 2026-09-11 (#907), lowered to 896 on 2026-09-14, and to **894 on 2026-09-15** when the count read 894 across 315 files.** **Read the budget off `ci.yml`, never off this line** — that is how it misled a session on 2026-09-15: the row still said 896, a change measured itself green at 896 locally, and CI refused it at 894. The ratchet's own comment says LOWER IT EVERY TIME THE COUNT FALLS, so this number moves more often than this file does, and a change that adds a near-miss literal fixes its own rather than raising the ceiling. The advisory-by-design instrument is `audit:design`, whose fixes live in another repository; this row and the nightly's own header both went on calling spacing advisory for three days after it became a gate, and the header called `audit:shell --strict` advisory for six weeks. Two checks in its first version were wrong and are worth knowing about: it counted `padding: 12px 20px` as "12 disagrees with 20" (one decision, not two) and it flagged a class carrying different padding at different breakpoints (correct, not drift) — which is why report 2 groups by ROLE rather than by selector. Values equidistant from two rungs (6, 10, 14, 18, 22) are marked `?`: for those, picking a rung is a decision, not a correction. **1,958 of the literals are inline `style={{ padding: 18 }}`** and cannot be corrected centrally. |
| `scripts/audit-design-templates.mjs` + `npm run audit:design` | **The audit that runs the other way (2026-08-14).** Every other check here tests the CODE against the design system. This one tests the DESIGN SYSTEM against `ADHERENCE.md`, because the assumption under all the others turned out to be false: **31 of the 41 DS8 templates break rules the design system itself publishes** — 71 emoji across 17 templates (§29 bans them outright), a **DJ role in 7** (§4: deleted from the product 2026-08-06, and `role-dashboard`/`profile-page` still ship a full `isDj` variant), promoter-as-a-role in 7 (§3), and 44 instances of white on the accent fill (§32: 3.27:1, fails AA). This matters more than a normal styling defect because **the templates are the source of truth**: a session told to "apply the template" faithfully will re-introduce a deleted role, and be right to. **Fix these in Claude Design and re-vendor — never by inventing corrections in the .tsx**, which the next session undoes. Deliberately **advisory, and it should stay advisory**: the fix lives in another repository, so a red build here could not be cleared by anyone working in this one. The emoji check flags `U+1F000–1FAFF` plus `U+2600–27BF` **only when followed by `U+FE0F`** — that range is text-presentation by default (`✕` is U+2715) and the design system explicitly allows those glyphs, so a naive check reported `♡` and `✓` as violations and would have been switched off within a day. **`templates/landing/` is the one to know about:** it pitches an open "Join beta — free" signup while the product is invite-only and the live page deliberately says so, plus four emoji and a DJ segment. Translating it faithfully would REGRESS `/`. |
| `src/lib/show-detail.ts` | **The rules two copies of the same page must not disagree about (2026-08-14).** A show renders at `/shows/[slug]` (public — the URL people share to sell tickets, must render logged out) and at `/app/shows/[slug]` (the same show in the signed-in shell). They **cannot be collapsed into one route** for that reason, so the only question is whether they agree — and they did not. `canViewShow()` is the one that had already broken: the public page has always let a show's **creator and an admin preview a DRAFT**, while the shell copy hid it from everyone, so an organiser opening their own unpublished show from the shell was told it did not exist. (The shell copy had also shipped promoter attribution as `null`, silently dropping the 10% credit for every purchase made in the new shell — fixed earlier, recorded in that file's own comment. Same class of bug.) `isTicketingOpen()`, `resolveShowSplits()` and `formatShowWhere()` moved with it. Pure and tested; each page keeps its own query and its own layout, because the design gives the shell a deliberately slimmer surface — what neither gets is its own opinion about who may see a draft. **`Show.promoterPayoutPercent` is `Int @default(10)`, never null** — the first draft of this module typed it nullable on the reasoning that a show without a promoter carries no share, and the compiler disagreed. **The other four pairs were done the same day** (`src/lib/profile-detail.ts`, `src/lib/track-detail.ts`), and the profile pair had drifted in BOTH directions at once: the **public** artist and venue pages filtered `status === 'LIVE' || startsAt >= now` over a query with no status gate, so a **DRAFT show was listed on a public profile** — disclosing the title, date and venue of a show whose own detail page 404s for everyone but its creator — and a CANCELED one was listed as upcoming; the **shell** copies queried `status in (SCHEDULED, LIVE) AND startsAt >= now`, which drops the show currently **on stage**, because a LIVE show that started an hour ago fails the clock test. `isUpcomingShow()` and `upcomingShowWhere()` are the one rule, and the query form has to stay a **disjunction** — written as `status in (...) AND startsAt >= now` it reads identically and is the bug. The track pair agreed already; `resolveCopyrightState()` shares the STATE only, because the public page translates its labels through `getServerT()` and the shell copy hardcodes English (a real gap, and separate work). **The playlist pair needs no module and got none**: `FanPlaylist` has no visibility column, so every playlist is public to anyone holding its id — including the OG card, which publishes the owner's username. That is the schema's decision, not a page's; a privacy model there is a product call with a migration attached. Note also what was deliberately NOT extracted — the type/demo gate all four profile pages share, because all four already agreed and a helper taking an injected `isDemoOwner` callback would be longer at every call site than the two checks it replaced. Duplication that has never drifted is not the problem; disagreement is. |
| **Deleted 2026-09-22:** `src/components/mmm/MmmDock.tsx` and `scripts/measure-dock.mjs` (the bottom dock and its probe are GONE — see the next row) + `MmmSectionStrip.tsx` (live) + `public/console/` | **THE DOCK IS DELETED (2026-09-22, DESIGN_SYNC row 500; owner: "The chrome button bottom nav is no longer the direction we're going for the design of this app. You can remove those components to save space"). Read everything below as the HISTORY of a bar that no longer ships: the four destinations are text links in the site header and the transport is a floating now-playing pill — the next row is the live one.** **THE MIDDLE ROAD: the console keeps its cabinet and loses its hardware (2026-09-04, DESIGN_SYNC row 341; owner: "let's do it — build and implement the middle ground").** The dock is a walnut TAB BAR carrying four labelled destinations — **Listen · Map · Tickets · Me** — plus, while a track is loaded, a mini player. The rotary knob, the tuner dial and the joystick are DELETED, and so is the nameplate. **Everything below about knobs, drums, needles, detents and `K=74` is history**; read it for the reasoning, never as a description of what ships. **The cabinet is NOT retired and must not be flattened** — walnut, brass lip, film grain, `--mmm-frame-max` centring and the safe-area contract are all unchanged, because the middle road's whole trade is keeping the console as a SURFACE and spending the rest of its distinctiveness on the map. **`--mmm-tab-h` (46px) is the origin of the geometry table now**, and the bar has TWO heights: **55px + safe area idle, 111px with a track loaded** (against the console dock's 105px, always). `--mmm-chrome-size` is derived once and has exactly ONE conditional override — the `:has(.mmm-dock[data-mini])` rule in `mmm.css`. Do not add a second. **The transport is still universal and still never inert**, and this is the wire most easily lost in a chrome rewrite: a tap resolves pause → the surface's registered intent (`MmmPlayIntent.tsx`) → turn the radio on. **AMENDED 2026-09-04 (owner: "remove radio tab on bottom it's already under listen") — the cold-start Radio key is DELETED and the transport is no longer universal.** It used to live in one of two places: the mini player's key when something is loaded, a **Radio** key in the tab row when nothing was. The objection is about the BAR, not the transport — Radio is a tab of LISTEN, so the key was a fifth destination-shaped control duplicating somewhere one tap away. **What it costs, stated because the rule it bends is real**: with nothing loaded there is now NO transport in the chrome, so a member on MAP, ME or a ticket has no play control until they start something from LISTEN. The mini player is unchanged and still carries it whenever a track IS loaded. `measure:dock` and `e2e/mmm-shell.spec.ts` were both asserting the OPPOSITE and would have failed the app for obeying the instruction; both now assert the key is absent, and what survives of the old rule is that there is never MORE than one transport. Do not re-add a play key to the tab row to "restore" the rule. **AND THE TEST THAT GUARDS THE TRANSPORT HAD NEVER RUN (2026-09-14, DESIGN_SYNC row 434)**: the shell spec's play-control tests skipped on every surface on every CI run since they were written — the seeded fan's `for-you` was empty by construction and the precondition was read through Playwright's cookie-less `request` fixture — and the first real run found the deck's invisible Skip stamp sitting over the play key (a tap fell through to the drag) and the Recommended AND Charts tabs with no play control at all since the joystick left — three of the five music tabs had lost their transport to the middle road. All three are fixed; the fixture warms a listener of its own (`seedPlayableStation`), each surface names its own control, and MAP and ME are asserted SILENT rather than skipped. A skip whose reason the fixture controls is a failure wearing a skip's clothes. **The track page and the shared playlist page were in the same state (row 435)** — both hand their rows to the dock through `MmmPlayHere`, which renders nothing, and neither had a key of its own; both carry `ReleasePlayButton` now and both are in the transport table. Five of the seven `/app` surfaces that could play something had lost the ability to start it over silence, and every one of them had a comment saying the dock would do it. **AND NO LISTEN WAS EVER COUNTED (2026-09-14, DESIGN_SYNC row 436)**: the player posts a track's `hexId` and `POST /api/media-listens` looked the row up by primary key (404 for every real completion), and the rows it did write for the walk were stored under the row id while `profile-insights`, `profile-stat-board`, the artist analytics page and the track page all count by `hexId`. `MediaListen.mediaId` IS the hexId now, the stored rows were migrated onto it, and walk item 10/11 reads the artist's Listens tile rather than a raw Prisma count — **a raw count proves a row exists; only a reader proves it is under the name the product looks for.** Asserting the completion in the shell spec then found three more refusals in the same call (a relative `mediaUrl` was a 400, a playlist row was addressed by its ITEM id, the deck's card carried no `mediaId`); `toQueue()` and `ReleasePlayButton` read `hexId`, then `mediaId`, then `id`, and the deck is the one surface whose completion the harness cannot observe, because it streams through the `/api/media` proxy, which refuses a loopback file by design. **And the two tables a fan fills by hand were worse (row 437)**: `FanFavoriteMedia.mediaId` and `FanPlaylistItem.mediaId` had five writers and three names for one track — the hexId, the asset's row id (a deck card, the deck's save action) and `radio-<hexId>` (an autoplay row) — while a like pressed on a deck card was a 400 the optimistic heart hid, because the route demanded an absolute URL and the deck plays a relative one. Every writer stores the hexId, the stored rows were migrated, `playableHref` admits a root-relative path, and a wiring guard refuses `mediaId: currentTrack.id`. **A column several surfaces write is named by one convention and a guard holds every writer to it.** The mini player shows `currentTrack` only, **never `nowPlaying`**: that is a server-resolved last listen with no url, so a play key under it would start a different song than the one named an inch above the thumb. **TICKETS is a promotion**: the wallet was a section inside ME (`?section=tickets`, which now forwards to `/app/tickets`), reachable by dragging a drum, and it is the surface with the least tolerance for being hard to find; `/app/me/tickets/<id>` did NOT move, because that URL is in sent email and in installed service-worker caches, and `moduleForPath` tests tickets before me for that reason. **A page with its own sections now DRAWS THEM** — `MmmSectionStrip`, mounted once at the top of the pane and once over the map (brass variant). The handoff's "one dial per screen, and it is the dock's" is reversed: it protected against two identical-looking controls, which is a consequence of the dial EXISTING, so check whether the chrome still carries one before re-deriving it. It does not. `MmmStations` and all four registrations are untouched — only the consumer moved. The dial's measured objection to strips (labels drift to 10-13px; two of six start off-screen at 393px) is ANSWERED, not ignored: fixed 13px tracked mono, the row scrolls rather than compressing, the active section is scrolled into view, and the right edge is masked so the scroll is visible. **Run `npm run measure:dock` after touching any of it** — it drives BOTH states and fails on a bar disagreeing with `--mmm-chrome-size`, a control under 44×44, a clipped label, more or fewer than one lit tab, a missing glyph, a transport that is absent or doubled, or a mini player title that does not ellipsise. It found three real defects on its first run, one of them in itself: the harness had no `box-sizing: border-box`, which the app has on every element, so it measured 56px tabs against a 46px table and reported the app broken. The photographed textures stay in `public/console/` (`walnut-v3.png`, `brushed.png`, `grain.png`); `brass-turned.png` was read by nothing and was deleted in the 2026-09-24 pre-launch scan (DESIGN_SYNC row 513). |
| `src/components/AdaptiveSiteHeader.tsx` (the four destinations, the commit rescue, `is-app`/`has-nav`) + `src/components/mmm/MmmNowPlaying.tsx` (new) + `--app-header-h` and the `.site-nav-link*` block in `src/app/globals.css` + the geometry table and the `.mmm-mini*` block in `src/app/mmm.css` + `scripts/measure-chrome.mjs` (`npm run measure:chrome`, in CI) | **THE BOTTOM DOCK IS GONE; NAVIGATION IS THE SITE HEADER AND THE ONLY CHROME IS A PILL THAT EXISTS WHILE A TRACK PLAYS (2026-09-22, DESIGN_SYNC row 500; owner: "The chrome button bottom nav is no longer the direction we're going for the design of this app. You can remove those components to save space", then, asked where navigation and the transport go: "Slim top header — the mark on the left, the four destinations as text links on the right, the same header the public pages already use, so the whole product has one header" and "Floating pill while playing — a compact pill at the bottom that appears only when a track is loaded and disappears when nothing is. No chrome the rest of the time").** **What was measured first**: three navigations for one signed-in member — the walnut dock inside `/app/*` (55px + safe area on every screen, 111 with a track), `SiteTabBar` along the bottom of every public page (Listen · Events · Dashboard, `rgba(13,13,13,.96)` literals no theme could reach, plus a `body { padding-bottom: 68px }` on EVERY page), and this header, which the shell HID under `html.mmm-locked`. `MmmDock.tsx` and `SiteTabBar.tsx` are deleted and `guard:design` asserts they stay deleted. **Four rules to carry.** **(1) The header is the navigation on every page a member reaches, including the shell.** `AdaptiveSiteHeader` draws `MMM_NAV` as `.site-nav-link`s with `aria-current` (a reader) and `data-on` (the paint); labels translate at the draw through `translateTabLabel`, keyed on module id, and the keys are `mmmNav.tab.*` (renamed from `mmmDock.tab.*` across all eleven dictionaries and six pages — a key namespace named after a deleted component is the stale record this file exists to refuse). The commit rescue (row 309's silent soft push from a detail route) moved into the header verbatim; the first bar that dropped it stranded a member on an artist page and CI caught it, so it is not optional. Inside `/app/*` the header is `is-app`: `--app-header-h` (54px) plus the top safe-area inset, no search field, no gear — the document there cannot scroll, so the 76→54 fold would never fire, Listen carries search and Me carries Settings. **`.mmm-frame` starts at `calc(var(--app-header-h) + env(safe-area-inset-top))` and the pane's top padding lost its safe-area term**: the header owns the Dynamic Island now, and a second pad would double it on a phone. Under 768px a signed-in header hides the search field and the gear in favour of the links (320px cannot hold a mark, four words, a field and a gear, and the tab bar this replaces carried neither); under 360px the links take 4px a side. **(2) The chrome is ZERO until something plays.** `--mmm-chrome-size` is `0px` in the geometry table and `calc(var(--mmm-mini-h) + var(--mmm-mini-gap) * 2)` under the ONE `.mmm-frame:has(.mmm-mini)` rule — the pill owns the answer; `--mmm-chrome-top` still adds `--mmm-dock-lift` (the cookie banner; the token kept the dock's name because the banner, the seed deck, `measure:layout` and two e2e files know it by that name), and everything that clears the chrome still reads `--mmm-chrome-top`. **`mmm-console.css`'s own `.mmm-pane { padding-bottom: calc(var(--mmm-chrome-size) …) }` is deleted**: it loaded LAST and read `chrome-size` where mmm.css reads `chrome-top`, so it had silently dropped the banner's lift from every pane — row 375's own defect, surviving in the one file that loads after the fix. `MmmSeedDeck.measure()` reads `.mmm-mini`, not `.mmm-dock`. **(3) The pill keeps the dock's mini player's wiring and its class names.** `wakeAudio` on the press, one `vibrate(6)`, the play-intent chain in the same order (`mmm-transport-chain.test.ts` pins it), `onExpand` to the full player; `.mmm-mini*` and `.mmm-key` on purpose, so the e2e suite, the probe and the deck's reservation read one vocabulary. Material is `--dock-*` tokens (iOS's translucent material by default, walnut and brass on the four character themes) — paint none of it in mmm.css. **(4) `measure:chrome` replaces `measure:dock` in CI and measures BOTH pieces, both states, six widths**: header height = token, frame top = header bottom, four links in one row inside the header's inner edge with none under 44×44 and none clipped, exactly one lit and it is "Listen", 15px not 11 (a destination is a control's name, not an eyebrow), no field and no gear inside the shell; pill height = `--mmm-mini-h`, `--mmm-chrome-size` = pill + 2 gaps playing and 0 idle, one width centred, three 44×44 keys, ellipsised title, square art. Its first run found the navigation **7px past the header's inner edge at 320px** — which is the 360px padding rule — and the pass's own new spacing literals pushed `audit:spacing` 884→886 before they were put on the scale (881 now; the ceiling in `ci.yml` follows the count). **What it costs, stated**: signed in on a PUBLIC page under 768px there is no header search any more (Listen is one tap away); the `SitePlayerDock` (`.site-dock`, the walnut "Now playing" bar on public pages) was NOT redesigned by this pass and was the one bottom bar left in the product — **it IS the same pill as of 2026-09-23 (DESIGN_SYNC row 508, owner: "do it")**: `.site-dock*` in `globals.css` mirrors `.mmm-mini` value for value from the `--dock-*` tokens, exists only while a track is loaded, rides `--consent-inset` like the shell's pill, and folds every control the bar carried into a sheet above the pill. Its phone rule had changed the day before: it was stacked `bottom: 60px` above the tab bar and floated there after the deletion (measured). **A bar that clears a sibling records the sibling's height; delete the sibling and grep for the number.** Verified on the built worker at 320/393/1280 against a scratch Postgres (the header, the pill over the deck, the map at full height) and by the browser suite's rewritten assertions; `e2e/mmm-shell.spec.ts`'s consent test now loads a track and measures the PILL riding the banner. |
| `src/components/mmm/MmmShelf.tsx` + the `.mmm-shelf-*` block in `src/app/mmm.css` | **Shelves (2026-09-04, DESIGN_SYNC row 342; owner: "do the shelves too").** One named row of artwork tiles that scrolls sideways — brass nameplate, optional See-all, the plate material every card uses. It replaced `.mmm-recents-*`, the same idea built once for one caller. **The rule to keep is what does NOT shelve, because getting it wrong loses content**: collections shelve (a station, an artist, a venue — one object with an identity), tracks do not, because fifty liked tracks behind a horizontal scroll is strictly worse than a vertical list. Three more stayed lists on purpose and each for its own reason: CHARTS because the rank is content and a tile cannot carry "3" and mean it, RECOMMENDED because the reason line is what stops a row reading as an advert and does not fit 104px, and the member's own PLAYLISTS because `OwnPlaylistRow` carries rename and delete and an editor is not a tile. **The fallback tile is the design at alpha** — six deterministic treatments keyed off the item's own id, so an artist gets the same tile everywhere, alternating three cabinet tones with three plate ones. It was six walnut variants first and rendered as six near-identical browns; that was found by rendering the real stylesheet, which is the only way it could have been. **Do not reach for the role hues to add variety** — they are semantic, so a track tile coloured venue-teal states something false, and `--accent` fails outright at 2.70:1 under cream ink. The edge fade appears only when the rail really overflows (`data-overflow`, measured in the component because CSS cannot ask), the same rule as `MmmSectionStrip` — a fade on a shelf that fits says "truncated" about a complete collection. |
| **History — the console dock's hardware (2026-08-24 → 2026-09-04)** | Kept because the figures were measured and the reasoning is reusable, NOT because any of it ships. It was a translation of `design/handoff-console/reference/console-dock/Console Dock.dc.html` at K=74: `--mmm-knob` 74px drove the whole table; statics in CSS and only live values (rotation, drum, tilt, speculars, the lamp's boot strike) written inline through refs, never React state at pointer frequency; three measured deviations (an 11px tracked-mono nameplate against the dc.html's 9px display face, two engraved step affordances plus a focusable centre tab so keyboard and Playwright had a non-drag way in, and `--mmm-drum-rest` stepping 26→24px under 393px because "Recommended" at 26px is 13px wider than the window). Two things from it are still live rules: **nothing on the dock reflects** (owner, twice — the fascia sheen tracked the handset's tilt and read as glare; what remains is occlusion and light SOURCES, never an image of the room), and the bar holds ONE width on every screen and centres above it. `git show 7a7ac0f7:src/components/mmm/MmmDock.tsx` and the same commit's `src/app/mmm.css` are the whole of it. |
| `src/components/mmm/MmmMap.tsx` (the map's chrome) + `src/lib/map-dates.ts` + `scripts/measure-datepicker.mjs` (`npm run measure:datepick`) | **The map starts where you are, and its only chrome is one search bar (2026-08-22).** Three controls retired in order and each removal has a rule attached. **(1) "Near me" is gone** — the owner's instruction is that the map "should always start where you are", so `getCurrentPosition` is now called on arrival and there is no button left to press. **That reverses a deliberate decision and the reasoning is worth keeping**: MOBILE.md says a capability is asked for "at the moment of use and never on launch", and this map IS the launch surface (`WORKBENCH_PATH` is `/app/map`). The instruction overrides that for this one capability. What is NOT reinstated is auto-opening the **primer sheet** — that was tried, and its scrim covered the whole shell before the member had touched anything, caught by the e2e that proves the map survives a module change by being unable to reach the nav at all. The OS prompt is system chrome and covers none of our DOM, which is the version of this that does not re-create that bug. It is asked **once** (guarded by a ref, asserted in e2e) and a refusal or timeout leaves the seeded county camera up, exactly as before anyone answered. **(2) The five date cards are gone**, replaced by a calendar popover inside the search field. `stripDays`/`describeSelection` went with them; `monthGrid`/`describeDayKeys` replace them, because a calendar reaches any date and a summary can no longer be derived by filtering a known five-item list. **The SET semantics are unchanged and are why this is not `<input type="date">`** — DS8 requires a Friday and a Sunday with nothing between them to be legal, a native date input is one value, and its platform picker cannot show which days are already chosen. **(3) The search bar now renders on EVERY layer**, where it was venues and artists only: dates belong to events, so a bar that skipped events would be a bar that never showed the control. `SearchableLayer`/`isSearchableLayer` are deleted with the exclusion. **`npm run measure:datepick` is the gate**, same method and same reason as `measure:dock` (the route is behind auth, so the picker is mounted against slices of the real stylesheet). It found both real defects on the way in, neither visible in the source: the popover's left edge at **-56px**, and the search field overflowing its own 420px column by **52px** above 620px — the design's `min(460px, 72vw)` is wrong in both directions and is now `min(460px, calc(100% - 24px))`. A day cell takes `min(44px, calc((100vw - 58px) / 7))` and not a flat 44px, because `aspect-ratio: 1` makes a too-narrow popover fail the other way: the cell holds its floor and the ROW spills, which clipped the whole Saturday column at 320px. |
| `src/app/mmm.css` (`.mmm-map-canvas` filter + `.mmm-map-layer::after`) | **The map is a sepia survey chart, and the order of the filter is the whole thing (2026-08-22).** The overlay is `templates/console-shell/map.html`'s ruled grid — a hairline every 33px in both directions, adopted verbatim, and the single cue that reads as an old chart rather than a modern map with a filter on it. **The tone is now full `sepia(1)`, and the earlier entry here arguing against sepia was wrong.** It concluded from correct measurements (the design's filter gives water `#fffee4` and land `#ffffff` — 1.02:1, 0° of hue separation, the harbour and the land the same colour) that sepia destroys a coastal map. The real fault is that `brightness` is applied **last**: the sepia matrix has row sums of 1.35 and 1.20, so a near-white tile clips at 255 in red and green and comes out white, and `brightness(1.04)` guarantees it. Darkening FIRST gives the tone somewhere to live — `brightness(.72) sepia(1) saturate(1.1) contrast(1.15)` measures water `#c8ae7f` against land `#ffe3a7`, **1.71:1** (1.69 once the overlay composites) against the 1.50:1 of the hue-preserving version it replaces. So full sepia separates land from water BETTER than not-sepia did, because the separation becomes tonal rather than chromatic, which is what a sepia print is. Verified by rendering all three variants in Chromium with the real voyager palette and this file's own grain on top. Pin ink measures 12.72:1 on land and 7.52:1 on water; `--accent` as a bare fill drops 2.70 → 2.28 on land, which is why it is a pin's stem and border and never its label. **Do not restore the light cast because the map looks dark** — it is darker on purpose, since that is the only way a white tile can hold a tone. |
| `src/app/mobile-fit.css` + `npm run audit:mobile` + the safe-area tokens in `shell.css`/`mmm.css` | **Phone fit (2026-08-06, DESIGN_SYNC row 272).** **iOS and Android are not a separate codebase** — `capacitor.config.ts` points a WebView at `https://ihype.org`, so the native apps render these stylesheets and a phone-fit bug is a CSS bug here, fixed by the next Cloudflare deploy with no store review. **`layout.tsx` sets `viewportFit: 'cover'` and `black-translucent`, which is a promise to handle the insets yourself** — and neither of the two newest shells did: `shell.css` and `mmm.css` had **zero** `env(safe-area-inset-*)` across 1,405 lines, so the app-shell header started behind the Dynamic Island and the MMM shell's logo trigger — the only control that navigates that whole shell — sat in the iOS home-indicator swipe zone. A browser hides this (Safari's own chrome absorbs it); the WebView does not, which is why it survived. The fix lives in the **geometry tokens** (`--shell-safe-top/-bottom` folded into `--shell-header-h` and both offsets; `--mmm-bottom` as the single origin for MMM's four bottom-anchored elements), never on an element — that is the same rule that says the geometry table lives once. Both resolve to `0px` off-device. **`mobile-fit.css` is gated on `(pointer: coarse)`, not on width** — a narrow desktop window is a mouse, an iPad is a finger — and every rule is a **floor** (`min-height`/`min-width`), so a control the design already sized keeps its geometry. Do not turn a floor into a size, and do not add a second copy of these rules inside a page. `input { font-size: max(16px, 1em) }` there is functional, not typographic: Safari zooms the page on a sub-16px focused input and never zooms back. **`npm run audit:mobile` measures what greps structurally cannot** — a rendered box too small to hit — by driving Chromium at 393×852 against production (or `--base=`), with three ratcheted budgets like `audit:css`'s `--max` (which reached 0 on 2026-08-31). **Not in CI on purpose**: the browser stages moved behind the `full-ci` label for cost, and an unlabelled browser stage would re-create that problem. **The type ramp is done as of 2026-08-19 (row 287) and this row's framing of it was wrong in a way worth keeping.** It said the sub-12px nodes were "inline px" and therefore "the type-ramp work, not a line of CSS". They were mostly lines of CSS. What made it look like a redesign is that type is written in **three** syntaxes here — the JSX `fontSize` prop, CSS `font-size` (in stylesheets AND in the `<style>` blocks 29 pages embed), and the **`font:` shorthand**, which carries the size as a bare length among its other components and which no `font-size` search can see. Item (1) below fixed only the first. Every non-eyebrow size now clears DS8's 12.5px body floor, `lint-source.mjs` fails the build on all three syntaxes **and now walks .css at all** (it collected only .ts/.tsx/.js/.mjs, so a third of the debt was outside every check it ran), and this script measures **3, from 246**. The DS8 mono-eyebrow exemption (mono family AND tracking >= .14em, down to 9px) is preserved — but note what went wrong with it, because it will happen again: it had leaked onto **content**, so error messages, hints, form field labels and the "or" divider on the only auth path were shipping at 9-10px because they matched the eyebrow SHAPE. A field label is a control's name, not metadata. **iOS also never got the reader's system text size** — Android's WebView scales web text with the OS font setting and WKWebView does not, so Dynamic Type never reached CSS and an older reader who had already enlarged text system-wide got nothing. Bridged via an `-apple-system-body` probe in `layout.tsx`; read its three guards before touching it, especially that it is a **floor** (`Math.max(1, …)`) and never a shrink. **Both items this row used to list as open are now closed, and the wording is kept so nobody re-opens them from memory.** (1) The inline `fontSize` px values — 691 of them across 77 files, of which 195 were under 12px — are **rem** as of 2026-08-12. They were never only a legibility problem: `shell.css` applies `--ihype-text-scale` to the ROOT font size, so `rem` follows Settings → Accessibility → Text size and `px` cannot, which meant that control did nothing on most of the app. Converted at px ÷ 16, so nothing moved at 100%. A `lint-source.mjs` rule now fails the build on a new inline px `fontSize`; Satori/ImageResponse, email HTML and the EPK print document are exempt, because px is correct there. (2) **`/launch`'s 95px overflow was not a layout bug.** It measures **0px** three independent ways: this script against a local build (its own 2026-08-11 note), an ad-hoc Chromium probe at 320/375/393, and a full `audit:mobile --base=` run.
