A production-content or stale-bundle artifact is the remaining hypothesis; if it returns on a production run, that is the signal. **`--base=http://localhost:…` did not work in any sandbox where `HTTPS_PROXY` is set until 2026-08-12** — Chromium bypasses loopback for a proxy it discovers from the environment but not for one passed explicitly, so every page was routed to the proxy and answered 405. The launch options now set `bypass`. **AMENDED 2026-09-25 (DESIGN_SYNC row 515) — IT RUNS `--strict` AFTER EVERY PRODUCTION DEPLOY NOW, AND ITS PAGE LIST FAILED ONE.** Deploy run 1014 published the Worker and then failed this step on a docs-only commit: `/status` redirects a signed-out visitor from inside the page, which under the root loading boundary is a 200 with a one-second meta refresh, and that refresh raced the script's 1.2 s settle. `/this-weekend` and `/community` were `redirects()` entries measuring `/login`. All three are out, and the loop refuses a page that lands elsewhere or carries a refresh before it settles, so a redirect fails by name on every run. **A page in this list must render itself signed out**; `audit-mobile-pages.test.ts` refuses a `redirects()` source. A failure here skips the cache purge, so read which page it names before re-running a deploy. |
| **Retired 2026-08-11:** the phone swipe shell | `MobileAppShell`, `MobileQuickGrid`, `MobileShellContext`, `RouteShellSlot`, `useDragCarousel` and `scrollLock` are deleted. They made `/listen`, `/shows` and `/pages` a permanently-mounted swipe carousel behind a full-screen quick grid at ≤768px, which is why `AppShell` had a three-clause stand-aside condition and why two components had an `isShellForeground` prop. DS8: "there is no separate mobile build." `/shows` and `/pages` now render their tab strip directly at every width (`.section-tabstrip`, renamed off the dead `mqg-` prefix), inside the one app shell. **Do not re-introduce a phone-only shell** — a phone gets the same shell as a desktop, plus `MobileBottomNav` and the floors in `mobile-fit.css`. One consequence worth knowing: `PullToRefresh` armed on `window.scrollY`, which is permanently 0 inside the shell (the document does not scroll; `.shell-content` does), so it now reads the nearest scrollable ancestor. |
| `src/app/globals.css` (`--f-d`) + `src/components/brand/IhypeMark.tsx` + `src/lib/brand-mark.ts` + `src/components/brand/BrandMarkImage.tsx` + `scripts/build-brand-assets.mts` (`npm run brand:assets`) + `src/app/api/og/palette.ts` + the `.ihype-mark` block + the uppercase rules in `mmm.css`/`globals.css`/`mmm-workflows.css` | **THE "LESS AI-GENERATED" PASS (2026-09-18; owner: "What can you do to make the entire site look less 'AI created'?" then "Do what you recommend. Alter the logo if you can as well"). Three measured findings, three changes.** **(1) THE DEFAULT THEME HAD NO TYPEFACE OF ITS OWN.** `:root` set BOTH `--f-d` and `--f-b` to the system stack, under a comment claiming Bricolage "stays loaded for the four character themes, which set --f-d/--f-b back" — and `var(--font-bricolage)` appeared **0 times in `src/` and 0 times in the built CSS**, because the four themes set `--f-d` to Anton, Chakra Petch, Playfair and Instrument Serif and not one of them sets Bricolage. So ~76KB of the family the handoff was chosen for sat first on the critical path of every request and painted nothing, while all 179 `var(--font-display)` sites rendered in the OS default — which is the single loudest generated-design signal there is, because system-ui is what a page renders in when nobody chose. It arrived honestly: the iOS skin (row 346) copied Apple Music, and copying Apple Music means copying SF Pro. **`--f-d` is Bricolage now and `--f-b` stays the system stack** — a list row, a tab label and a control should read as the platform's own; a heading is where the product sounds like itself. No new bytes. The four character themes still override `--f-d` and were never the problem. **Measure the `var()` count before believing a comment about which faces are live.** **(2) EVERYTHING WAS AN EYEBROW.** 60 `text-transform: uppercase` rules and ~40 at >=0.1em tracking, so the tracked-mono eyebrow was the texture of the whole product rather than a voice, and hierarchy flattened — when every label is metadata, none is. **21 rules converted to sentence case**, chosen by one rule: uppercase is for a STATUS, a UNIT or a RANK, never for content, a control's label or a sentence. Kept: badges, `VALID`, stat labels, `SEP`/`OCT`, `22S CLIP`, the named eyebrows. Converted: `RANKING PORTLAND`, `HAVE A TRANSFER CODE?`, `FRESH MUSIC FOR YOUR SEED MIX`, `19 MORE WAITING`, `VERIFICATION REQUIRED`, `<- MUSIC`, the datepicker's Clear/Done — and **`.field span` plus `.authcard-field > label`, form field labels, which row 287 already records as the mistake to avoid ("a field label is a control's name, not metadata")**. **THE CONSTRAINT THAT SHAPES THIS: the type floor is 15px and the mono-eyebrow exemption is the only way under it**, so converting an 11px eyebrow forces it to 15px. That is right for content and it is why **`.mmm-tab-label` was left alone** — a sentence-case tab label would be 15px, larger than any tab bar Apple ships, so converting trades one wrong reading for another. Row 346 reached the same conclusion; this pass re-derived it rather than inheriting it. **The rules were rewritten by PARSED BLOCK, not by a context window** — a `grep -B6` first pass attributed six classes to uppercase rules they do not own (`.mmm-deck-artist`, `.mmm-search-input`, the `.mmm-charter-*` family), which is this repository's recurring window-attribution trap. **(3) THE LOGO WAS A STICKER, AND THE WORDMARK WAS LETTERSPACED.** `logo-sticker-2026.png` is 1MB carrying five competing ideas — a rounded-square frame, a crowd silhouette over orange embers, a distressed brush wordmark, `.ORG`, and a ruled `LOCAL MUSIC` tagline. Measured against it: at the 54px it rendered at, the tagline and TLD are illegible; brush type over a crowd with sparks is the most generic live-music image there is; its colour is two grounds out of date (DS8 orange on near-black, against Apple Music red on white since row 346); and a domain inside a mark spends its best space on what the address bar says. `IhypeMark.tsx` replaces it — the `i` as an accent block with a SQUARE dot (drawn in SVG, because a glyph's stem is a property of whichever font loaded and a mark may not change shape on a font swap), then HYPE tight in the display face. The wordmark was **+0.17em tracked**; a wordmark is tight, and +0.17em at 800 weight read as four letters rather than one word. Deleted with it: a **four-stop rainbow hover** (`#ff4635 -> #ff3d87 -> #7c5cff -> #39d8df`, four raw literals no theme could reach — a purple-to-blue sweep is the most recognisable generated flourish there is) and the signed-in tile's `#070605` ground with a hover ring in `--role-venue`, a SEMANTIC role colour spent on the app's own home link. **The sticker is NOT deleted and the app icons are NOT changed**: both store builds ship icons built from it and are in review, so that is a store-asset change with a build attached. **What this pass did NOT do, stated because it is the remaining half:** the product ships **two** non-icon raster assets, both logos, so every card, tile and hero falls back to a computed gradient or a letter in a grey box. A music platform with no photograph of a person or a room reads as generated whatever the CSS does, and the fix is commissioning photography, not code.  **AMENDED 2026-09-18 — THE SWEEP THAT FOLLOWED (owner: "Do anything you think is necessary to maintain the app while updating to limit 'AI look'. It's our first big feedback item, and it was almost entirely universally stated by users").** 29 surfaces captured signed-out and signed-in at 393 and 1280 on the built worker, then ranked by weight. **(1) THE PASTEL GRADIENT WASH WAS PAINTED IN THREE PLACES AND A TOKEN COULD REACH NONE OF THEM.** `:root` carries `--site-orb-opacity: 0` under a comment saying the ambient ground is "off on iOS: Apple's pages are white" — and the `body` rule at the END of `globals.css` painted its own pair of radial orbs as literals (accent at 10%, `--role-venue` at 8%), `.fan-entry` painted a second pair positioned exactly where the landing hero's artwork sat, and `.fan-entry::before` drew a 1px teal-into-accent sweep under the header. So every page in the product washed pink and teal on the theme whose whole point is a white page, while the token that exists to stop it turned off an orb ELEMENT nobody could see. Measured, not reasoned: `getComputedStyle(body).backgroundImage` on `/app/me` at 1280 returned both gradients live with the opacity token at 0. **`--site-wash` is the fix — `none` in `:root`, the pair restored in the four character themes, read once by `body`** — and a census then found four more of the same device: `.rk-page` (a 1100x560 radial in the role tint, 2.9 MILLION pixels, on all three kit pages), `.rk-hero-glow` (a second 600px circle of the same hue on the same page), the public show page's hero card (a 140deg accent-into-`--role-venue` sweep, 685,000px, on the URL this product exists to share), `/launch`'s closing panel and `/walkthrough`'s two 600px blurred orbs. All gone; grounds and edges only. **Five of the seven spent `--role-venue` or `--role-fan` — SEMANTIC tokens meaning "this thing is a venue" — on page decoration**, which this file already records as a mistake twice. **(2) THE FOUR RECRUITING PAGES DREW A SECOND HEADER.** `RecruitingKitPage` opened with its own sticky `.rk-nav` carrying an `iHYPE` wordmark and a role CTA, under the sitewide `AdaptiveSiteHeader` that `layout.tsx` mounts on every page — two brand marks about 100px apart with two primary buttons, signed in and signed out, on every kit page since they were built. Deleted; the hero's own CTA is the same `/register?role=` link, so nothing was lost, and `navCta` went with it rather than staying as a field no reader uses. **(3) `/for-artists` AND `/for-venues` ADVERTISED A 24-HOUR PAYOUT, AND `PAYOUT_HOLD_DAYS` IS 10.** Two stat tiles ("Payout turnaround" / "Settlement turnaround") plus a checklist line promising "your 70% hits your account night of show" — the pre-hold behaviour, on the pages an artist and a venue read BEFORE signing up, translated into eleven languages. Row 474's fix went to the surfaces that COMPUTE a release date and never to the page that states the number as a headline. `audit:retired-claims` now matches both shapes and **was break-tested in both directions**: its first draft caught the stat tile and silently missed the checklist, because `\b` after `%` can never match (`%` and the space after it are both non-word), so the whole alternative was dead — a guard that reads as working. **(4) "1 MUSIC FANS" ON THE FRONT DOOR.** `LandingStats`' labels were four bare plural strings in an array, so nothing could agree with the count, and being string constants they were invisible to `audit:untranslated` and therefore English in eleven locales. Both forms now go through `t()` as literals. **(5) The landing hero is ONE COLUMN and carries no artwork.** Its right half was the 1MB sticker at 42vw under a blurred two-colour stage light — the same asset this row removed from the chrome, which made no sense to leave as the largest thing on the front page; the numbered `01/02/03` markers went too, since three capabilities are not a sequence. **A deliberate NON-change worth knowing about: the public show page's "Show details" table and "Why you're seeing this" block read like a debug dump — `Heuristics 1.3.0`, `Status: SCHEDULED`, `Promoter pool unassigned`, a ranking explanation on a page reached by direct link — and they were LEFT ALONE.** That block is the integrity ledger's disclosure (`FEED_HEURISTICS_VERSION`, GOV-001), and this file's own RADIO-001 note says deleting a disclosure makes a page less truthful. Restyling it is an owner's call; deleting it for looks is the mistake. **Two instrument notes.** `audit:css` counted `.nav-inner` as live only because `.rk-nav-inner` in a component's `<style>` block contains the substring — removing that nav unmasked three dead rules, so **re-measure after a deletion, always**. And a comment inside a `<style>{`...`}</style>` template literal may not contain a backtick: one closed the template and the build failed with `Property 'rk' does not exist on type`.  **AMENDED AGAIN 2026-09-18 — THE STICKER'S LAST LIVE SURFACE WAS THE LAUNCH SCREEN, AND "STORE ASSET" WAS THE WRONG REASON TO SKIP IT (owner: "It's ok to not have the audience in the logo, the mission is what's important (and the testers really hated the AI look and feel, including the logo)").** After the header and the landing hero, three references to `logo-sticker-2026.png` remained and were left as "store assets with a build attached". Two were: `public/icons/*` and both submitted binaries. **The third was `AppSplash.tsx`** — the installed-PWA launch screen, drawing the 1MB sticker at 144px on a pulsing rounded tile over a hardcoded `#fefdfe`. An installed app renders that from the WEB bundle, so it ships with a Cloudflare deploy and no store review, and it is the first thing a tester sees on every launch of the app they were asked to test. **A grep found all three; what was missing is asking what each hit COSTS to change, and the answer differed per hit.** It draws `IhypeMark size={52}` now (149x52, measured), the ground is `var(--bg)` rather than a near-white literal two grounds old, and the tile radius, the infinite pulse and its keyframe and reduced-motion rule are gone with the raster. **The icons are deliberately NOT regenerated**: the PWA set at `public/icons/*` is equally free to change, but changing it alone makes the installed icon disagree with the store icon, and a SQUARE lockup is a design decision with no source — `IhypeMark`'s own header records why the bare `i` on a red square was the weakest thing in the set. That needs a direction, not an inference. **The harness lesson is the reusable one**: the first render of the splash came back in a browser SERIF and looked like a live font bug. Bricolage is a `localFont` woff2, not Google Fonts, and pointing the harness at Google made `--font-bricolage` resolve to an uninstalled family — which satisfies the `var()` default and therefore DISCARDS the whole fallback list after it, leaving one unavailable family and no sans at all. Slice the real woff2 into the harness the way `measure:dock` slices the real `:root`; a harness that substitutes a font is the same defect as one that restates a token.  **AMENDED 2026-09-22 — **THREE LOGOS BECAME ONE, AND THE APP ICON IS THE MARK (2026-09-22; owner: "Update design to be less AI looking, update logo to be less AI looking" — a re-ask, because none of rows 496-498 had reached production: the branch has no PR).** **WHAT WAS MEASURED FIRST.** The product carried THREE logos, none of them the header's: the brush-type sticker (with `.ORG`, `LOCAL MUSIC` and the crowd) on all nine PWA icons, all fifteen Android launcher PNGs and the iOS 1024; a serif `iH.` on a DS8-dark tile with two blurred orbs on all eleven Android splashes and the three iOS launch images — the FIRST FRAME of every native launch, still on the ground the product left in August; and a white-`i`-orange-`HYPE` lockup (the mark's colours inverted, in DS8 orange on DS8 navy, beside gradient orbs, a grid overlay and an `H` in a gradient tile) on the nine Satori image routes that stand behind every shared link. No instrument here could see any of it: an icon is a PNG, a Satori card is a literal by necessity, and no audit reads either. **(1) THE MARK IS GEOMETRY NOW.** `IhypeMark` typeset HYPE in `var(--font-display)`, so the mark wore Anton on the street theme and Playfair on classical while its own header said a mark may not change shape on a font swap — true of the `i`, false of the word. `src/lib/brand-mark.ts` holds Bricolage Grotesque's own outlines for H Y P E (wght 800 / opsz 96, the variable font's default instance, taken with fontTools; y flipped so the cap line is 0 and the baseline 660) placed where Chromium laid them out at 1000px with the mark's -0.015em tracking (advance minus 15; the face has no kerning pairs for these letters). The `i` is drawn, not the font's glyph: square dot, 130-unit stem against the H's 121 because a free-standing stem reads lighter. One viewBox, 2545x660, whose HEIGHT is the cap height — so `size` on the component is the cap in px now (12 in the header, 16 on the landing page, 44 on the splash), not a font size. **(2) THE ICON IS THE `i` ALONE, and this reverses a judgement rows 496 and 498 recorded.** Both said the bare `i` on a square was "the weakest thing in the set" and that a square lockup needed a direction. Measured instead: the wordmark rendered into a 48px square reads at 60px and is a smudge at 29px, while the glyph reads at every size — and a home screen prints the app's name under the icon, so the icon does not have to. Two blocks at 56% of a white side is deliberately plain, and plain is the point (**SUPERSEDED 2026-09-23 — THE ICON IS THE WHOLE WORDMARK NOW**, owner, choosing it from six options rendered on the iOS mask at 300/120/60px: "this is the logo i want to use going forward, simple and non ai looking". The red `i` and ink HYPE on white, at 74% of the side, 56% on the Android adaptive foreground; see DESIGN_SYNC row 506. The measurement below is still true — the word is small at Spotlight size — and the owner saw it there and chose it): the generated-icon look the testers named is a gradient blob, a glossy tile, a letter in a rounded square with a glow. `npm run brand:assets` (sharp over the SVG — no font, no browser; Chromium was used ONCE, to measure, and the measurement is baked into the lib) writes 39 files: the PWA set at every size the manifest declares (RGBA, the 32-bit shape Play asks for), the iOS 1024 flattened to RGB because Apple refuses an alpha channel, the legacy Android launchers rounded and circled with transparent corners, the adaptive FOREGROUND transparent with the glyph at 44dp of the 108dp canvas (the launcher shows the centre 72 and guarantees a 66dp circle), eleven Android splashes at their density folders' exact sizes (the window background is STRETCHED, so each orientation keeps its aspect) and three copies of one 2732 iOS square with the wordmark at 34% of the side, because `LaunchScreen.storyboard` fills by aspect and a 19.5:9 phone sees the middle 46% of the width. **The colours are read off `:root` in globals.css and restated nowhere** — the launch colour already lives in five places outside the stylesheet, and this is how the icons stop being a sixth; the first slice of `:root` ended at a `[data-theme` inside a COMMENT and reported `--accent` undeclared, so the block is cut by its own braces. The three sticker files are deleted (`public/brand/` is empty) and `screenshot-mobile.png` — a serif mock of a homepage that never existed, declared to the PWA install prompt as "iHYPE homepage on mobile" — is gone from the manifest. The PWA and the installed web app pick the icon up with the next deploy; the store listings with the next native build. **(3) THE SHARE CARDS.** `src/app/api/og/palette.ts` is the one palette the nine Satori routes paint with, each literal labelled with the token it copies and `brand-assets.test.ts` holding every labelled value to `:root` — the `sw.js` contract, for the same reason. `BrandMarkImage` draws the mark from the same geometry with the colours passed IN (a Satori canvas has no `:root`; the routes are `audit:retro`'s exemptions and the component is not). Every route repainted white/ink/accent, orbs and grid and gradient stripe gone; the milestone card had written `var(--role-venue)` INTO a Satori gradient, which resolves to nothing. **AND FIVE OF THE NINE HAD NEVER RENDERED.** Driving the built worker, `/shows/<slug>/opengraph-image` hung for 90 seconds ("failed to pipe response") on `Expected <div> to have explicit "display: flex" … if it has more than one child node`; bisected in Node, it is the footer — `ihype.org/shows/{slug}` is TWO text children to Satori, and the original routes had the same footer, so the show, artist, venue and fan cards and the poster's span have been failing since they were written. One template literal each. Nobody noticed because nothing links to them: the show page advertises `/api/og?…` as its `og:image`, and `/artists/<slug>/opengraph-image` (with `/venues/`, `/fans/`) sits under a `redirects()` prefix and answers 307. **Recorded, not deleted** — whether the panes should carry their own card is a decision, and today they advertise the generic one. **(4) THE GRADIENTS ROWS 493-494 LEFT.** Eleven decorative gradients still painted on the default theme: the accent-into-walnut band at the top of every artist, venue and advertiser pane (with a radial glare on it), the accent-into-`--role-fan` circle behind every initial in the sheets, the six shelf tiles, the station art, the CTA buttons. Each is a token — `--profile-band`/`-venue`/`-advertiser`/`-glare`, `--tile-tone-0..5`, `--accent-grad`/`-warm` — flat in `:root` and inheriting on dark, the console's own sweeps restated in the four character themes (row 346's rule: set the token, the themes decide). Flattened with them: the player dock art, the charter split bar, the embed widget, the booking and believers avatars, the welcome avatar, the fan page avatar, the payout hero and its four cells, the profile editor's initials, the seed deck's card fallback, the recruiting apply card, three DS8 literal washes in globals.css (a cyan-into-violet playing row, a navy playlist panel, a cyan trailing stop), and the walkthrough deck's own typeset `iHYPE` — 900 weight, +0.08em, uppercase, a four-hue gradient clipped to the letters on the closing slide — which is `IhypeMark` now. **The 404 page still carried `#ff4635,#ff3d87,#7c5cff,#39d8df`**, the exact four-stop rainbow row 496 deleted from the header, twice: a wrong-token fix does not travel unless somebody greps for the value. **Verified on the built worker against a scratch Postgres** at 393 and 1280 (landing, artist, venue, playlists, ME, the public show page, the payout breakdown, `/for-artists`) plus the site and generic cards as bytes; the icons at 48/96/180/512 and both splashes on a contact sheet. Gates: 1853 unit tests, tsc, `typecheck:scripts`, `lint-source`, `audit:css` (0 overrides; the two dead classes are MapLibre's), `audit:retro` 76/76, `audit:shell` (every `var()` resolves), `audit:contrast` (6 themes), `audit:untranslated` 0, `audit:retired-claims` 0, `audit:doc-paths` 0, `audit:mounts` 0, `audit:spacing` 884 and the ratchet lowered 885 -> 884. **DONE 2026-09-22 (DESIGN_SYNC row 502) — this sentence used to open “NOT DONE, stated”**: the full player's transport keys (`.mmm-full-play`, `.mmm-full-step`), both range sliders' rails and knobs and the art bezel were brass, bakelite and lamp RADIAL gradients with SEVEN literal hex stops in `mmm.css` (`#fff6dd`, `#ffb08c`, `#9c3016`, `#6b4526`, `#3a2110`, `#170c04`, `#6d5222`) — console hardware drawn on the white default, and `audit:shell` never saw it because its colour half judges declared colours, not gradient stops inside a `box-shadow` or `background`. Each is a `--transport-*`/`--range-*`/`--art-*` token now: iOS in `:root` (a solid `--ink` disc that turns `--accent` while playing, bare glyphs for the skips, a white knob on a `--ink-1-rgb` rail with the played part filled from the component's own `--fill`, the art under a soft cast at 10px), the console's literals restated verbatim in the four character themes, dark inheriting the flat set. Measured on the built worker at 393: default play disc `rgb(224,38,62)` with white ink while playing, skips transparent with `--ink`, art radius 10px; street still `radial-gradient(… #ffb08c, accent 58%, #9c3016)` with the brass collar shadow and the 2px bezel. **A gradient stop is a colour literal the audit cannot see; grep `mmm.css` for `#` after any theme work.** The eight member-selectable profile skins (`lib/profile-design.ts`, "y2k-sparkle", "neon-script") are a fan's own choice and were left. Imagery remains the largest item and is not code. **AND THE FULL PLAYER'S HYPE WAS UNREACHABLE FOR THE LIFE OF THE MIDDLE ROAD (2026-09-22, DESIGN_SYNC row 503; owner: “I don't see hype button”).** `MmmShell` gated it as `canHype = !currentTrack && Boolean(nowPlaying?.artistProfileId)` — the hype state of the viewer's LAST LISTEN, resolved by the `/app` layout, offered only while NO track was loaded — and the player opens only from the now-playing pill, which exists only WITH a track loaded. A gate and its only entrance in contradiction, so the control never rendered, and no instrument saw it: `mmm-shell.spec.ts` opened the pill on every surface and nothing ever pressed `.mmm-mini-open`. The player's HYPE is keyed on the PLAYING track's artist now: every queue writer carries `artistProfileSlug` and none an id, so `GET /api/hype?targetType=profile&slug=` answers `{ hypeable, profileId, hyped, nextHypeAt }` (`private, no-store`; not found, not discoverable and the viewer's own all read `hypeable: false` so a control is never drawn that is guaranteed to fail) and `POST /api/hype` takes `slug` beside `targetId` — exactly one, checked in the handler because a `.refine` is a ZodEffects and `z.discriminatedUnion` refuses those. The shell fetches the state per track with a cancelled flag and every write is guarded by the profile id it was read for, so a track change mid-request cannot light the wrong artist. **Three rules.** A control's state comes from the thing the control is ABOUT (the playing track), not from a neighbour that is usually the same thing (the last listen). A gate written as “only when nothing is loaded” has to be checked against how the surface is REACHED. And a fixture that leaves a hype behind (`seedPlayableStation`) is one a spec pressing HYPE must undo (`openHypeWindow`) — the row-434 rule, the fixture owning its state. `route.test.ts` beside the route is its first test; the shell spec presses the control on the built worker. **MERGED 2026-09-22 as #1033 (`6b54c038`) — this sentence used to read “NOTHING ABOVE IS LIVE UNTIL THE BRANCH MERGES”, and the branch had carried rows 496-503 with no PR, which is why the owner asked for this twice. Merged is not deployed: the production deploy of it sat in `waiting` on the `Production – ihype` environment's manual approval for two and a half days (DESIGN_SYNC row 504). **LIVE as of 2026-09-23 01:05 UTC — run 1007 (`ca20246b`, #1034) carried it after the owner approved the run.** **That approval rule did NOT hold the next two deploys (measured 2026-09-23 through the Actions API): runs 1008 (`357ccf20`, #1035) and 1009 (`7fd47c34`, #1036) each started the second they were created and finished green in ten minutes with no `waiting` state, while run 1006 had sat 2.5 hours and run 1007 needed the owner's click the same morning.** **The owner confirmed on 2026-09-23 that the environment's required-reviewer rule was REMOVED, deliberately** ("Yes, removal was deliberate"), so a merge to `main` now deploys without a click; run 1010 (`c0c07011`, #1037) started the second it merged. The sentence that outlives the change: a merge is live only once its Deploy to Cloudflare run reads success; read the run, not the PR.** |
| `src/app/mmm.css` (`.mmm-chip` · `.mmm-section-label` · `.mmm-profile-card[data-hero='false']` · `.mmm-empty-state`) + `src/app/mmm-workflows.css` (`.profile-panel-empty` · `.mmm-profiles-eyebrow`) + `:root`'s `--mmm-frame-max`/`--mmm-pane-pad` and `.adaptive-site-header.is-app .adaptive-site-header-inner` in `src/app/globals.css` + `MmmMusic.tsx` (the reason line) + `MmmTickets.tsx` (the claim form's place) | **THE VISUAL PASS AFTER THE DOCK (2026-09-22, DESIGN_SYNC row 501; owner: “Any other visual improvements that would make this app better?” then “do recommendations above”).** Fifteen surfaces captured at 393 and 1280 on the built worker, eight defects ranked, all eight done and each is a rule. **(1) ONE CHIP.** `.mmm-chip` (the filter chips on Charts, Radio and ME) was the console's 11px tracked-mono pill beside the section strip's 15px sentence-case iOS pill, two vocabularies for one control a row apart; it paints from the same `--pill-*` tokens and the body face now, with `text-transform: none` and no tracking. **A filter and a tab are the same control and wear one pill.** **(2) NO HERO, NO BAND.** Every profile without a hero image drew the empty accent-into-walnut band with the avatar pulled 34px up into nothing; `data-hero` on `.mmm-profile-card` hides the band and lets the head sit flush. Measured `display: none` on both panes with no image. **(3) ONE EMPTY STATE.** `.profile-panel-empty` and `.mmm-empty-state` each carried their own dashed serif plate as literals; both read the `--empty-*` tokens `.mmm-empty` already read (iOS filled cell by default, the console's dashed serif on the character themes). **A second empty plate written as literals is the same defect as a second material.** **(4) A REASON IS NOT AN ACTION.** The Recommended tab's “Because you hyped …” line painted `--accent`, so five red sentences competed with the one red key on the screen; it is `--ink-3`. **Accent is the colour of the thing to press.** **(5) THE HEADER ALIGNS TO THE FRAME.** On desktop the header's inner row spanned the viewport while the pane sat in a 712px frame, so the mark was 284px left of the content; `--mmm-frame-max` and `--mmm-pane-pad` moved from `.mmm-frame` to `:root` and the `is-app` header's inner width is `min(100%, frame) − 2·pad`. Measured: header inner 300–980, pane content 300–980, at 1280; 16–377 both, at 393. **A token two elements must agree on lives on `:root`, not on one of them.** **(6) THE WALLET LEADS WITH THE TICKETS.** The transfer-code form sat above the member's own tickets; it renders below the list now (above it only in the demo, where there are no real tickets to lead with). **(7) THE AUTH GROUND WAS NOT GREY.** Measured: `body` and `.authcard-page` are `#ffffff`, `--auth-wash` is `none`; the grey in the capture is `.authcard-box` at 4% ink over white — the card, by design. Recorded so nobody chases it again. **(8) THE LAST CONTENT EYEBROWS.** `Your listening`, `Most played artists`, `About me · visible activity`, `{role} stats`, `Your playlists · n`, `Page toolkit`, `Your pages`, `Have a transfer code?` and the shelf names were 11px tracked-mono uppercase; they wear `.mmm-section-label` (display face, 17px/700, sentence case) — row 496's rule, applied to the surfaces it missed. **`MmmShelf` drew `mmm-eyebrow` AND `mmm-shelf-name` and the eyebrow won the cascade**, so the `content: none` override on the shelf name was fighting a class the element should not have carried; the class is gone rather than overridden again. Two English fallbacks changed case under existing keys and were RENAMED (`pagesHome.yourPagesTitle`, `pageRoleModules.pageToolkitTitle`) with eleven hand-written translations, per row 386. **LEFT ALONE HERE, BY THE RECORDED DECISION IN ROW 338 — AND REVERSED THE NEXT DAY (row 508)**: a Radio station that resolves to no tracks stayed listed, disabled and saying so, because a station that vanishes reads as a fetch that failed; measured against production the tab was eight disabled rows and nothing to play, and the reasoning is about a FAILED read, which a count of zero is not. The empty stations fold under one sentence naming them; a null count keeps its row. | `SitePlayerDock` in `src/components/GlobalMediaPlayer.tsx` + the `.site-dock*` block in `src/app/globals.css` + `src/components/PlayerQueuePanel.tsx` + `RadioTab` in `src/components/mmm/MmmMusic.tsx` | **THE PUBLIC PAGES' PLAYER IS THE SAME PILL AS THE SHELL'S, AND AN EMPTY RADIO STATION IS A SENTENCE, NOT A ROW (2026-09-23, DESIGN_SYNC row 508; owner: "do it").** `SitePlayerDock` renders only while a track is loaded and the session is authenticated: a 56px pill (art · title/artist · previous · play · next, 44×44 keys) that mirrors `.mmm-mini` value for value from the `--dock-*` tokens under its own `.site-dock*` names — mmm.css loads only under `/app`, and the shell's e2e reads `.mmm-mini` as the shell's — and a SHEET above it, opened from the title, carrying everything the 82px walnut bar used to: the keyboard-operable waveform, shuffle, repeat, mute + volume, speed, sleep, share, `PlayerQueuePanel` and `FanPlaylistManager` (mounted by nothing else, per `audit:mounts`; the playlist panel used to open BELOW its launcher off the bottom of the viewport and was unreachable from the dock). **Four rules.** (1) It rides `--consent-inset` like the shell's pill rides `--mmm-dock-lift`; without that term the cookie banner covered the sheet and intercepted the title, measured. (2) `body:has(.site-dock) .site-shell` is the ONLY bottom clearance — an unconditional `.site-shell { padding-bottom: 82px }` had padded every public page for a bar that might not exist. (3) `html.mmm-locked .site-dock { display: none }` in mmm.css is what keeps one pill on screen inside the shell; do not remove it. (4) `.walnut-panel`/`.walnut-lip-top` are deleted (the `ds/` components wear `-frame`/`-plate`); paint the pill from tokens, never a material. **The Radio tab** folds stations with a ZERO count under one sentence naming them and keeps a `null` count (a failed read) as a row — reversing row 338's disabled-row decision, whose reasoning was about a failed read. Measured on the built worker at 393 and 1280, with and without the banner; the probe is scratchpad-only. **Harness trap**: `pkill -f` on a substring the background shell's own command line carries kills the chain before its build runs, and a stale workerd then holds the port — kill by port (`fuser -k 8787/tcp`) and verify the served CSS hash against the bundle before reading a measurement. |
| `src/app/app/loading.tsx` + `experimental.staleTimes` in `next.config.mjs` + `src/lib/session-user-cache.ts` (the memo `src/lib/auth.ts` reads; `forgetSessionUser()` in `src/app/admin/users/actions.ts` and `src/lib/privacy-actions.ts`) + `src/app/app/layout.tsx` (session only) + the payload cache in `src/components/mmm/MmmMusic.tsx` + `includeTickets` on `loadMmmMe` + `[placement]` in `wrangler.toml` + `src/lib/__tests__/shell-navigation-speed.test.ts` | **THE LAG BETWEEN A MENU TAP AND THE SCREEN WAS ROUND-TRIPS, NOT RENDER TIME (2026-09-23, DESIGN_SYNC row 509; owner: "There's a bit of a lag in between selection of new menus and displaying them" then "do all!").** Read row 509 for the numbers; carry these six rules. **(1) A `force-dynamic` route tree needs a `loading.tsx`, or a `<Link>` into it prefetches NOTHING.** Every `/app/*` route is dynamic and until this date nothing under `/app` had a boundary: every RSC prefetch measured 0 bytes and the old screen stayed up until the new payload landed. `src/app/app/loading.tsx` wears the tabs' own `mmm-empty mmm-loading` plate (one shape for "filling", row 338) and renders inside the pane under the header, map layer and strip the layout already holds; `staleTimes.dynamic = 30` makes a hop back instant. Do not delete either on the reasoning that the shell "already renders" — measure a hop first. **(2) `auth()`'s security-version read is memoised per isolate for 30s** (`session-user-cache.ts`): a shell screen calls `auth()` in its layout, its page and every API call after mount, each a cross-region read. The trade is that a `userSecurityVersion` bump can take 30s to land on an isolate that read the row just before it, so BOTH writers call `forgetSessionUser()`; a new writer must too, and `shell-navigation-speed.test.ts` refuses one that does not. Single-device sign-out is the KV tombstone and is untouched. **(3) The `/app` layout awaits the session and NOTHING ELSE.** It ran three dependent reads for a `nowPlaying` no surface could show (the pill draws `currentTrack` only and the full player opens only from the pill — row 503); `MmmShell` no longer takes the prop. A "Resume last listen" needs an endpoint that returns a playable url, not a layout read. **(4) The MUSIC tabs read through a module-level payload cache** (60s stale-while-revalidate, raw payloads keyed by URL, mapped by the caller) — **except the member's OWN library** (`OWN_LIBRARY_READS`: favourites, likes, playlists, listens), which revalidates on every arrival once its reading is two seconds old, because a dozen writers in five components change it and none of them invalidates (row 513). A failed revalidation keeps the rows on screen; a failed first read is still an error, never an empty claim (row 408). **AMENDED 2026-09-24 (DESIGN_SYNC row 512): the sibling warmer this sentence used to describe is DELETED, and each tab's first reads are answered by the SERVER inside the page render** (`src/lib/mmm-music-bootstrap.ts`, next wiring row) — a warmed payload would never have been read, because the server's answer wins in `useJson`. | `src/app/globals.css` (token aliases) | The design system names tokens `--bg-base`/`--bg-surface`/`--ink-1`; this codebase has always called them `--bg`/`--bg-2`/`--ink`. Both names are live — the aliases point at the canonical tokens, so a theme only overrides one set. **Never assign a raw colour to an alias.** The RGB triplets (`--bg-base-rgb`, `--ink-1-rgb`, `--surface-tint-rgb`) are the exception: a custom property can't be decomposed in CSS, so they are literals and **must be re-stated in every theme block**. Missing them in `[data-theme="light"]` is what made every composed-alpha surface (scrim, header blur, hover tint) paint near-black over a light page. (**The plural is back as of 2026-08-24** — the owner asked for selectable themes, so "every theme block" now means `:root` plus the five `[data-theme]` blocks (dark/flowery/street/metal/classical). Each restates the triplets and the whole `--ink-a*` ramp; a triplet that disagrees with the `--ink`/`--bg` it was derived from still paints a composed surface wrong, and `npm run audit:contrast` now walks every block.) `--role-advertiser` is now its own token — do not go back to borrowing `--warn`. **The brand triplets (`--accent-rgb`, `--accent-2-rgb`, `--role-fan-rgb`, `--role-venue-rgb`, `--role-promoter-rgb`) are the opposite case and are deliberately NOT restated per theme** — they are brand fills, identical in both. **`--heat-*-rgb` was in that list and is DELETED (2026-09-15, DESIGN_SYNC row 486)**: all four were defined once and read by nothing, while every other triplet here is read 1–135 times and the non-rgb `--heat-fire` is read 5 — so the list named a dead subset as live. A token nothing reads is not a brand decision, it is weight; measure the `var()` count before adding a name to this sentence. The ink/bg triplets and the whole `--ink-a*` ramp ARE per-theme; adding a rung to one block and not the other is the same bug. **`--ink-on-accent` is ink ON a brand fill; `--accent-text` is the accent used AS copy** — they are inverses and are not interchangeable. **`--ink-on-accent` is `#1c1408`, not white — that changed and this line used to say otherwise.** White was 3.27:1 on the accent and failed AA; the console ink measures **5.58:1** and passes. (This line said `#0b1220` at "6.03:1" until 2026-08-20; the value was DS8's navy, and the ratio was wrong even for that — `#0b1220` on `#ff5029` measures 5.73:1. Both numbers here are computed, not quoted.) It being one token rather than ~20 inline `color: #fff` is what made that a one-line change. Do not "restore" white on the grounds that dark text on orange looks unusual: it is the measured value. (This used to cite "the light-theme pilot further down this file" re-measuring it at 5.0:1 against a darkened accent. There is no light theme any more, and no such pilot — the number that governs is the 5.58:1 above.) **Design System 6 (row 267) added a third rule to the two above: a fill token and a copy token are different tokens even when the hex matches.** `--role-promoter` was `#ffb84a`, the same amber as `--warning` and `--role-advertiser`, and was therefore being used for pending pills, a ⚠ callout and a demand bar — none of which are promoters. Do not reintroduce it; there is no DJ role to keep from repainting. `--warning-text` is the amber-as-copy token (`#ffb84a` is 2.63:1 on cream); `--warning` stays the fill. The `--duration-*`/`--ease-*`/`--sheet-*-dur` set is zeroed by a **token-level** `prefers-reduced-motion` block, so a surface animating through them needs no media query of its own — but that block complements, and does not replace, the keyframe-disabling rules further down the file. |

'')`**, so a request with no `tab` at all matched, captured `tab: ''`, and handed that to path-to-regexp's `compile` — which **throws** on an empty required param rather than emitting an empty one. The throw became the 500. **Next's own router rejects an absent value before it runs the regex**, so `next start` answered 307 the whole time and nothing but curling production could see it. It also **never reached Sentry**: it is thrown inside the routing layer, ahead of the Next handler and our instrumentation. Fix is one character (`.+`). The generalisation is the part to keep: **any `has` regex whose destination re-emits the capture must refuse the empty string**, and the test asserts that for every entry in the list — plus the `/legal` pair's ORDER, since OpenNext takes the first rule whose regex and `has` both match. No `error` event, no `load`, every dependency answering 200 — `check:basemap` was green throughout the outage because the eight things it fetched were all fine. Raster never needed the worker, which is why the map drew before #810 and why row 343's CSP fix looked like the end of it. **The build now copies `maplibre-gl-worker.mjs` and `maplibre-gl-shared.mjs` into `public/vendor/maplibre-gl/` (gitignored; `dev`, `prebuild` and `cf:build` all run the copy, and the unit test refuses a script list that does not) and `MmmMap` calls `maplibre.setWorkerUrl(MAPLIBRE_WORKER_URL)` BEFORE `new maplibre.Map(`** — the constructor spawns the workers, so the order is the fix; `maplibre-worker.test.ts` asserts it by index. Same origin, so `worker-src 'self'` already permits it. **Do not point the URL at a `_next/static` chunk or a CDN**: the first does not exist (webpack never emits the worker) and the second needs a new `worker-src` entry and a version pinned in two places. `check:basemap --base=` fetches the worker from the origin and fails on a non-JavaScript content-type, the one row that would have caught this. **The 15s deadline is a diagnosis**: `describeBasemapStall()` reads `isStyleLoaded()`, `areTilesLoaded()`, frames drawn, context loss, error count and `document.visibilityState` and names the stage on screen — this failure reads "style loaded, tiles never parsed — the map worker may be blocked"; **a hidden tab is never judged**, because `load` fires from a render frame and background tabs stop framing, and the deadline re-arms on `visibilitychange`. **This sandbox cannot render the real basemap** (Chromium's connection to cartocdn is reset by the proxy; Node's is not), so the local probe proves the worker URL, its liveness and its MIME and then reaches the error-event branch. Do not conclude the map works in production from a green `check:basemap` alone; open it. |
| `src/lib/csp-routes.ts` (`MAP_TILE_HOSTS`) + the basemap URLs in `src/components/mmm/MmmMap.tsx` | **A CSP wildcard does not match the bare domain, and that blanked the map in production for about nine hours (2026-09-03).** The basemap moved raster → vector the same day. Raster fetched only from `a.`…`d.basemaps.cartocdn.com`; the vector **style document is on the apex** `basemaps.cartocdn.com`, and `*.basemaps.cartocdn.com` stands in for one or more labels, so it never matched. Our own `connect-src` refused the style, MapLibre got none, `load` never fired, and `/app/map` drew as **bare parchment** — the ground and the ruled grid are painted by `mmm.css` OVER the canvas, so a completely dead basemap is pixel-for-pixel a quiet region with no pins. Reported by the owner ("Nothing showing on map now"), not by anything here. **Both entries are load-bearing**: the apex for the style, the wildcard for the tiles, glyphs and sprites the style then reaches for, all of which really are on `tiles.`. CSP has no single source that means "this domain and its subdomains". **The test that was here asserted the list CONTAINED the wildcard string** — true throughout the outage, and the reason nothing caught it; `csp-routes.test.ts` now resolves every cartocdn URL built in `MmmMap.tsx` against the list under real host-source semantics, with the matcher itself verified in both directions. Diagnosed by measurement rather than by reading the spec: Chromium, the exact `connect-src` production was serving, and `securitypolicyviolation` — which needs no network, because CSP refuses **before** any connection, so it works from a sandbox that cannot reach CARTO at all. **The map also said nothing while showing nothing**, which is why only a human could find it: `failed` meant "the maplibre bundle would not load" and nothing else. It now also trips on a `BASEMAP_LOAD_DEADLINE_MS` (15s) that `load` clears, so a slow connection retracts the line instead of being called broken — "not yet", never a verdict. Note this is the SECOND time this file's list went stale behind a move (the first named `/listen` after the landing surface became `/app/map`); both times the policy was wrong for a route that had changed under it, and both times it presented as a layout bug. |
| `src/lib/rate-limit.ts` + `worker.js` (`RateLimiterDO`) | **Rewired after a real, 8-day-old production degradation (2026-07-28).** Sentry showed `/api/analytics/track` on ihype.org repeatedly falling back to the non-atomic KV counter **at half the configured limit**, with DO calls timing out at 1500ms. Three causes, all fixed: the DO wrote `storage.setAlarm()` on *every* `consume()` (a third storage write per request that always stored the value already there — every request for one key serializes through one DO instance, so it compounded under a burst); the DO call shared KV's 1500ms deadline (split into two deadlines, `DEFAULT_DO_TIMEOUT_MS` with a `RATE_LIMIT_DO_TIMEOUT_MS` override — **1800ms as of 2026-08-22, not the 750ms this line used to name**; see the `timeoutMs` row below); and `consumeDurableObject` returned `null` for both "binding absent" and "call failed", so every failure logged twice — the real error *plus* a misleading "RATE_LIMITER_DO unavailable" — which is why one fault showed up in Sentry as two issues with different counts, the louder naming the wrong cause. Now a 3-way `AtomicOutcome`; only a genuinely missing binding logs the fallback. **New `atomic: false` option** routes a bucket to KV on purpose (used by `/api/analytics/track`: highest-volume bucket on the site, and a refusal there only drops an analytics event) — an opted-out bucket keeps its **full** configured limit, since it isn't degraded. |
| `worker.js` (Sentry `beforeSend`) | Drops events whose request host is localhost/127.0.0.1. A local `wrangler dev` run loads the same `wrangler.toml`, so it picks up the real `SENTRY_DSN` and reports itself as `environment: production` — two "production" issues (110 events) turned out to be one developer's miniflare session, not the live site. Check the request URL before believing an `environment: production` tag. **AND THE BROWSER HALF NEVER RAN UNTIL 2026-09-14 (DESIGN_SYNC row 443)**: `sentry.client.config.ts` is bundled only by `withSentryConfig`, which this build deliberately does not use, so the client init was in no chunk and the deploy inlined no `NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN` — every event ever recorded here is server-side. The browser init is `src/instrumentation-client.ts` (the entry point Next loads natively), the DSN is inlined at BUILD by `deploy-production.yml`, and `sentry-client-wiring.test.ts` pins both plus the `connect-src` read in `middleware.ts`. A config file is wiring only if something loads it — check the built output, not the filename. |
| `src/app/api/shows/[showId]/cancel/route.ts` (organizer message) | **DESIGN_SYNC row 246, built 2026-07-28.** Optional free-text note to ticket holders on cancellation. Stored in its own nullable `Show.cancellationMessage` — deliberately **not** folded into `cancellationReason`, which only ever holds one of four fixed labels, so an organizer's words stay distinguishable from the platform's. Capped at 400 chars, control chars and whitespace runs stripped server-side, always rendered as plain text and never auto-linkified (a cancellation notice is about money; it must not be able to carry a "claim your refund here" link). The platform's refund sentence stays first and unconditional in the notification; the organizer's text is attributed after it. Both fields now render on `/shows/[slug]` for a cancelled show — before this, `cancellationReason` had been stored since row 227 and shown to nobody. |
| `src/app/api/ads/impression/route.ts` + `src/lib/ad-play-token.ts` | The budget guard now lives in the `updateMany` `WHERE`, not in a JS `if` between a read and a write — concurrent impressions could previously all pass the check before any increment landed. The impression row is only created when the conditional spend actually matched, so `spentCents` and the impression count can no longer disagree. The pre-read stays: it's what tells the caller *why* an impression was skipped. **THE CAMPAIGN NOW COMES OUT OF A SIGNED PLAY TOKEN, NEVER OFF THE REQUEST BODY (2026-09-09, DESIGN_SYNC row 372).** `adId` is public in every station payload and every show production plan, and until this date naming one was enough to spend a real advertiser's authorized budget — the rate limit and the once-per-listener-per-day dedup bounded how MUCH any caller could cost a campaign and never asked whether the ad had been served to them at all, so the cheapest attack was not a loop from one address but a spread. `createAdPlayToken()` is HMAC-SHA256 over `{adId, subject, exp}` with `AUTH_SECRET`, domain-separated by a message prefix so it can never be replayed as a session or an unsubscribe link, and it is minted wherever a `mkt_` clip is actually chosen: `/api/stations/[slug]/tracks` **bound to the listener** (that response is `private, no-store`), `/api/radio/station` as a **bearer** token with no subject (it answers `public, s-maxage=5`, so a member-bound token there would be handed to whoever fetched it next and refused for all of them), and `src/app/shows/[slug]/page.tsx` for the clips in the production plan — because `buildResolvedSequence` runs in the BROWSER off a plan the client already holds, so the clip ids in it prove nothing. **It fails towards NOT CHARGING, which is the opposite of `passkey-challenge.ts` and `session-revocation.ts` and is deliberate**: an unreadable secret or a mangled token costs an advertiser some delivery, which is recoverable; the other direction bills somebody for airtime nobody heard. **It is deliberately not single-use** — a KV spend-marker would put a write on the serving path to buy what the daily dedup already buys, since replaying a token you legitimately hold is free either way. What it does NOT prove is that the audio reached a speaker; that needs a signed progress report and is recorded as the remaining limit in `docs/security-sweep-2026-09-02.md`. TTL is 12 hours because a station queue is served once and its last break can play hours later. `wiring-guards.test.ts` asserts on the CALLERS, not the route: the failure to fear is an honest future caller written from the old shape (`{ adId: clipId.slice(4) }`), which the route refuses forever while nothing errors and the other surfaces keep billing. Walk item 20c asserts the bare-`adId` refusal FIRST and then bills with a token the product actually served it; item 33 asserts every break carries one. |
| `docs/security-sweep-2026-09-02.md` + `src/lib/stripe-webhook-guards.ts` + `src/lib/html-escape.ts` + `src/app/api/dmca/route.ts` | **The full security sweep (2026-09-02, owner: "Do full security sweep") — read the document before touching anything it names.** Six review passes, every finding verified in code; 27 fixed in one change, the rest recorded as operator items or accepted with reasons. The five that were HIGH and are worth carrying in memory: **(1) a Stripe signature is not proof the event is about YOUR object** — connected accounts hold their own keys, so a venue could pay $0.50 on its own account with another order's `confirmationCode` in metadata and have the webhook finalize that order; every ticket branch now checks `event.account` against the order's settlement account and the amount against `totalChargeCents`, and ad events must be platform-account and cover the budget (`stripe-webhook-guards.ts`, pure and tested — keep any new branch behind it). **The LIVE dashboard endpoint (`we_1Tv6Ev…`, `https://ihype.org/api/stripe/webhook`) was read through the Stripe connector on 2026-09-02 and subscribes to `payment_intent.succeeded/canceled/amount_capturable_updated/payment_failed` and `account.updated` ONLY — not `checkout.session.completed`, which is the one event that wrote a hosted checkout's PaymentIntent id onto its order.** So a live ticket sale would have been paid on Stripe and finalised by nothing: `payment_intent.succeeded` looked the order up by an id no event had stored. The route now also resolves the order from the intent's own `metadata.confirmationCode` (set at session creation through `payment_intent_data`) and stores the id, under the same account and amount guards, so finalisation depends on one subscription rather than two; an order already bound to a different intent is never re-bound. **The six missing events were added to that endpoint through the Stripe connector at 22:29 UTC the same day (owner: "Do what you recommend")** — `checkout.session.completed`, `checkout.session.async_payment_succeeded/failed`, `checkout.session.expired`, `charge.dispute.created`, `charge.updated` — so it now carries all eleven the route handles; the signing secret did not rotate (updating `enabled_events` never does). The code fallback stays, because it is the defence against the next time a dashboard setting and the code disagree. **The Connect endpoint ("listen to events on connected accounts") exists as of 23:00 UTC the same day** — `we_1UBN94…`, the same `https://ihype.org/api/stripe/webhook` URL, the same eleven events, `application` set (which is what marks it Connect-scoped in the API) — and the owner set its signing secret on the Worker as `STRIPE_CONNECT_WEBHOOK_SECRET` from the Cloudflare dashboard (Workers & Pages → ihype → Settings → Variables and Secrets; a dashboard secret is identical to `wrangler secret put` and survives deploys). **It matters for more than venue-direct sales**: a connected account's `account.updated` only ever arrives on a Connect endpoint, and that event is what flips `Profile.stripeConnectOnboarded`, so without it no venue or artist could ever finish payout setup and every sale would fall back to `PLATFORM`. Three endpoints now exist in live mode and only the two `ihype.org` ones are iHYPE's; the third (`…supabase.co/functions/v1/stripe-webhook`, ~95 events, `managed_by: stripe-sync`) belongs to the Stripe Sync Engine named in the infrastructure list and must be left alone. No venue has onboarded yet (`/v1/accounts` is empty). Also `STRIPE_CONNECT_WEBHOOK_SECRET` for the second dashboard endpoint, and a live/test mode check. **(2) `POST /api/dmca` was an unauthenticated auto-cancel**: it armed a 10-day `dmcaStatus: 'PENDING'` on any show and the cron cancelled it without refunds; nothing else ever wrote that column. It now files a `ContentReport` (`dmca_notice`) for the moderation queue and emails the admin; the cron acts only on `CONFIRMED`, which nothing sets automatically. **Do not restore the arm.** **(3) `isTrustedStorageUrl()` is for CLIENT-submitted URLs and trusts only our own `/cdn/` path** — it used to trust any `*.r2.dev` host (anyone has one) and `data:`, so an ad spot could skip the upload route and be swapped after vetting; `isStoredMediaUrl()` keeps the legacy arms for DB-sourced delete paths only. **(4) `/api/newsletter/confirm` wrote `Profile.name` unescaped into HTML outside the CSP matcher** — `escapeHtml()` from `html-escape.ts` now wraps every member-derived value in every hand-built HTML response and email; use it on any new one. **(5) `/api/ads/impression` took anonymous hits** at 100/hour/IP with no dedup and settlement captured the spend; sign-in is required and a member is charged once per ad per day. **THE SENTRY WEBHOOK FAILURES ARE UNATTRIBUTED, AND THIS LINE USED TO NAME A CAUSE IT COULD NOT SEE (corrected 2026-09-10).** It read: "(109 + 10, 2026-08-26 to 08-30) were NOT an attack or a dashboard misconfiguration — every one came from one Cloudflare WARP egress address, the same address that browsed `/welcome`, and not from Stripe's published webhook IPs — a `stripe listen --forward-to` session on the owner's machine, which signs with the CLI's own secret." **The address proves nothing.** Measured through the Sentry connector: all **173** events in `JAVASCRIPT-NEXTJS-D` carry `user.ip` `2a06:98c0:3600::103`, and so do **all 71 errors in the project that have nothing to do with Stripe**, over the same 30 days — one address for every event iHYPE has ever recorded. So "the same address that browsed `/welcome`" is true of every error in the product, including ones no laptop produced; the field is the Worker's own view behind Cloudflare, not a client address, and it cannot distinguish a CLI session from Stripe's own delivery. The conclusion is not reversed, it is **withdrawn** — a `stripe listen` session is still the likeliest explanation, and nothing here has ever tested it. **Never attribute a caller from `user.ip` on this project.** What IS measured: the failures **recurred**, 64 more events 2026-09-06 to 09-09, last at **02:57 UTC on 09-09** and none in the 35 h since — so whatever the source, it is quiet now, and neither its identity nor its stopping is explained from here. The question is answered in **Stripe Dashboard → Developers → Webhooks with the TEST-mode toggle on**: a test-mode endpoint pointing at the live `https://ihype.org/api/stripe/webhook` signs with the test secret and can never verify, which would look exactly like this. Read it there before writing a cause into this file again. **MEASURED 2026-09-23 THROUGH THE RECONNECTED CONNECTORS (DESIGN_SYNC row 504): THE DELIVERIES ARE THE SANDBOX'S.** 341 events in three batches, and each batch opens within seconds of a SANDBOX PaymentIntent being created here — the 09-06 `stripe:rehearsal`, then the first Stripe-keyed nightly (run 34914298500 started 00:42:40 on 09-15; first failure 00:45:10, inside its walk step) and every nightly after it (09-19: walk step 07:20:00, first failure 07:20:11, then Stripe's retries at 07:23 · 07:50 · 08:20 · 08:50 · 10:20 · 10:50) — while the LIVE account holds zero PaymentIntents, so no live event could have produced them. A webhook endpoint in the Stripe sandbox points at `https://ihype.org/api/stripe/webhook` and Stripe signs its deliveries with that endpoint's secret, which production does not hold. The Stripe connector reads the live account only, so the endpoint itself is read and deleted in the Dashboard with the sandbox selected; the failures stopped on 09-19 while the nightly kept creating sandbox objects on 09-20, 21 and 22, which is what Stripe disabling a persistently failing endpoint looks like. **The owner deleted that endpoint on 2026-09-23**, so the source is gone; the route now reads the body's own `livemode` BEFORE the signature check and refuses a delivery declaring the other mode at `warn`, naming this cause (`declaredLivemode()` in `stripe-webhook-guards.ts`): unverified by construction and used only to refuse, 400 kept so the sandbox dashboard shows the failure, the verified-event check kept for a body that lies. **A `stripe listen` session was the likeliest story for two weeks and was wrong; the measurement that settled it was the TIMESTAMP of the first failure against the run log of the harness that creates test objects.** **Three of those follow-ups are CLOSED as of 2026-09-06 (row 355, next row down) and this line used to list all of them as open**: sign-out now revokes the token's own `jti`, passkey challenges are marked spent in KV, and the magic link is no longer consumed on GET. **Still open**: impressions are self-reported (a signed play token would make them provable, and the always-on station reports none at all, so station ads are unbilled); ~~a member can park public audio under `ads/audio/` with no campaign row~~ (CLOSED 2026-09-14 by the `ad-audio-sweep` cron job, DESIGN_SYNC row 420); and whether `/believers` and `/epk` should render for any member is a question about intent rather than a defect. **Operator**: ephemeral CI runner, branch protection requiring review, required reviewers on the `Production – ihype` environment. **A second scan the same day (after #797 merged) found and fixed another 23, three of them HIGH and all in business logic every individual request authorised correctly**: `delete-account` hard-deleted the User and CASCADED every buyer's ticket and every payable on the organiser's shows (now `executeAccountErasure`, the path privacy-actions.ts always said to use); a buyer could name their OWN profile as the affiliate promoter and take 10% of their ticket back from the acts; and an email transfer rotated the ticket ids but left `buyerUserId` on the sender, who kept the new QR. Also: the reservation-expiry cron voided orders at 15 minutes under a 30-minute Checkout session (paid, then VOID — now 35); `GET /api/shows` shipped whole rows including organiser notes under a public cache; `GET /api/profile/[slug]` was a five-minute public cache of a payload carrying fans' `contactInfo`; fan cities were stored at signup and painted into an unauthenticated OG image; profile image URLs accepted any host (a viewer-IP beacon — new image URLs must now be ones iHYPE stored). The one thing left undecided is that **ad-campaign card holds expire in ~7 days while runs go to 90 and pauses are unbounded, so long or paused campaigns are unbilled** — a product change (charge up front, refund the remainder), recorded in the document. |
| `src/lib/passkey-challenge.ts` + `src/lib/session-revocation.ts` + `src/app/auth/confirm/page.tsx` + `MagicLinkConfirm.tsx` + the split `GET`/`POST` in `src/app/api/auth/magic/route.ts` | **The three sweep follow-ups on the way IN and the way OUT (2026-09-06, DESIGN_SYNC row 355; owner: "do sweep").** **(1) A passkey challenge is spent once.** It lived only in an httpOnly cookie, so a captured request — assertion body plus its `Cookie` header — could be replayed for five minutes and handed a session; `claimPasskeyChallenge()` marks it used in KV on all four ceremonies (sign-in, admin step-up, admin device binding, adding a passkey). **(2) Signing out ends the session on the SERVER.** The token is a self-contained twelve-hour JWT and sign-out only expired the cookie, so a copy taken beforehand kept working — which matters most in exactly the situation that makes someone press sign out. Every token already carries a `jti`, so a KV tombstone under it revokes ONE device; bumping `userSecurityVersion` was the one-line version and would have closed the phone's session too, which is a different button. **(3) `GET /api/auth/magic` no longer signs anyone in.** Corporate mail security fetches every link in a message, so a scanner burned the token before the member read it, and asking for another produced another one it burned — with no password anywhere in this product, that locks a whole mail domain out permanently. GET reads and writes nothing and forwards to `/auth/confirm`, whose form POSTs the token back; the POST refuses a `Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site` request (login CSRF — judged on the header rather than by comparing `Origin` to `request.nextUrl.origin`, because behind Cloudflare with a scheme- and host-rewriting middleware the origin Next derives need not match the one the browser stamped, and that comparison would refuse every real sign-in while reviewing as correct) and answers 303 so the browser GETs the destination. **THE RULE UNDER ALL THREE, and the one most easily reversed by a later edit that only reads the code: a control on the ONLY way into an account must fail towards letting the member in.** So the passkey marker is written on USE, not at issue — KV lag can then only weaken the barrier, never refuse a member's first attempt — and an unreadable KV means "not revoked". `auth.ts`'s security-version check deliberately fails the other way, because a database outage means the primary control cannot be evaluated at all; both files say so. `en.json` holds 557 keys and English comes from the source fallbacks, so only the eleven other dictionaries needed the five `auth.confirm.*` strings. **AMENDED 2026-09-25 (DESIGN_SYNC row 516) — (2) revoked almost nothing, because `@auth/core`'s `encode()` replaces `jti` on EVERY rewrite of the cookie; revocation keys on a stable `sid` claim now (`sessionRevocationId()`, read by both sides), impersonated sessions cannot add or remove a passkey or attach a recovery email (`impersonation-guard.ts`), and `/auth/confirm` auto-submits only in the browser that asked for the link (`magic-link-pending.ts`) — an unconditional submit signed a victim in to whoever's inbox the token came from. Never tombstone a `jti` again.** **VERIFIED on the real path the same day (row 518)**: `session-revocation-reencode.test.ts` drives `@auth/core`'s `Auth()` session action with the middleware's `authConfig` and shows the `jti` changing on every pass while `sid` survives; `authCallbacks` is exported from `auth.ts` so the refusal is tested through the jwt callback itself. A pre-`sid` token is revoked by its `jti` (only the cookie presented at sign-out) and has that `jti` pinned as its `sid` on its next refresh; all such tokens are gone within twelve hours. **AND BOTH OTHER HALVES WERE FINISHED THE SAME DAY (row 519)**: an impersonated credential write is refused AND audited under the operator's id, registration and deletion of a passkey each write an audit row, and route tests call the handlers; the confirm page's marker is now a list of digests of the tokens THIS browser was sent, so a member's own pending link no longer makes an attacker's auto-submit. Do not replace it with a `document.referrer` or `Sec-Fetch-Site` rule: webmail taps look cross-site like an attack, and an attacker's EMAILED link has no referrer like the member's own. **Walk item 39 is the durable proof and it changed shape**: it used to assert that GETting the link set a session cookie, which is now precisely the bug — it asserts two GETs spend nothing, then that the posted token works once and is refused on replay. |
| `src/lib/signing-secrets.ts` + every signer (`auth-session.ts`, the sign-out decoder, `ad-play-token.ts`, `unsubscribe.ts`) + `docs/runbooks/secret-rotation.md` | **THE SIGNING SECRET ROTATES ON AUTH.JS'S CONVENTION, AND ONLY ONE MODULE READS IT (2026-09-10, DESIGN_SYNC row 379; owner: "Do what you recommend").** `AUTH_SECRET` signs the session, the ad play token and the unsubscribe token; rotating it used to end every session, refuse every receipt in flight and kill every unsubscribe link ever sent, so it was never rotated. **Do not invent an `AUTH_SECRET_PREVIOUS`** — `@auth/core` already reads `AUTH_SECRET_1..3`, and its order is the OPPOSITE of what that name suggests: the numbered key is the NEWEST, it signs, and `AUTH_SECRET` is the old one that only verifies (`config.secret.unshift(...)` in its `setEnvDefaults`; a test reads those lines). `signing-secrets.ts` mirrors that exactly so the session layer, which Auth.js signs, and the tokens this repository signs agree about which key is current — two conventions would present as members logged out while receipts verify, or the reverse. Every signer reads `currentSigningSecret()` to sign and `readSigningSecrets()` to verify; `signing-secrets.test.ts` walks `src/` and fails on any other direct read of the variable, because one signer reading it by hand is one signer that fails to rotate. Runbook: put `AUTH_SECRET_1`, wait 24 h (the 12 h session and the 12 h play token have expired), put the same value under `AUTH_SECRET`, delete `_1` — put before delete, or there is a moment with no key and every request signs out. Unsubscribe tokens never expire by design, so links in emails sent before a rotation are its one real cost: keep the retired key in `AUTH_SECRET_2` for a routine rotation, drop it for a suspected exposure. |
| `docs/runbooks/money-path-rehearsal.md` + `src/lib/__tests__/ticket-order-money-path.test.ts` | **The money path, and what is still unproven (2026-08-22).** The refund and capture state transitions are now covered against a fake transaction client — before this they existed in the suite only as `vi.fn()` stubs at the route level, so every refund test asserted the function was CALLED and nothing asserted what it does. Two assertions are load-bearing: the payable filter is **PENDING-only**, so a refund cannot claw back a transfer that has already left the platform balance (Stripe will not undo one), and a lost race stops before voiding a ticket rather than decrementing capacity twice. **What no test can prove is that any of it works against Stripe** — live mode still holds zero PaymentIntents, and a test-mode account now exists, so the runbook is the sequence to walk before the first sale: the Stripe-side rehearsal, then the app's own path against a scratch database, ending with **running the payout cron twice and seeing `released: 0`**. A cron that pays twice is invisible until it happens. Do not enable `FEATURE_ENABLE_TICKET_PAYMENTS` in production before that runbook has been walked. **Half of it is walked as of 2026-09-06**: `npm run stripe:rehearsal` completed 25/25 against a real test key on the operator's machine, all three settlement modes, so the STRIPE-side semantics this module is written against are now measured rather than assumed (see that row for the ids). **CORRECTED 2026-09-09 — this line used to say "the half that is still unproven is this repository's own — every state transition above has only ever run against a fake transaction client, never against a database holding a real PaymentIntent", and that was two claims, both false.** Step 2 of that runbook was walked on **2026-08-30**, against a scratch Postgres and a real worker build, in all three settlement modes — and it is exactly the kind of walk that earns its cost, because it **found a real defect no unit test could**: a cancelled show could not refund any `VENUE_DIRECT` order, twice over (the PaymentIntent lives on the venue's account, so the platform-scoped refund answered "No such payment_intent"; and `reverse_transfer` is rejected on a direct charge). Fixed and re-run the same day. **The payout cron's double-run `released: 0` was proven there too**, against real Stripe and a real database, so this line's closing instruction was asking for something already done. **Step 3 is also done**: `FEATURE_ENABLE_TICKET_PAYMENTS = "true"` has been in `wrangler.toml` since 2026-08-31, which makes the old "do not enable it before the runbook has been walked" sentence above HISTORY rather than a live gate — read it for its reasoning, not as an instruction. **The one leg genuinely unexercised** is Stripe's own webhook delivery: the walk runs `REHEARSAL_PAY_MODE=api`, which makes a genuine PaymentIntent but SYNTHESIZES the `checkout.session.completed` envelope (correctly signed), so what is unproven is Stripe's delivery and retry against the live endpoint, not the route's handling. Re-running the walk in its default mode from a machine whose browser can reach `checkout.stripe.com` closes it. **Read this row as the warning it now is**: the runbook contradicted itself for ten days — its own summary said "the remaining steps still need a human" while a section above it recorded the walk, with ids — and this file inherited the stale half. Verify against the artefacts (the fix is quoted in `refundTicketPaymentIntent`'s comment; the flag is one grep) before repeating any "still unproven" claim here. |

The passkey path is therefore a WebAuthn **conditional ceremony** — `autocomplete="username webauthn"` on the email field, armed on mount, cancelled via `WebAuthnAbortService` when the user submits email instead. The explicit "Sign in with passkey" button is kept **on purpose** against the .dc.html, which shows one field and one button: conditional mediation isn't universal, the autofill dropdown is undiscoverable for many users, and it is browser chrome rather than DOM so **Playwright cannot click it** — without the button the mandatory `e2e/passkey.spec.ts` sign-in coverage disappears. **(2) There is exactly one `.authcard-*` definition and it is the block at the top.** A later "Scene-signal auth journey" layer used to re-declare `.authcard-page`/`-wrap`/`-box`/`-btn-*`/`-field` as a light-cream two-column editorial layout on hardcoded `#ff4d2e`/`#18ded1`/`#17130f`; it won on source order alone, so `/login` and `/register` rendered *that* for months while the canonical single-card block sat dead directly above it. That is why the sign-in screens looked un-synced no matter what the design said. Do not add a second definition — change the first one. |
| `src/components/AuthRegister.tsx` (what signup collects) | **A fan account asks for nothing (2026-08-05, explicit product decision).** Signup used to require name, email and phone from everyone. `User.phone` was **read by nothing anywhere in the app** — pure dead PII collection — and is gone. `name` is asked only when it is a public profile identity (ARTIST/DJ/VENUE); a fan sets a display name later in Settings if they want one. **Email now follows the method rather than being unconditional:** the passkey path asks for nothing at all (`/api/register`'s `passkeyFlow` has always accepted a signup with neither email nor phone, and `User.email` is nullable), while the magic-link path still needs it because the address *is* the delivery target. The old label said email was "required so passkey setup can fall back safely" — that fallback is now collected **at the moment the passkey ceremony actually fails**, inside the passkey step, which also re-renders Turnstile because its token is single-use and already spent. That fallback **re-registers rather than reusing the account**: `/api/auth/magic-link` answers `ok:true` for an address it cannot find (correct — it must not reveal whether an account exists) and sends nothing, so pointing it at the address-less account would park the user on "check your inbox" forever. The abandoned row has no address, name or credential and nobody can sign into it. The recovery gap this opened is now closed by `POST /api/me/email` (see the row below). **Terms acceptance is recorded** as of 2026-08-05: `User.tosAcceptedAt` had existed since migration `20260602000000` and was written by *nothing*, while signup showed Terms/Privacy/Community-rules as three bare links with no statement of agreement anywhere — the terms were displayed but never accepted. The 13+/18+ attestations stay; those are legal, not preference. **AND THERE IS NO PASSWORD COLUMN EITHER (2026-09-14, DESIGN_SYNC row 440).** `User.passwordHash`, `mfaSecret`, `mfaEnabledAt` and `mfaBackupCodes` were written by register (an optional `password` body field no client sent), both seeds and erasure, and read by nothing; they are out of the schema and the drop is parked in `prisma/migrations-pending/` with the counts to run. Deleted; `no-passwords.test.ts` refuses a password-shaped column, a `password` on register, `bcryptjs` anywhere but the email-code hasher, and an unconditional DELETE from a credential table in any workflow or script. `PasswordResetCode` is the misnamed email-verification store and stays.
