| `src/app/api/me/email/route.ts` + `AddRecoveryEmail` in `src/components/mmm/MmmSettings.tsx` (it moved with the shell; `src/app/settings/` is gone and `/settings` redirects to `/app/me/settings`) | **Attaches a recovery email to an account that has none (2026-08-05).** Signup stopped asking fans for an address and the passkey path asks for nothing, so an account can exist whose only credential is one device — and Settings could *display* an address with no endpoint anywhere to set one. Losing that passkey meant losing the account permanently. **It only ADDS, never CHANGES**: a session that already has an address gets a 409. Change-email is a materially more dangerous feature — whoever holds a session could repoint recovery at their own inbox and take the account — and doing it safely needs re-authentication plus a notice to the displaced address, neither of which exists. Returning 409 is deliberate, not an oversight; the gap being closed is "no recovery path at all". Reuses the existing 6-digit machinery (`createEmailVerificationCode`/`verifyEmailCode`, which are already keyed on `(userId, email)`), and the address is written **only after a code sent to it comes back**, so a typo cannot strand recovery on an inbox the user does not own. `sendVerificationEmail` never reports whether an address is already on another account — a signed-in caller could otherwise walk a list and learn which addresses have accounts; the collision surfaces on confirm as a generic 409. |
| `scripts/audit-css.mjs` + `npm run audit:css` | **The check `audit:shell` structurally cannot do (2026-08-05).** It asks whether a class is *named* right and paints from tokens; it cannot see a selector **defined twice where a later block silently wins**. That is what made `/login` and `/register` render a retired two-column design for months while the correct block sat dead 300 lines above — valid CSS, no error, every other check green. `.nav` was found stacked **four** deep the same way (only harmless because `.adaptive-site-header` happens to come last — verified in-browser, not assumed). Reports three things: **overrides** (same selector, same cascade context, later rule re-declares properties the earlier one set — disjoint properties are normal layering and are not flagged), **a comment inside a selector**, and **dead classes** (advisory; a class can be added by markup this scan cannot see). The middle one is new (2026-08-22) and is not part of the `--max` ratchet — it fails unconditionally, because it is never pre-existing debt to pay down, it is a rule that does not do what it says. A comment is whitespace to a CSS parser, so `.a /* why */ .a > .b { … }` is `.a .a > .b`, a descendant combinator matching nothing. That shipped: `.mmm-music-controls > .mmm-search-wrap { flex: 0 0 auto }` had its explanation written between the two halves of its own selector, so the rule stopping a 300px flex-basis becoming 300px of HEIGHT in a column had **never once applied** — measured, a 41px field inside a 300px box, 273px of empty cream between MUSIC's search bar and the first card, and the deck pushed below the fold. Valid CSS, no error, no override to report, all class names real. The comment explaining the fix was the reason the fix was absent. CI passes **`--max=0` as of 2026-08-31 — the ratchet reached zero and is now a gate.** **And it refuses zero stylesheets with exit 2 as of 2026-09-14 (row 433)** — it had passed over an empty `src/`, and a gate that cannot name its denominator is not a gate. It started at 145 (the debt that already existed; fixing it all at once was a large refactor with real regression risk, and a gate that blocks every merge gets disabled), was lowered as pairs were fixed, and the baseline is fully paid down. Any new duplicate selector fails the build. **Never raise it.** `shell-surfaces.css` is exempt from the override check by design — aliasing many names onto one primitive is its whole purpose. |
| `src/lib/analytics-engine.ts` + `analytics-metrics.ts` + `/admin/analytics` + ~~`GET /api/analytics/summary`~~ (**the route was called by nothing and is deleted 2026-09-06, row 353; the two libs stay and `/admin/analytics` reads them directly** — the scope rules below still govern any future caller) | **The analytics engine (2026-08-06).** Before this, five surfaces — fan, artist, venue, promoter and `/admin/growth` — each opened their own Prisma queries, defined their own "last 30 days", and named the same quantity differently. Five implementations of one question is five chances for two screens to disagree with no way to tell which is wrong. A metric is now declared **once** in `METRIC_CATALOGUE`, and a surface renders metrics rather than re-deriving them. **Two rules shape the types and both are load-bearing. (1) `null` is not zero** — every resolver is independently `.catch()`'d and returns `null` on failure, which renders as an em dash; a dashboard showing `0` for "could not read" is worse than one showing nothing, because `0` is a claim. Same rule `admin-workbench.ts` already follows. **(2) A metric knows its own audience** — `audiences` on each definition is what "tied to user types" means; `metricsForAudience()` filters one catalogue instead of four hand-maintained lists that drift. The engine module imports **no `@/lib/db`** so it stays testable and client-importable (same split as `profile-stats-catalog.ts`); the DB half lives in `analytics-metrics.ts`. **`GET /api/analytics/summary` derives scope from the SESSION, never from the request** — a client-supplied scope would be a plain IDOR (pass another userId, read their listening history). `profileId` is the one identifier a caller may pass and it is ownership-checked against `ownerId` before use; failures are 403, not an empty result, because empty reads as "you have no data". Platform scope is admin-only and the response is `private, no-store` — a shared cache in front of per-account figures is a cross-account leak. Two deliberate definitions: **`shows` counts `startsAt`, not `createdAt`** (a "show scheduled" is about when it happens), and **`tickets_sold` counts only `CAPTURED` orders** (a `RESERVED` order is a held seat that may never be paid for). **Known limit: `ad_spend` is derived, not stored.** `Ad.spentCents` is a running total with no history so it cannot be windowed; spend is inferred from impressions in the window priced at each campaign's own delivered rate. That is an approximation and is commented as one — a real per-period figure needs a spend-ledger row per impression. |

A web-only merge rebuilds nothing, on purpose: the shell loads the live site, so `deploy-production.yml` already updates both apps and the installed PWA (which is also the desktop app — there is no separate desktop binary). Both stores read `package.json`'s `version`. A push with publication on and a missing upload secret FAILS naming the secret, rather than building a binary nothing uploads. App Store and Play production still review each release; TestFlight is automatic, App Store submission is not. **Store distribution (2026-08-18).** The signed-release jobs were written before any developer account existed and had never run; they skip with a notice until their 12 secrets are set (names documented inline in the workflow). Three real gaps were closed when the accounts landed. **(1) iOS build number was never injected** — Android already derives `versionCode` from `github.run_number`, but the iOS archive used the committed `CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION = 1`, and App Store Connect rejects a duplicate build number, so the *second* TestFlight upload would have failed. Now injected the same way. **(2) `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` was undeclared** — `@capacitor/push-notifications` is installed and `NativePushRegistration.tsx` calls it, but Android 13+ will not even show the prompt without the manifest entry, so push failed silently rather than being denied. **(3) No privacy manifest** — required since May 2024 and enforced at UPLOAD, not review. It is wired into `project.pbxproj` in four places (build file, file reference, group, **Resources build phase**); the last one is the one that matters — without it the file sits on disk, ships in no `.ipa`, and is rejected exactly as if it had never been written. It declares the binary only; the **App Privacy questionnaire in App Store Connect is separate, filled by hand, and must not contradict it**. `NSCameraUsageDescription` is declared **ahead of the scanner it is for**: check-in is a text field today and nothing calls `getUserMedia`, but a WKWebView camera request with no usage string does not fail — iOS kills the app. Android `CAMERA` is deliberately NOT declared for the same feature, because Play shows declared permissions to users; add it in the same commit as a real scanner. **MEASURED 2026-09-07, and this row's "12 secrets are set" framing was two weeks out of date — most of the store work is DONE.** Both signed release jobs SUCCEEDED on 2026-09-05 (run `33977344491`, `publish=false`), and the `publish=true` run an hour later (`33977825909`) **uploaded the iOS build to TestFlight** and failed on exactly one thing: `GOOGLE_PLAY_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON`. So the Apple certificate, provisioning profile, App Store Connect API key and the Android upload keystore all exist as repository secrets; `apple-app-site-association` answers **200 in production** with Team ID `662XY74534`. What is genuinely left is listed below — verify against the workflow's own run history before quoting any of it, because that is how this line went stale. **(1) CLOSED 2026-09-08 — `assetlinks.json` answers 200 with THREE SHA-256 fingerprints** (Play app-signing classical, its quantum-ready sibling, and the upload cert), set as `ANDROID_CERT_SHA256_FINGERPRINTS` once the first bundle reached Internal testing. **The sequencing this row described is real and is the durable lesson**: the fingerprint that matters is Google's app-signing certificate, which the Console only reveals after a bundle is uploaded, so Android deep links cannot verify before release one. **Two things cost an operator four wrong turns and are worth carrying**: the certificates are at **Protected with Play → App signing** (`/keymanagement`) — "Test and release → Setup → App signing" no longer exists and "App integrity" now renders only "App Integrity settings have moved" — and **`npm run check:app-links` used to read `public/.well-known/` off disk**, so it reported both files "absent" while production served them correctly; it probes the live origin now. Set BOTH certs: with only the Play one, store installs verify and local installs do not, which reads as flakiness. **(2)** `GOOGLE_PLAY_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON`, needed only to AUTOMATE the Play upload — a hand-upload of the `.aab` needs no secret at all. **(3)** ~~APNs key + FCM `google-services.json` are still absent~~ — **the client config landed 2026-09-09 (#860) and this line was stale within hours**: `google-services.json` and `GoogleService-Info.plist` are both in the repository for project `ihype-d2083`, and the plist is in the Xcode **Resources build phase**, not merely on disk. **Both operator legs are DONE per the owner (2026-09-25: "APN key is already uploaded. FCM secrets already uploaded.")** — the APNs `.p8` is in Firebase and `FCM_PROJECT_ID`/`FCM_CLIENT_EMAIL`/`FCM_PRIVATE_KEY` are Worker secrets, so push should now deliver; **it has not yet been observed arriving on a handset**, and until it has, treat this as configured rather than proven. **Before those legs were set, push could not deliver** — `native-push.ts` logs and returns, so devices register tokens, `/admin` reads healthy, and nothing arrives; that is the quietest failure in the stack and the reason `docs/runbooks/push-setup.md` leads with it. `android/app/build.gradle` skips the google-services plugin when the file is missing, so a build with no config succeeds and push silently does nothing. A placeholder association file is still worse than none, since a malformed one breaks verification silently. **The standing approval risk is Apple guideline 4.2**: `server.url` points the WebView at production, which is the property that makes a web deploy reach both stores in two minutes and also the shape Apple rejects as "just a website". Push and location are the defence and should work *before* the first submission. The route now resolves `@handle` or an exact name to `artistProfileId` (looser matching would attach a fan's request to the wrong artist) and captures the fan's location ONCE — their own profile location, else the request's edge geolocation — into four nullable `requester*` columns, never updated afterwards. **The analysis is `scoreFanDemand()`, pure and tested**: time = 30-day half-life, never dropping a request for age; frequency = DISTINCT fans (one fan asking five times is one fan — only their strongest ask weighs); location = Haversine when both sides have coordinates (40 km nearby, 160 km regional), else city/state match, else a MIDDLE weight — an unknown address is not evidence of distance. PENDING only: BOOKED is done and DISMISSED is the venue saying no. Demand is added on top of the normalised heuristic score, so any act a fan asked for outranks every act nobody did; acts fans named that have no profile are surfaced with `profileId: null` ("six fans want a band you have never heard of" is what a demand radar is for). The AI pitch rewrites HEURISTIC reasons only — a demand chip ("3 fans asked · 2 nearby · this week") is a fact. **The stats board reads the same rows**: Requests = fan requests (naming the artist / received by the venue, every status); Recommendations = the live ranking (distinct venues recommending the artist / distinct acts pending at the venue). One core (`groupDemand`), two groupings; a test asserts both views agree on weight for a single venue. **And the third reader — the fan's own recommendations (same day, owner: "The recommendation and seed engines feed a big way into what a fan will be shown for their own recommendations").** Two engines actually decide what a fan hears, and neither is `src/lib/recommendations.ts`: the computed stations (`stations.ts` → `GET /api/stations/[slug]/tracks`, which the MUSIC Recommended tab reads as the `friends` station) and the discover deck (`GET /api/discover/seeds`). `summarizeRequestSignals()` (pure, in `fan-demand.ts`; reads in `src/lib/request-signals.ts`, caught to empty so a failed read never takes a station down) yields two signals from the request rows: **requested** (acts the fan asked a venue for) and **wantedAt** (acts other fans want, PENDING, at venues the fan follows or asked, distinct fans, with the venue to name). `for_you` plays hyped ∪ requested ∪ wantedAt; `friends` plays followed ∪ wantedAt (a venue you follow is an account you follow). `reasonFor` gained the two reasons after follow/hype — "You asked a venue to book them", "Fans want them at <Venue>" — derived from the same context as the `where`, so they cannot disagree with it. The deck leads with up to 5 request cards (one per act, requested acts first, each with its own reason) ahead of the co-hype pool, and co-requesters (fans who asked for the same acts) join the collaborative neighbourhood, because a fan can want an act booked without ever hyping their page. **`src/lib/recommendations.ts` is LIVE again as of later the same day (owner: "keep the recommendations engine and build it up so when activity comes in it will make those recommendations. Will say nothing yet until something makes sense")**: `GET /api/recommend` runs it for the signed-in viewer and the MUSIC Recommended tab reads that instead of the `friends` station (still in the Radio tab's list). Three signals were added so it hears everything a fan does — follows (known, 2× taste weight), fan requests (known, 3×; acts other fans want at the viewer's venues get a demand boost and the reason "Fans want them at <Venue>"; co-requesters join the collaborative neighbourhood) — and a **readiness gate**: `meta.ready` is false until the viewer has left one taste signal (hype, seed, follow or request; location alone does not count), and the tab then shows an empty state saying what will start it. | `src/components/OfflineTicketWarmer.tsx` (now a CONTROL) + `warmTicketCache` in `src/lib/private-cache.ts` + the `WARM_RESULT` reply in `public/sw.js` + `.mmm-offline-save` in `mmm.css` + `src/lib/__tests__/offline-ticket-save.test.ts` + `e2e/offline-ticket.spec.ts` | **A FAN CAN ASK FOR THEIR QR CODES AGAIN, AND THE FIRST VISIT NOW SAVES THEM AT ALL (2026-09-10, DESIGN_SYNC row 387; owner: "Make sure fan QR tickets can be redownloaded for bad service situations as well").** **The first-load hole is the real bug and it is the same one `warmDoorCache` was written to avoid.** `warmTicketCache` read `navigator.serviceWorker.controller`, which is **null on the first load of a session** — the worker claims a page on activate, after that navigation has already been served — so a member who opened the wallet ONCE and left for the venue had cached nothing, silently. The door control's comment even says the wallet "gets a second load for free"; that is true only if they come back, and buy-on-the-bus-open-at-the-door is precisely the journey that does not. It awaits `serviceWorker.ready` now, like the door, and **the e2e proves it on a SINGLE load** — the spec used to open the wallet twice and its own comment named the null controller as the reason, so the workaround was documenting the defect. **The control exists because automatic warming cannot cover the cases that strand someone**: a browser that evicted the cache (Safari clears unused site storage after seven days, and a ticket bought a month ahead is exactly that), and a TRANSFER, which reissues every `serializedId` in the order so the page cached under the old id is a dead link and the new one was never fetched. The automatic warm stays — most members press nothing. **The worker reports what it actually stored, and that is not decoration.** `WARM_TICKETS` now answers on a `MessageChannel` port with `{stored, failed}`; the page reports the real count, distinguishes a partial save, and resolves **`no-answer`** when a worker too old to reply says nothing rather than showing a tick. **Do not make this control optimistic**: an older worker, a 404 on a transferred ticket and full storage all look identical from the page, and a tick over an empty cache is worse than no control because the fan stops worrying and arrives at a door with nothing — the same defect class as the resale button in row 385. The port is optional, so a page from an older bundle keeps warming exactly as before. **THE SAME READ WAS IN `clearPrivateCaches()`, AND THAT ONE IS A PRIVACY BUG** — found by scanning for siblings, not by a failure. Sign-out and account deletion posted `CLEAR_PRIVATE` to `controller`, which is null until the worker activates and calls `clients.claim()`, so on a first load **the previous account's ticket QR pages survived on a shared device**, in the one cache nothing else ever wipes. Fixed the same way with one difference that matters: **do not make it a plain `await`** — both callers navigate immediately afterwards, so a late resolve unloads the page before the message is sent, which is worse than the bug. Synchronous controller path first, `serviceWorker.ready` raced against 1.5s otherwise, callers navigate in a `finally`. **Sign-out must never be blocked by a cache operation.** Cleared by the same scan and not worth re-checking: `ServiceWorkerRegister` reads the controller on purpose (first install vs update), the push toggle already awaits `ready`, the referral beacon and `BarcodeDetector` both have fallbacks, and `Notification.permission` is read at the moment of use. **Not built, on purpose**: a per-ticket save on the ticket page. The wallet is the one surface that knows every ticket the member holds, one press covers all of them, and a second control saving one ticket invites the belief that the others are saved too. |

It was false in every case, for three compounding reasons, all of them the retired URL: `/tickets/<id>` is a REDIRECT to `/app/me/tickets/<id>`, `/app` is in `NETWORK_ONLY_PATHS`, and the fetch handler returned on that gate BEFORE reaching the ticket branch — so the one page this cache exists for was never stored, whether or not it had been viewed. The `WARM_TICKETS` message refused the live prefix for the same reason, and `OfflineTicketWarmer` was mounted on no page. Now `isTicketDetail()` is the one matcher (both prefixes, trailing segment REQUIRED — the list is never cached, which is what the 2026-09-02 sweep took out of the precache), it is consulted before the network-only gate, and `MmmTickets` warms every real ticket the member holds. Demo rows are excluded: their ids resolve to nothing. **AMENDED 2026-09-05 — that was still not enough, and only a browser could say so**: the cached HTML rendered as "Loading…" offline because hydration could not load the route's own chunk and tore the server-rendered ticket down (DESIGN_SYNC row 351). the route answers `private, no-store` and walk item 43 asserts the response text contains no code. **Do not add the code to the manifest to make anything easier**; a stolen door phone would then hold every ticket to the show. **(2) The server's answer wins.** An offline admission is recorded PENDING with the moment the fan walked in and posted when the network returns, carrying `scannedAt`; the route honours a plausible claim (past, within seven days — `resolveScanTimestamp`) so the record holds the door's time, not the reconnect's, and answers **409** when the same ticket was also scanned elsewhere — the row on the phone then reads "also scanned elsewhere" and the phone never settles that by itself. **The gate is one module**: `canWorkTheDoor()` admits the venue's owner, the headliner's owner, the creator and an admin — the cancel route's four — and both door routes and the page read it. **Reading the QR**: `BarcodeDetector` where the platform has it, `jsqr` otherwise (dynamic import — a page that never opens the camera never downloads a decoder); Neither can answer **can somebody buy a ticket right now** — and that is not hypothetical: every ticketed event created through the app was permanently unbuyable earlier the same day while /admin showed a clear queue and normal traffic, because nothing was queued and nothing was wrong with traffic. A board of queues structurally cannot see a feature that is simply off. **18 capabilities, each carrying the runtime flags that gate it, the dependencies it needs configured, the workbench queues whose items belong to it, a `METRIC_CATALOGUE` id for "somebody used this", and the `feature-health.ts` journey the nightly proves it with.** Six states, ordered worst-first: **BLOCKED** (offered to members and cannot work — the whole reason this exists) · ATTENTION · UNKNOWN · OFF · IDLE · OK. **The load-bearing distinction is BLOCKED vs OFF**: a missing dependency behind a flag that is OFF is not blocked, because the product is not offering it and a permanently red row is noise at the top of a board — the failure that got the nightly's own gate ignored. Tie-break within a band is **age, not count**, the same rule `orderByUrgency` already follows. **Three rules inherited rather than reinvented**: `null` is never 0 (a flag, count or metric that could not be read is UNKNOWN and renders a dash — and a named queue the workbench could not produce is UNKNOWN, not clear); activity comes from `METRIC_CATALOGUE` rather than a sixth definition of "uploads this week"; issues come from `getWorkbenchQueues()` rather than counting rows again. The catalogue imports no `@/lib/db` and the reads live in the `-data` half, the same split as `analytics-engine`/`analytics-metrics`. A test asserts every journey it names is a real journey. **The console is now five domains behind a `?tab=` strip** (Overview · Activity · Support · Finance · System), resolved on the SERVER with only the active tab rendered — the shape `/admin/review` already used, which is why the strip is plain links and not a client component. **Every one of the 25 panels MOVED; none was rewritten or dropped**, and the transform was checked by conservation: each of the 745 body lines lands in exactly one tab, verified afterwards by diffing headings, component mounts, element ids and hrefs against the original (24/13/2/24, nothing missing). The single two-column grid that held nine panels belonging to four different domains is re-opened per tab. **Queries are still one `Promise.all`, so every tab still pays for all of them** — making them per-tab is a real improvement and deliberately a SEPARATE change, because doing it in the same pass would have made the conservation check meaningless. **AMENDED 2026-09-15 (DESIGN_SYNC row 477) — A CAPABILITY WHOSE WORK IS A CRON GOES DARK WHEN THE CRON STOPS, AND THIS BOARD COULD NOT SEE IT.** It judged every row on flags, configuration and queues, so `payouts` was judged on `needs: ['stripe']` — `isStripeConfigured()`, i.e. "an environment value starting with `sk_` is present", the same key `ticketing` already covers through the stricter `payments` check. The row could only go BLOCKED in a state where its sibling was BLOCKED too, so it **carried no signal of its own**, while the one thing that ever issues a transfer — `triggerShowPayouts()` on the `0 13 * * *` slot — was invisible: a stopped payout cron read **OK** with every act and venue unpaid. `ad-settlement` shares that slot and is the same case for `advertising` (a sponsorship's unused remainder is refunded only there). **`job` on a `FeatureDefinition` names the `cron-alive:<job>` key; stale → BLOCKED, unreadable → UNKNOWN, and an entry the board never asked about → UNKNOWN, never healthy.** Four rules: the reason **names the job and says it has not run**, never "is not configured", because the job IS configured and armed and "configuration" sends an operator somewhere else; liveness comes from `admin-routine-data.ts`'s `readLiveness`, **exported rather than rewritten**, since two readers of `cron-alive:` with two ideas of what an absent key means is the drift that file's own header warns about; **OFF still beats a stale job**, by the same BLOCKED-vs-OFF rule above; and **name a `job` only when the capability's member-visible work happens THERE and nowhere else** — a cron that merely supports something a member drives themselves does not block it, which is why 2 of the 18 rows carry one. |

Reinforce daily vs weekly todo and nightly tasks").** The workbench's body is the ROUTINE BOARD — Daily · Weekly · Monthly · Automated behind one strip. **Cadence is derived, never assigned**: `cadenceForQueue(slaHours)` files a queue with a promise of 96h or less under daily and anything looser or unpromised under weekly, because a four-day promise cannot be kept on a weekly look and the 12:00 UTC digest is what catches an item between looks. The hand-written duties (`OPERATOR_DUTIES`) are what is SENT to the operator or done by hand: last night's board, the 07:00 ops mail, the Monday report, the six-hourly backup's keys, the advisory audit numbers, and the monthly restore drill — the last reading DUE with its live age from `RESTORE_DRILL_VERIFIED_AT`. **AUTOMATED restates every dispatcher job in `workers/cron.ts` and both scheduled workflows, in the operator's terms, so nobody does the machine's work by hand** — payouts and settlement at 13:00, backups every six hours, the acceptance walk at 07:00 — with last-run from the `cron-alive:*` keys the jobs already write. **That is a second copy of the schedule on purpose**: the cron Worker is bundled alone and imports nothing from `src/`, and `admin-routine.test.ts` fails on any disagreement in either direction, plus a liveness key no route writes or a route writing one the board calls untracked. `stale` (key expired), `unknown` (KV unreadable) and `not tracked` (no key) are three facts and render as three words; do not collapse them. Relative times render against `board.now` from the server, never the client clock, or hydration mismatches. **The phone**: measured at 393 on the built worker, `/admin` went from 6,538px (7.7 screens) to 1,889px (2.2) with nothing dropped — the feature board folds its OFF/IDLE/OK rows under one summary (`<details>`, every card still in the document worst-first, which is what the e2e counts), the hero loses its subtitle, the tab strip and console index scroll sideways, lists scroll inside their panel past 300px, stat cards go two-up. | `scripts/e2e-workerd.mjs` (`ADMIN_DEVICE_SECRET`) + `e2e/admin-console.spec.ts` | **Nothing had ever rendered a single `/admin` page in a browser, and the harness was the reason (2026-09-03).** The admin layout's device gate reads `ADMIN_DEVICE_SECRET` through `readRuntimeEnv`; `e2e-workerd.mjs` did not forward it, so every admin URL redirected to `/admin/device-register` and any spec would have measured the redirect rather than the console. | `scripts/lighthouse-budget.mjs` + `scripts/lib/lighthouse-report.mjs` | **The CI performance REPORT — as of 2026-08-05 it no longer gates.** It still measures every page and still writes the job summary; a breach just doesn't fail the build. Set `LIGHTHOUSE_BUDGET_ENFORCE=1` to restore gating. **Do not "fix" a red pipeline by widening a budget — that is the failure mode this change exists to end.** Ten days of history: three recalibrations (2026-07-27, 07-29, 08-05), zero regressions caught, every failure the instrument rather than the code. The mechanism is `throttlingMethod: 'simulate'`, whose mobileSlow4G preset carries **`cpuSlowdownMultiplier: 4`** — Lighthouse measures real CPU time on the runner and multiplies it, so a shared-runner hiccup becomes an 8x swing in modelled LCP. Neither the median-of-5 nor the re-sample helps, because both draw from the same machine in the same minute: **medians suppress independent noise, and runner contention is correlated.** That is how `/` measured 8802ms "confirmed on re-sample" and 5000ms on the next run of the same code. Before re-enforcing, make it an absolute measure rather than a relative one — production-mode server, or `throttlingMethod: 'provided'`, or a same-job baseline comparison. On a confirmed LCP failure it now also names the **LCP element** (Lighthouse's `largest-contentful-paint-element`); `/` was twice diagnosed from inference alone (the DB transaction, then the hero PNG) and both were wrong. A transient `NO_LCP` trace flake no longer aborts the smoke run — it retries 3x and reports "not measured". **Read the job summary before re-running it.** Every run now writes a measured-vs-budget markdown table to `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY`; previously a failure printed only the breached metric, so learning *why* meant downloading a JSON artifact or grepping ~300KB of wrangler request logs — which is why two failures on 2026-07-29 were both written off as flake. They were not: both were `/info` scoring **0.74 against a 0.75 budget**, the same page and metric twice. `/info` had inherited `/about`'s thin-marketing budget when that page was retired into it, despite being a six-panel hub with a client tab strip and two live Prisma aggregates; it now sits in the data-backed tier with `/discover`/`/shows`. A page over budget is also **re-sampled** now, and only fails the build if the *same metric* exceeds twice — a median of 5 still straddles the line when a budget sits on a page's median. **That re-sample only happens when `LIGHTHOUSE_BUDGET_ENFORCE=1`, as of 2026-08-18 — it is a second full 5-run audit of the slowest pages in the set, and confirming a breach that cannot fail anything buys nothing.** It cost PR #716 its CI run: four of six pages went over, `/discover` took 7.5 minutes and `/shows` 8.75 against ~35s for a passing page, and the step was killed by its `timeout-minutes` **five seconds after its own script printed "all checks passed"** — a green-looking script inside a red job. The cap is now 30. **A timeout here is not a budget failure**: read which step died before concluding anything about a page. Numbers are `wrangler dev`/workerd on a shared runner: a relative regression detector, not production UX. **`PAGES` must not list a route that redirects**, and the reason is worse than mislabelling. **A `redirect()` under a `loading.tsx` boundary cannot answer with a 307** — the shell is already streaming, so Next emits a **200 carrying `<meta http-equiv="refresh" content="1;url=…">`**, and `src/app/loading.tsx` is such a boundary **for every route in the app**. Verified 2026-08-18 by building with and without it: `/discover` and `/studio` return 200 with it, 307 without. So a redirect entry scores an almost-empty shell and then has the document swapped under it mid-run. That is what #716's `/shows` CLS **0.753** and `/discover` **0.371** are — the swap, not a page — and `/listen`'s **0.92 "PASS"** is the identical artefact wearing the opposite face: a blank shell scores well and proves nothing, which is how an entry measuring nothing stayed green for weeks. All three are replaced by `/app/map` and `/app/music/discover`, measured authenticated (the only way to reach them), on conservative provisional budgets. **`export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'` does NOT fix the 200** — tried, built, measured, no effect; the boundary is what matters, not the render mode. Fixing it for real means moving the alias into `next.config.mjs` `redirects()`, which resolves in the router before any rendering. |

Sentry then showed `POST /api/discover/seeds/[id]/save` timing out at 750ms **158 times and counting**, each one silently halving that member's limit. The default is now **1800ms**, with **KV cut 1500 → 1000ms** so the worst case (both deadlines expire) is 2.8s — still under the 3s the module's own comment has always refused, which is the constraint that keeps this from becoming a blanket 2500. Read the arithmetic above `DEFAULT_DO_TIMEOUT_MS` before changing either number; they are a combined budget, not two independent ones. |

Neither run proves the Worker holds the SAME pair — that is read off an upload's `scan` array, which says `configured: false` when it does not. (2) melodic/motif matching stays `configured: false` (ACRCloud identify covers exact/near-exact recordings, i.e. layer 1; cover-song/humming detection is a separate ACRCloud bucket config, not enabled). Response now includes a `scan` array (per-layer results) consumed by the new upload UI. No video.")**: the rule is PLAYABILITY on the two players iHYPE has — Safari/WKWebView and Chromium — so `src/lib/validate-upload.ts`'s `sniffAudio()` reads the first 64 KB and accepts MP3, AAC/M4A (ADTS too), WAV and FLAC; **refuses Ogg, AIFF and WebM with the reason** (each plays on one player and not the other); and **refuses video however it is dressed** — a QuickTime/M4V brand, an AVI RIFF, or a `vide` handler in the head. ALAC-in-M4A is the known gap (Safari only, indistinguishable from AAC by brand). Extensions in `media-validation.ts` match. **Caps are sized for lossless and bounded by the Worker, not taste**: 60 MB a file (a six-minute WAV, a twelve-minute FLAC), 70 MB request, 1 GB a profile — the route reads the whole file into a 128 MB isolate to sniff, measure and scan it, so going higher means streaming the body to R2 and scanning only its head. `audio-duration.ts` now reads FLAC (STREAMINFO) and MP4 (`mvhd`) durations as well as WAV and MP3; null still means unknown, never a guess. |
| `src/lib/loudness.ts` + `measure-upload-loudness.ts` + `track-gain.ts` + `src/lib/http-range.ts` + the `/cdn/[...key]` route + `describeAudio()` in `src/lib/audio-duration.ts` + six columns on `ArtistMediaAsset` + `docs/audio-quality.md` | **THE AUDIO PATH ITSELF (2026-09-09, DESIGN_SYNC row 370; owner: "Keeping looking for opportunities to improve the quality of audio").** Everything between an artist's file and a listener's speaker had treated the audio as bytes. **Read `docs/audio-quality.md` before changing any of it — it also records the one thing deliberately NOT built.** **Loudness**: BS.1770-4 programme loudness measured in the uploader's BROWSER (the Worker has 128 MB and 1500 ms and cannot decode audio), stored on the track, applied at playback. **Playback only ever ATTENUATES, and that rule is what makes a client-reported number safe** — a track measured quieter than target is left alone, and so is an unmeasured one, so under-reporting buys exactly what not measuring buys (nothing) and over-reporting only makes the artist quieter. There is no lie that wins; **do not "fix" the missing boost without reading why**, since boost needs a Web Audio `GainNode` through `GlobalMediaPlayer` and must re-measure TRUE peak first (`peakDbfs` is a SAMPLE peak, and a signal exceeds it between samples). The gain is a MULTIPLIER of the member's own volume at all four sites that set `audio.volume`, never a replacement, or the volume slider stops working on levelled tracks; **an ad break is never levelled**, because a spot is levelled by whoever cut it. K-weighting coefficients are derived PER SAMPLE RATE, not copied from the standard's 48 kHz table — that table over a 44.1 kHz file mis-weights the spectrum and still returns a plausible figure, so the tests assert the standard's own calibration (a 1 kHz sine reads its own dBFS level) rather than our output. Three traps the tests hold: `Number(null)` is 0 and 0 is a legal loudness, so an absent field must never be coerced or an upload that measured nothing gets turned DOWN 12 dB; attenuation is floored at -12 dB so a broken reading cannot silence a recording; and the filter uses one shared scratch buffer, because a copy per channel per stage over a decoded lossless master is a quarter-gigabyte on a phone. **Ranges**: the CDN route serves 206/416 and advertises `Accept-Ranges`, using `head()` for the size rather than fetching a body to cancel — **`bytes=-N` is a SUFFIX**, and reading it as "0 to N" serves the wrong audio while still looking like a working 206; a malformed header falls back to 200, never 416. **Shape**: `describeAudio()` returns codec, duration, sample rate, channels and bit depth from the one header read the duration parser was already doing, `parseAudioDuration()` is now a wrapper so no caller changed, and **every field is nullable where a null means UNREAD, never a default** — AAC gets `bitDepth: null` rather than the 16 every encoder writes into the `mp4a` template. |

Renders on `/artists/[slug]` (Tracks tab, `profileType="ARTIST"`) and `/promoters/[slug]` (Crate tab, `profileType="DJ"`) for the owner only. Shows a staggered, sequential reveal of the 4 scan layers after upload — this animates the reveal of a result the server already computed synchronously (no background job queue exists in this codebase to genuinely poll against; the design spec's `POST returns async job` / `GET .../scan/:jobId` contract was not implemented as literal async infra for that reason). Also has an optional cover-art field (`ArtistMediaAsset.artworkUrl`, same image-vetting/storage pattern as profile graphics) rendered by `ArtistMediaPlaylist.tsx` via the pre-existing `previewImageUrl` field. |
| `src/lib/stripe.ts` + `src/lib/show-payouts.ts` | **Fixed a severe payout-routing bug (2026-07-14).** `createTicketPaymentIntent` used to set `transfer_data.destination` to a single Connect account (venue if present, else artist) with no `amount` sub-field — Stripe therefore transferred the ENTIRE captured charge to that one party, not the charter's 70/20/10 split; the other two parties' shares were only ever computed as `AccountsPayableEntry` rows that nothing ever actually paid out (`AccountsPayableStatus.RELEASED` was never set anywhere in the codebase). Fixed via the standard "separate charges and transfers" pattern: `createTicketPaymentIntent` no longer sets `transfer_data` at all (the full charge captures to the platform's own Stripe balance), and a new `createPayoutTransfer()` issues one real `stripe.transfers.create()` per `AccountsPayableEntry` (idempotent via the entry's own id). `triggerShowPayouts()` (the `show-payouts` cron job) was completely rewritten — it used to bypass `AccountsPayableEntry` entirely and just email an *estimate* computed from a different, wrong formula (hardcoded 5%/85% split); it now processes real PENDING entries for ended shows and actually pays them, emailing the real recipient once money has moved. Tax entries (no Connect account) stay `PENDING` — remittance is a manual accounting matter, out of scope. New `AccountsPayableEntry.stripeTransferId` field records the real transfer id. **AMENDED 2026-08-28 — the platform-balance capture described above is now only the FALLBACK.** Connect was signed up for with **Stripe** as both `fees_collector` and `losses_collector` (sellers collect directly, hosted onboarding, Express Dashboard, Stripe liable for a seller's unrecoverable loss), because the org has no reserve and no headcount for tax or ticket support. `createTicketPaymentIntent` is **deleted** — it was unreferenced, and the live path is `createTicketCheckoutSession`. `POST /api/shows/[showId]/tickets` now resolves one of three settlement modes and records it on the order (`TicketOrder.settlementMode`/`settlementAccountId`): **`VENUE_DIRECT`** (charge created ON the venue's account via `createVenueDirectCheckoutSession`'s `{ stripeAccount }` — the venue is merchant of record, and iHYPE's `application_fee_amount` carries the artist's 70% and the promoter's 10% into the platform balance for the payout cron; the venue's 20% and tax are **not** payable rows, because that money never left the venue), **`DESTINATION`** (no venue merchant, artist payout account exists — `transfer_data` + `application_fee_amount`, and deliberately **no `on_behalf_of`**: the owner's call is that iHYPE stays the name on the purchase), and **`PLATFORM`** (nobody connected — the 2026-07-14 design above, unchanged). The 70/20/10 arithmetic in `src/lib/ticketing.ts` is identical in all three; only who holds the money in between changes. `TICKET_RESERVE_PERCENT` (1.5%, a checkout line item) is charged only when `platformBearsRisk` is true — modes 2 and 3 — and `refundTicketPaymentIntent` takes `{ wasDestinationCharge }` so a mode-2 refund also reverses the transfer and the application fee. **Do not re-add `debit_negative_balances`**: a platform that is not liable cannot debit its connected accounts, Stripe recovers instead, and the call was against a v2 account where it never executed. |
| `src/app/api/tickets/[serializedId]/transfer/route.ts` | **Fixed a real double-use bug (2026-07-14).** Transferring a ticket used to just email the recipient the *existing* serializedId — since every ticket lookup (QR page, both scan endpoints) has no ownership check beyond the id itself, the original buyer (who may have already opened/screenshotted/printed the QR before transferring) still held a fully valid, scannable copy; whoever scanned first won. Now reissues every ticket in the order with a brand-new `serializedId` (`createSerializedTicketId()`) on transfer — the old QR 404s immediately, only the freshly emailed one works. `holderName`/`holderEmail` update to the recipient; `reassignCount`/`reassignedAt` increment (the same fields the venue-staff "Venue reassignment" feature on `/tickets/[serializedId]` already used). |
| `src/app/api/shows/[showId]/scan/route.ts` | **Fixed a real scan-replay race (2026-07-14).** Used to `findFirst` then check `status` in JS then a separate `update` — two concurrent scans of the same ticket could both pass the check before either write landed, both reporting success. Now atomic via `updateMany({ where: { id, status: 'VALID' }, data: {...} })` and checking `result.count`, matching the pattern `/api/tickets/[serializedId]/scan/route.ts` already used correctly. |
| `src/lib/ticket-order-state.ts` + `src/app/api/shows/[showId]/lineup/*` | **Lineup & Split Agreement (2026-07-19, DESIGN_SYNC row 226) — real multi-act payout splits, built only after an explicit product decision** ("only applies when multiple acts are booked for a single event; splits are proposed by venue and accepted or rejected by artists"). New `ShowLineupSlot` model — a show with zero rows is completely unaffected, same single-headliner payout as always. When lineup slots exist, `buildPayableEntries()` splits `artistPayoutCents` across ACCEPTED slots proportionally (siblings sum to `Show.artistPayoutPercent`), last slot absorbing the rounding remainder so the split always sums exactly, no leakage. Booking-lock gating reuses the existing DRAFT-shows-are-private mechanism (`/shows/[slug]/page.tsx` already hides DRAFT shows) rather than a new visibility flag — a lineup-pending show stays DRAFT until every act accepts, then `PATCH .../lineup/respond` flips it straight to SCHEDULED in the same request as the deciding accept. Real UI at `/shows/[slug]/lineup` (split visualization, accept/decline, venue composer). |
| **Deleted 2026-08-12 (commit b7fb6205):** `src/app/api/tickets/[serializedId]/refund/route.ts` | **Buyer refunds are REMOVED — all ticket sales are final (product ruling).** The organizer cancellation flow (`/api/shows/[showId]/cancel`) is the only path that refunds, and it refunds every captured order except those with a scanned ticket. The rest of this row describes the deleted route as it was, kept because the money-path rehearsal was still testing it from this description two weeks after deletion: **Refund was real (2026-07-14).** Used to only file a `SupportRequest` and leave the order/tickets/capacity completely untouched — the UI told buyers "our support team will process your refund" but nothing further ever happened automatically. Now calls `refundTicketPaymentIntent()` (a real Stripe refund) for `CAPTURED` orders, or `cancelTicketPaymentIntent()` for `RESERVED` ones (never actually charged), then `refundCapturedTicketOrder()`/`voidReservedTicketOrder()` (`src/lib/ticket-order-state.ts`) void the order, void unscanned tickets, release sold capacity, and void any still-`PENDING` `AccountsPayableEntry` rows so the payout cron can never pay out a refunded order — safe by construction since payouts only run for `ENDED` shows and refunds are only allowed >48h before a show starts, so a refundable order's payable entries are guaranteed still `PENDING`. Blocks refund if any ticket in the order was already scanned. New `TicketOrder.refundedAt`/`stripeRefundId` fields. |
| `src/app/app/me/payouts/page.tsx` (History tab) + `src/components/payouts/PayoutsHistoryPanel.tsx` — `/payouts` and `/me/payouts` both redirect here | **New (2026-07-14), consolidated into the tabbed hub (2026-07-26, DESIGN_SYNC row 249).** Real payout history for the signed-in user's own profiles — every `AccountsPayableEntry` that actually moved money via `triggerShowPayouts()`, split into Received (`RELEASED`) and Pending (rendered by `src/components/payouts/PayoutsHistoryPanel.tsx`, extracted verbatim from the former standalone page). **CORRECTED 2026-09-15 (DESIGN_SYNC row 474) — this line used to end "Distinct from `/payout/[id]`, which shows a computed 70/20/10 projection for one show regardless of whether it's actually been paid out yet", which described the defect as a design.** That page called itself "Where the money went. Here's every dollar, accounted for." while reading no order, no payable and no Connect account; it sums the real rows now. **The rule that generalises, and the one to keep: `src/lib/payout-release.ts` holds the FIVE conditions a payable must meet before `triggerShowPayouts()` pays it — a transferable category, a profile, a Connect account on it, an ENDED show, and `PAYOUT_HOLD_DAYS` past the date — and the cron reads them from there.** Every surface that tells a member when their money arrives asks `describePayableRelease()`; three of them used to state one sentence unconditionally and it was false for a tax entry (remitted by hand, never released by anything), false for a payee with no payout account (skipped silently on every run, for ever), and ten days early even when everything was in order. **Do not write a second copy of either constant** — the test refuses one, because two copies is exactly how "Same night" outlived the hold by three weeks. **And `Profile.name` is not the only figure on these pages that is a claim: `ticketsSoldCount` increments at RESERVATION, so it is a capacity counter and never a revenue input** — money comes from CAPTURED `TicketOrder` rows, whose own `artistPayoutCents`/`venuePayoutCents`/`promoterPayoutCents` are the split as it was actually computed (the promoter tenth is zero with no HYPE link, and redistributes 7:2). |

| `src/lib/i18n/dictionaries/*.json` (the eleven non-English files) + `scripts/apply-i18n-batch.mjs` | **THE MEMBER-FACING SURFACE IS TRANSLATED IN ALL ELEVEN LOCALES, AND THE ADMIN CONSOLE DELIBERATELY IS NOT (2026-09-10, DESIGN_SYNC row 386; owner: "Do it").** **Measure before you translate: the raw gap was 646 called keys missing from every locale, and only 147 of them were member-facing.** The other 499 are `admin*` namespaces plus six components that live in `src/components/admin/` under member-shaped names (`bulkActions`, `reportPageBulkButtons`, `featureToggle`, `playlistActions`, `playlistCreateForm`, `socialPostCopy`), `ModerationActions` (imported only by `/admin/moderation`) and `statusPage` (`/status` redirects a non-admin at line 81). **A namespace without an `admin` prefix is not evidence of a member surface — check the importer.** Translating those 499 would have been days of work on a console this product deliberately ships in English. **75 of the 147 were `doorScanner`** — the venue door module shipped the same morning (row 376), so a venue in a non-English market got an English scanner at the door, offline, under time pressure, on the one surface with the least tolerance for hesitation. That is the block to do first whenever this recurs: **translate by where the member is standing, not by namespace size.** **Use `scripts/apply-i18n-batch.mjs`, always `--dry-run` first.** It refuses a key not called in `src/` (a typo would otherwise sit dead in a dictionary forever), refuses an empty translation, never overwrites an existing key, and **refuses stray scripts per locale** — Cyrillic spliced into an Arabic word has happened here and is invisible to anyone reviewing a language they do not read. Identical-to-English only warns, because brand terms and loanwords legitimately match. **The paired rule, learned the hard way earlier the same day: changing the English fallback under an EXISTING key leaves eleven dictionaries translating the retired sentence.** Two keys did exactly that when advertising moved to monthly sponsorship and told a Spanish sponsor their unspent budget would be refunded. **Rename the key when the meaning changes**, so every locale falls back to correct English until a translator catches up, and re-run the before/after comparison of `extract-i18n-keys.mjs` to prove no key changed meaning under its old name. **A dictionary key nothing calls is refused by `i18n-parity.test.ts` as of 2026-09-14 (row 419)**: live means extracted, OR under a template-key family (`t(\`ns.${x}\`)`), OR named as a dotted string literal anywhere in `src/` — prune by that rule, never by name; 1,931 dead entries went that day. **And a template or variable key — `t(\`ns.${id}\`)`, `t(row.labelKey, …)` — is refused in member-facing code by `i18n-enum-labels.test.ts` (row 422)**: such a key is invisible to the extractor and therefore to the applier and the untranslated audit, so it is English in every locale forever while every instrument calls it done; 29 families were. Enumeration labels translate at the draw through `src/lib/i18n-enum-labels.ts`, a switch of literal `t()` calls keyed on the English the enumeration declares. **Still open and NOT what this row closed**: a dictionary gap is not the same defect as a string never wrapped in `t()` at all. `src/app/app/me/notifications/page.tsx` still renders "Me · Settings" and "Notifications" as literal JSX, and it is not alone; no instrument in this repository measures that, which is why the count in the backlog has never been verified. |
| `src/lib/format-locale.ts` + `getServerI18n()` in `src/lib/i18n/server.ts` + `src/lib/__tests__/locale-formatting.test.ts` | **DATES AND FIGURES ARE FORMATTED IN THE MEMBER'S LOCALE, AND A HARDCODED `'en-US'` OR A BARE `toLocaleString()` IN MEMBER-FACING CODE FAILS THE BUILD (2026-09-14, DESIGN_SYNC row 424).** Until this date 65 sites passed `'en-US'` and 79 passed nothing, so every member read English dates and English number separators beside translated copy, and no i18n instrument here could see it because none of them reads a `Date`. `formatDate(locale, date, opts)`, `formatNumber(locale, n)` and `formatUsd(locale, cents, 0 | 2 | 'auto')` take the product `Locale`: a client component gets it from `useI18n()` (`const { locale, t } = useI18n()`), a server component from `getServerI18n()` — ONE cookie read for both `t` and `locale`, so they cannot disagree — and a lib takes it as a REQUIRED parameter (never an optional default, which is English silently). **English is pinned to `en-US` and its output is byte-identical to before**, asserted by the test; do not change that pin, because every measurement in this repository runs in English. **Money goes through `formatUsd`, never `'$' + n.toLocaleString()`** — Intl places the currency mark the way each language does. The gate's ALLOWLIST is the set of surfaces with no member locale to read (email, cron, OG image, poster, admin), each with a reason and each required to still excuse something. `User` carries no locale column, which is why email stays English; storing one is the change that would let a notification follow the member. **AMENDED the same day (row 425) — a lib string DRAWN RAW is the sibling defect, and no instrument sees it**: `showRowTrail()`'s "On sale", `formatTicketPrice()`'s "Free", `describeDayKeys()`'s "Any day", `venue-dashboard`'s "After … show ends", `mmm-me`'s row fallbacks and `timeAgo()`'s five strings were all English in every locale. **A lib returns DATA or an English KEY; the component that draws it translates** (`showTrailLabel`/`ticketPriceLabel`/`meActivityFallbackTitle` in `i18n-enum-labels.ts`; `describeDayKeys` returns a shape; `MmmActivityRow` carries `{ unit, n }`, never a sentence). `timeAgo()` is gone — `formatRelativeAge()` on `Intl.RelativeTimeFormat` keeps English at "5m ago" and gives every other locale its own. When a lib's return value is printed by a component, read the return sites: that is the only way this class is found. |
| `Show.timeZone` + `formatDoorTime()`/`isValidTimeZone()` in `src/lib/format-locale.ts` + `src/lib/zoned-time.ts` + `formatShowTime()` in `src/lib/utils.ts` + `src/lib/__tests__/door-time-guard.test.ts` | **A SHOW'S START IS AN INSTANT; THE DOOR TIME IS THE VENUE'S WALL CLOCK, AND EVERY READER NAMES IT (2026-09-14, DESIGN_SYNC row 464).** Until this column the product stored only the instant, so every reader formatted it in whatever zone the RUNTIME was set to — UTC on a Cloudflare Worker — and the public page that sells the ticket showed a 9pm Saturday show in Portland as "Sunday, March 15 · 1:00 AM". **Ingestion was never wrong**: the event creator is a client component, so the browser resolves the wall clock correctly; what was lost is which clock it was. `Show.timeZone` is captured from `Intl.DateTimeFormat().resolvedOptions().timeZone` at creation, validated by the route and NULLABLE — a show that predates it renders on the runtime's clock and says so. **Format a show start ONLY through `formatDoorTime` (or `formatShowTime`, its wrapper), never `formatDate`**: `door-time-guard.test.ts` fails the build on a line naming `.startsAt` beside a zoneless formatter, with the one non-show `startsAt` allowed by name. Three rules inside the helper, each of which has already bitten: a clock time always adds `timeZoneName: 'short'` and a caller cannot turn it off; an unknown zone degrades rather than throwing; and `dateStyle`/`timeStyle` are refused by the SIGNATURE, because Intl throws outright when either meets `timeZoneName` — which is why `formatShowTime` is components now. **The EDIT form converts both ways** (`zoned-time.ts`, offsets found by iterating through `Intl`, never a DST table): the field speaks the venue's clock, names it, and an edit only ever ESTABLISHES a zone, never reassigns one. Read the browser's zone in an EFFECT, never in render, or the server's UTC and the client's disagree in the hydrated input. `locale-formatting.test.ts` counts these two formatters too — without that, this conversion would have emptied six of its allowlist entries and shrunk that gate while reading green. **AMENDED 2026-09-22 (DESIGN_SYNC row 501) — THE GUARD IS LINE-BASED AND THREE HELPERS ESCAPED IT.** `door-time-guard.test.ts` refuses a line naming `.startsAt` beside a zoneless formatter, so a helper that takes the instant as a PARAMETER — `formatShowClock()` in `show-row.ts`, `dayParts()` in `MmmTickets.tsx`, the month/day split in `ProfileRow.tsx` — never wrote `.startsAt` on the offending line and formatted in the runtime's zone unchallenged: the wallet read “Doors 00:00” and the venue calendar “12:00 AM” for the show the ticket page renders as 8:00 PM EDT, both on the built worker. Each takes `timeZone` now (`MmmMeTicket.timeZone`, `ProfileRow`'s `timeZone` prop, a third argument on `formatShowClock`), and the guard has a second half, `SHOW_TIME_HELPERS`: the three files may name no zoneless formatter on any line that does not also name `timeZone`. **A line-based guard sees the call site, never the helper; when a rule is “format X only through Y”, list the helpers that format X and hold them to it by file.** Measured after: “Doors 8:00 PM EDT” and “OCT 2 · 8:00 PM EDT”. |
| `src/components/I18nProvider.tsx` (`initialLocale` / `initialDictionary`) + `src/app/layout.tsx` (`<html lang dir>`) + `getServerDictionary()` in `src/lib/i18n/server.ts` + `e2e/locale-hydration.spec.ts` | **THE CLIENT i18n PROVIDER STARTS FROM WHAT THE SERVER RENDERED, NEVER FROM ENGLISH (2026-09-14, DESIGN_SYNC row 426).** The root layout reads the locale and its dictionary once (`getServerI18n()` + `getServerDictionary()`), renders `<html lang dir>` from it, and hands both to `I18nProvider` as initial state — until it did, every string a client component translated arrived in English for every non-English member (the header, the dock, every control in the shell) and flipped after hydration, under a document that said `lang="en"`. **Never reset the provider's initial state to `'en'` or `{}`**, and never read the locale in a mount effect except when NO cookie was sent (a first visit, where the server had nothing to honour). English's dictionary is `{}`, so an English member's payload is unchanged. `e2e/locale-hydration.spec.ts` is the proof and belongs to the browser suite: it loads two pages with `ihype_locale=es` and asserts, in the RESPONSE BODY (the DOM would lie — the old effect rewrote it after mount), `lang="es"` and a client component's Spanish. It was written expecting a React hydration mismatch and the pre-fix run reported none — the server rendered the same English — which is worth knowing before anyone reaches for `suppressHydrationWarning` here: the defect was the wrong language, not a disagreement. |
| `scripts/audit-untranslated.mjs` + `npm run audit:untranslated` + `src/lib/__tests__/untranslated-audit.test.ts` | **THE OTHER HALF OF i18n, WHICH NOTHING COULD SEE (2026-09-10, DESIGN_SYNC row 388; owner: "Proceed").** `i18n-parity.test.ts` asks whether every `t()` CALL has a fallback and whether the dictionaries agree with it. It is blind by construction to **the string that was never passed to `t()` at all**: to that test, a page with one translated word and forty hardcoded ones looks finished. Both facts were true at once on 2026-09-10 — every member-facing key existed in all eleven locales (row 386) while `/app/me/notifications` still rendered "Me · Settings" and "Notifications" as literal JSX — which is exactly why the backlog item kept reading as nearly done. **The backlog's "21 pages, 22 components" was a memory, not a measurement**, and it is wrong: the first honest run reads **181 strings across 38 files**, and the `--max=` ratchet starts there. Advisory for now, for the reason `audit:spacing` is advisory — a gate at a number nobody has agreed to pay is a gate people switch off. **The admin console is excluded BY NAME as well as by directory.** It ships in English by decision (every `admin*` namespace is untranslated on purpose), it is roughly three times the size of the member-facing surface, and `AdminAdsClient` lives in `src/components/` rather than `src/components/admin/` — one file in the wrong folder would otherwise put console strings in a member-facing count. **A bracket-only regex cannot tell JSX from a comparison, and that is the failure mode to guard.** `index >= 0 && index < queue.length` and `state === 'fail' ? …` both open on a `>` and close on the next `<`, so the first draft reported `"= 0 && index"` and `": state === 'fail' ?"` — and the first list containing something like that is the last one anyone reads carefully. Both are excluded, **both were found by sampling the output rather than by reading the code**, and `untranslated-audit.test.ts` pins them so the next heuristic change cannot quietly reintroduce them. Comments are masked with the shared `mask-comments.mjs` first, for the reason that module exists. **It reports files and lines, never a translation**: whether a string should be wrapped is a judgement about what the sentence is for — a field label is a control's name, an eyebrow is metadata — and this script has no opinion about that. **AMENDED the same day — 175 → 144, and the tranche taught that the audit sees a SUBSET (DESIGN_SYNC row 389; owner: "Proceed").** `MmmFullPlayer` and `MmmMusic`, the two live music surfaces, showed 31 findings between them and needed **77 keys**: the same components also carried English in `setNote(...)` calls, in template `aria-label`s (which the script skips by design), in `emptyChartMessage`'s six sentences, and in the `RADIO_FILTERS`/`CHART_DATASETS`/`CHART_SCOPES` label constants — none of which a bracket-only scan can reach. **Fix the file, not the finding.** Three rules the pass applied: label constants translate AT THE DRAW keyed on their English (`radioFilterLabel`/`chartLabel`), because `t(entry.labelKey, entry.label)` is invisible to `extract-i18n-keys.mjs` and therefore to the applier; **`t` must never enter a callback that has to keep a stable identity** — `RadioTab`'s `openStation` is a registered play intent, so its error state holds `{ title, reason }` and the render does the wording; and a sentence that interpolates a lowercased English label into itself (`No {label.toLowerCase()} station…`) cannot be translated at all and was rewritten to name the filter by the chip above it. Sentences naming other tabs reuse that locale's own `mmmStrip.*` tab name rather than the English one. **AMENDED again — 144 → 110, and THE FIGURE IS A FLOOR (DESIGN_SYNC row 390).** The TEXT rule needs its capture on ONE line, so JSX text broken across lines is invisible: `MmmMissing`'s own "Back to the map" was untranslated for the life of the component and was found by reading the file, not by running this. Say "at least" when quoting the number; teaching it to span lines will RAISE the count and must not land in a pass that is also paying the count down. **Two more false-positive classes are closed**: `() => Promise<void>` reads as ">Promise<" (the arrow gives the `>`, the generic the `<`), so the character BEFORE the match is tested — and **only `=`**, because the obvious wider set `=!<->` would silently suppress every `<>fragment</>`; a test writes one to a scratch dir and asserts it still reports. A bare domain is excluded too, since `ihype.org` is the one domain this product may name. **A test must never write a probe into `src/`** — the first one did, `wiring-guards.test.ts` walks that tree, and it failed only under parallel execution while passing in isolation; `--roots=` exists so the probe lives in `tmpdir`. **`MmmMissing` takes a `kind`, not a `title`/`body`**: seven call sites each carried their own English, which is how its no-such-tab copy came to list the Library tab five weeks after Library was retired. It renders only when `getLocale() !== 'en'`, says the English text governs, and **offers help understanding the document** — the mitigation for shipping something somebody cannot read, and it has to stay true, so `admin@ihype.org` must keep being answered. **NOT LEGAL ADVICE, and the limit is recorded rather than buried**: "English governs" is the standard, defensible choice for a US-incorporated, invite-only alpha in Portland, Maine, and it is **not** sufficient everywhere — Quebec's Charter of the French Language, France's Loi Toubon and several EU member states require consumer-facing terms in the local language, and a governing-language clause does not displace a consumer-protection statute. The privacy policy already OFFERS GDPR and CCPA rights, so those readers are being addressed now; **re-open this with a lawyer before marketing into those jurisdictions.** **Two mechanical traps.** The notice's own sentence was first written as a MULTI-LINE `t(...)`, which `extract-i18n-keys.mjs` cannot see — so the one string whose entire purpose is reaching a non-English reader would have silently stayed English and the applier would have refused its translations: **a wrapped `t()` is an untranslatable `t()`.** And the charter's revenue section was the only one of fourteen written as inline JSX rather than a `SECTIONS` entry, which is precisely why the audit saw it and not its thirteen siblings; A redirect to an anchor anywhere in this shell needs the same. The public `/info` hub is untouched and keeps its six tabs. **RESOLVED 2026-09-25 — THERE IS ONE LEGAL TEXT (DESIGN_SYNC row 524; owner: "Combine legal of both together").** Until then `/info?tab=terms|privacy` carried its own translated prose and this document its own English clauses, and nothing decided which bound. The response now says what happens (an asking price on file, nothing listed, nobody contacted) and **the modal says it BEFORE the button**, which is the half that matters: the old copy was only ever read after the member had already committed. The control is "Register a resale price", not "List for resale", because the product cannot list. **Transfer is the working way to hand a ticket on and the copy points at it.** Do not restore the listing language without a listing. **(2) Three notification toggles that controlled nothing.** `radioLive`, `crateUploads` and `bookingRequests` were written by Settings, echoed by `/api/me`, and **read by no sender anywhere** — `sendMarketingEmail` consults only the four general fields. `crateUploads` now gates the publish-scheduled notice and `bookingRequests` the booking-request notice, so both do what their label says. **`radioLive`'s row is GONE and its column is KEPT**: it read "When a show you follow goes live" and no show can go live — DJ show creation was retired and the always-on station is never announced — so it has no sender and could not have one; the column stays because live shows remain a stated product intention. **Restore the row with the feature, not before.** Both new gates fail towards SENDING — an unreadable preference or a member with no row still gets the notice — because the notification arriving is the point of the feature and a failed read must never silently mute an inbox. **(3) The booking toggle was hidden from half its recipients**, gated on `role === 'VENUE'` while the demand radar is a venue writing to an ARTIST — the identical type-gate bug row 383 fixed in the inbox itself. **Gate a control on ownership or on who receives the thing, never on profile type.** **(4) The advertise page sold co-funding that does not exist**: a "Co-op handling · 0%" receipt line at $0.00, "fans with a referral link can co-fund coverage for artists they back", and "referral-funded spots split billing across backers automatically". No co-funding, backer or split billing appears anywhere in the advertise API or the ad libraries — one advertiser pays one sponsorship on one card.
