| `src/lib/ad-pricing.ts` + `Ad.pricingModel` + `sponsorshipRefundableCents()`/`planSponsorshipSettlement()` in `src/lib/ad-settlement-plan.ts` + the term picker in `src/components/AdvertisePage.tsx` + the sponsorship branch in `src/lib/ad-clip-selection.ts` and `POST /api/ads/impression` | **ADVERTISING IS SOLD BY THE MONTH, NOT BY THE IMPRESSION (2026-09-10, DESIGN_SYNC row 384; owner: "Let's move to your suggestion and we can always adjust if we go to scale").** **The storefront and the delivery engine had been quoting two prices that disagreed by 480x.** The Coverage Builder sold "spots per day" — LOCAL at $0.15 a spot a day against a stated 800 impressions, a **$0.19 CPM** — while `POST /api/ads/impression` decremented the budget by a flat **9c an impression, a $90 CPM**. Both numbers were fiction in the same direction: a break airs every 15 minutes with two spots (`station-breaks.ts`), so the station makes **eight impressions per listener-hour**, and filling ONE LOCAL buyer at the quoted rate needed 100 listener-hours a day (GLOBAL, 5,250). The buyer took the loss twice over — a $45 local campaign quoted 240,000 impressions, delivered 500, hit its budget, **went dark for the rest of its run and was refunded nothing**, because a metered campaign that exhausts `spentCents` is filtered out of every break while its `endsAt` is still weeks away. **So the unit is TIME.** `SPONSORSHIP_MONTHLY_USD` is LOCAL $25 · REGIONAL $60 · NATIONAL $150 · GLOBAL $300, terms of 1/3/6/12 months at `DAYS_PER_SPONSORSHIP_MONTH` = 30. **$25 is the floor because Stripe's 2.9% + $0.30 is 8.9% of a $5 sponsorship and 4.1% of a $25 one.** There is deliberately **no prepay discount**: a flat rate makes the pro-rata refund exactly `paid x unused/term`, which is a sentence a sponsor can check, and a term discount stays available as a lever later. **Impressions are the DELIVERY REPORT, never the meter** — the impression route increments `impressions` and leaves `spentCents` alone for a sponsorship, and `viableAdsForScope` applies its budget filter only to `METERED` rows. **Do not reintroduce a spend check for a sponsorship**: going dark mid-term is precisely the failure this model exists to remove. Nothing else had to change on the serving side, because `resolveWeightedAdBreakClips` already orders by `impressions: asc` and therefore already shares airtime equally among live sponsors — **only the storefront ever thought in CPM.** **`Ad.pricingModel` (`METERED` by default, additive migration) exists because the planner cannot infer it.** A metered campaign that delivered nothing is identical to a sponsorship on every other column, and it is owed a **full** refund where the sponsorship is owed **none**; every campaign sold before this date keeps its old settlement untouched. `sponsorshipRefundableCents()` is **exported on purpose** — the advertiser's dashboard quotes it as "Refund if cancelled" BEFORE the sponsor presses the button and `planSponsorshipSettlement()` pays it after, and the figure they read is the one they will hold you to. "Spent" and "Budget remaining" are metered words and are hidden for a sponsorship, which would otherwise read $0.00 spent and a full budget remaining on a term that had run to its last day. **NOT BUILT, and it was part of the recommendation: a sponsors-per-city cap.** `Ad` and `AdSlot` carry no geo column, so LOCAL is a tier NAME and not a place — every LOCAL sponsor shares one rotation, and the tier oversells nothing only because nothing is promised. A real cap needs a city concept in the schema first. **WHEN TO REVISIT.** Per-impression pricing becomes viable once a month's listener-hours are large and steady enough that a CPM is a promise you can keep; until then CPM's failure mode is overselling the local businesses this platform most needs to keep. **AMENDED 2026-09-11 (DESIGN_SYNC row 394) — THE PRICE CHANGED AND FOUR SURFACES WENT ON SPEAKING THE OLD MODEL.** Gating the per-campaign rows was done; the same page's four SUMMARY tiles, the cancel confirm and the settlement email were not, so the only customers the product has read "Spend $0.00", "Effective CPM $0.00" and "budget remaining" for a term they had paid in full — and a sponsor refunded for cancelling on day one was told their campaign had underdelivered. **`spentCents` NEVER MOVES FOR A SPONSORSHIP: any figure derived from it is $0.00 forever, and any sentence containing "spent", "budget" or "CPM" must be behind a `pricingModel` check.** `describeSettlement` takes the model as its fourth argument for exactly this reason and every metered sentence is byte-identical, pinned by a test. `npm run audit:retired-claims` is the gate that now catches the next one. |
| `src/lib/stripe.ts` (`createAdCampaignCheckoutSession`/`settleAdCampaign`) + `src/lib/ad-settlement.ts` + `src/lib/ad-settlement-plan.ts` | **SUPERSEDED IN PART 2026-09-10 — a SPONSORSHIP settles on unused DAYS, not on an unspent budget; read the `ad-pricing.ts` row above before changing any of this.** The charge-up-front mechanics below are unchanged and still govern the legacy `METERED` campaigns. **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-02 — campaigns are CHARGED UP FRONT and the unspent remainder is REFUNDED (owner: "Do your suggestion it makes sense", after the security sweep found the flaw below).** The pre-auth model described in the rest of this row put a manual-capture HOLD on the card and captured the spend at the end of the run — but a card authorisation lives about seven days, runs go to ninety and a pause was unbounded, so every campaign longer than a week delivered its impressions and then could not be billed at all. Now `createAdCampaignCheckoutSession` sets no `capture_method` (the whole budget is captured when the advertiser pays, like a ticket), the campaign goes live on **`payment_intent.succeeded`** with `metadata.adId` (platform account, `amount_received` covering the budget — `stripe-webhook-guards.ts`), and `settleAdCampaign` reads the intent and follows **`planAdSettlement()`** (pure, tested): a `succeeded` intent is refunded `budget − spent` (everything under Stripe's 50c minimum — we delivered too little to bill for; a remainder under 50c is kept because Stripe cannot refund it), a `requires_capture` intent from before this change is still captured or released. **A campaign PAUSED for 60 days is settled as cancelled** (`PAUSED_CAMPAIGN_SETTLE_AFTER_DAYS`) so unspent money never sits on iHYPE's balance without an end date. `describeSettlement()` is the one place the advertiser's sentence comes from, and it says refunded or charged according to what Stripe actually did. **Refunds are transparent as of later the same day (owner: "Be transparent about the refunds", DESIGN_SYNC row 340)**: settlement persists `Ad.settledChargedCents`/`refundedCents`/`stripeRefundId` through `settlementRecord()` (cron and cancellation alike), the advertiser dashboard reads them back as Paid · Spent · Refunded with the date, the `5–10 business days` window (`REFUND_WINDOW_BUSINESS_DAYS`, the single source every surface quotes) and the Stripe refund reference, the cancel confirm names the dollar amount about to come back before the advertiser agrees, and the settlement email carries the same sentence under a Settlement label. A campaign settled before the columns existed renders a dash, never $0. **Cost accepted**: Stripe keeps its processing fee on a refund, so a campaign that spends nothing costs iHYPE about 3% of the budget — accepted at alpha volume rather than adding a non-refundable minimum. The `amount_capturable_updated` webhook branch is LEGACY for a session issued under the old model and completed after the deploy; do not delete it until no `requires_capture` ad intent is open. **The original design, kept for what it explains about the columns:** Real ad-spend billing — pre-auth-then-capture (2026-07-19, DESIGN_SYNC row 234), explicit user decision. Closes the gap flagged since row 224: campaigns previously ran on a purely notional budget, no Stripe charge anywhere. A vetting-cleared campaign now lands in `AWAITING_PAYMENT`, not `APPROVED` — a Stripe Checkout Session (`capture_method: 'manual'`) authorizes the full quoted budget via Stripe's own hosted page (no client-side Stripe.js/Elements exists anywhere in this codebase, so this needed no new UI dependency). On successful authorization (`payment_intent.amount_capturable_updated` webhook) the campaign flips to real `APPROVED` and `startsAt`/`endsAt` are resolved from the stored `runDays` only then — a campaign awaiting review/checkout never loses run length to the wait. The new `ad-settlement` cron (shares `show-payouts`' daily 1pm UTC slot) captures only the actual `spentCents` delivered once a campaign's `endsAt` passes (capped at `budgetCents` — the impression route's non-atomic check-then-increment can drift `spentCents` a few cents over), or releases the hold outright if nothing aired; early self-serve cancellation runs the identical settlement. `PATCH /api/admin/ads`'s manual-approval path routes through the same flow. New `Ad.runDays`/`stripePaymentIntentId`/`authorizedAt`/`settledAt` fields. |
| `src/lib/ad-vetting.ts` (`vetAdAudioContent`) | **Rewritten as a real 2-layer ad audio scan that fails to a human, not open (2026-07-28).** Layer 1 is acoustic fingerprinting via `identifyAudio()` (`src/lib/acrcloud.ts`) — **the same ACRCloud account the track-upload scan already uses, so this added no vendor.** It exists because the transcript layer structurally cannot enforce its own first policy bullet: Whisper transcribes *speech*, so a commercial track playing as a music bed under a voiceover never reaches the transcript, and no language model of any size can see it from text. A fingerprint match routes to manual review rather than rejecting (the opposite of `runAcousticFingerprintScan`'s handling for track uploads) — an advertiser may hold a real sync licence for the bed in their own spot, and nothing automated can tell that from an unlicensed rip. Layer 2 is the existing Whisper → Llama transcript screen. `combineAdAudioLayers()` is a pure, tested folder of the two. **The bug this fixes:** every failure path used to return `isApproved: true` — no AI binding, unparseable JSON, and "no speech detected" all cleared the spot, and under pre-auth billing clearing is what moves a campaign to `AWAITING_PAYMENT` and authorizes the budget on Stripe. A Workers AI outage therefore silently cleared every ad submitted during it, and a music-only spot (the shape most likely to carry unlicensed music) took the *cleanest* path through vetting. Now: any layer that flags or is unsure → `PENDING`; if no layer actually inspected the audio (`inspectedContent`), that is also `PENDING`. Only an outright policy violation rejects. `POST /api/advertise/campaigns` no longer swallows an audio-fetch failure either — unreadable audio is manual review, not a pass. **Note the deliberate asymmetry:** `vetTrackAudioContent` (`media-vetting.ts`) stays fail-open, because uploads are high-volume and layer 1 covers the same ground, whereas clearing an ad spot has money attached. |
| `src/lib/ai.ts` (`runTranscription`) | **Returns a `TranscriptionOutcome` discriminated union (`ok`/`no-speech`/`unavailable`), not `string \| null` (2026-07-28).** The old signature collapsed "the model heard no speech" into the same value as "the binding is missing" and "the call threw", which is what made the ad-vetting fail-open bug above unfixable at the call site. Also caps input at `MAX_TRANSCRIBE_BYTES` (3 MB): Whisper takes the audio as an array of byte values (~8 bytes/element in V8), the ad-audio upload route accepts up to 10 MB, and an unbounded `Array.from()` could allocate ~80 MB against the Worker's hard **128 MB** memory ceiling — a crash, not a fail-open. 3 MB is ~3 min of 128 kbps MP3, so ad spots are never truncated; a full track may be, which is fine for a lyrics check. |

Wired into `src/app/api/profile/upload-graphic/route.ts` (avatar/hero/logo/gallery — raises `ContentReport` `targetType: 'profile-image'` on flag). `src/app/api/verify/route.ts` (ownership proof documents) is deliberately left un-AI-vetted — it already requires mandatory human review via `/admin/verifications`. |

Until that date the Overview PATCHed a second route (`/api/admin/content-reports/[id]`, deleted) with REVIEWED · RESOLVED · DISMISSED · HIDDEN — three status flips and a HIDDEN table that disagreed with this one (a show CANCELED with paid orders and no refund; a track "hidden" by turning off free-use consent) — and `/admin/review`'s Resolve and its bulk Resolve set RESOLVED and removed nothing. A report leaving the queue means ACTIONED (removed by this route) or DISMISSED (an operator looked), nothing else; do not add a second route or a third word. |

Categories with fewer than 5 reports (`K_ANON_FLOOR`) are folded into "Other" so a small bucket can never identify a specific user or piece of content. Distinct from `/transparency` (financial stats) and `/status` (system health, admin-gated). |
| **Retired and dropped:** `AdSubmission` "Supporter" ad system (text + optional image banner tiles) | iHYPE only ever runs radio-style audio ad spots — no visual/banner placements. `src/components/AdBanner.tsx`, `src/app/api/ads/upload/route.ts`, `src/app/api/ads/[id]/impression/route.ts`, `src/app/api/ads/[id]/click/route.ts`, and `src/app/api/ads/stripe-webhook/route.ts` were deleted outright — investigation found this pipeline had **zero live submission entry point** (no form anywhere ever called `ads/upload`) and `AdBanner`'s only mount point (`/shows/[slug]`) has been removed, so nothing broke. The `AdSubmission` Prisma model has since been **removed from schema.prisma**, with a hand-written drop migration (`prisma/migrations/20260714020000_drop_ad_submission`) — explicitly flagged in the migration's own comment as **not safe to apply blind**, since this sandbox can't confirm the table is actually empty in production; confirm row count and get owner sign-off before running it. `src/app/api/cron/route.ts`'s `expire-ads` job (and its entries in `workers/cron.ts`/`wrangler.cron.toml`) were removed along with it. The real, live ad system is the `Ad`/`AdSlot` self-serve Coverage Builder (`/advertise`, `src/app/api/advertise/campaigns/route.ts`) — audio-only, `audioUrl` now **required** to submit a campaign (`src/app/api/advertise/audio-upload/route.ts`), wired to real playback via `src/lib/ad-clip-selection.ts` + `getStationState()` (see the ad-interjection rows below; the `GET /api/radio/ad-clips` route this line used to name was deleted with `RadioShowCreator`), with both the campaign copy AND the audio spot itself now AI-vetted (`vetAdAudioContent()` in `src/lib/ad-vetting.ts` — see the ad-audio-scan row below). `/admin/ads` reviews these real campaigns (`AdminAdsClient.tsx`, `src/app/api/admin/ads/route.ts`) instead of the retired `AdSubmission` queue, and `/advertise/dashboard` shows a 14-day daily impressions breakdown (no clicks/CTR — `Ad.clicks` is real schema but never incremented anywhere, an audio spot has no click-through UI the way a banner did, so showing a permanently-0 stat was removed rather than left misleading). Advertisers get email on every status change (`src/lib/ad-campaign-notify.ts`'s `notifyAdvertiser()`, called from both the initial AI vetting in `POST /api/advertise/campaigns` and an admin's manual decision in `PATCH /api/admin/ads`) and can self-serve cancel/pause/resume a running campaign (`PATCH /api/advertise/campaigns` with `{action: 'cancel'|'pause'|'resume'}`, `CampaignCancelButton.tsx` on the dashboard) — `CANCELLED`/`PAUSED` are `AdCampaignStatus` values set only by those actions, never by vetting; resuming shifts `endsAt` forward by however long the campaign was paused (`Ad.pausedAt`), so a pause never eats into the paid-for run length. `audioUrl` accepted by `POST /api/advertise/campaigns` must pass `isTrustedStorageUrl()` (`src/lib/object-storage.ts`) — a CodeQL-flagged SSRF (the route used to `fetch()` a client-submitted URL unvalidated for audio vetting) fixed same-day after the finding landed on an already-merged PR. `Ad.imageUrl` (dead field — ads are audio-only, nothing ever read it) was dropped the same way as `AdSubmission`: removed from `schema.prisma`, with a gated, unapplied drop migration (`prisma/migrations/20260714050000_drop_ad_image_url`) since historical rows from before this session's changes may have set it. |

## CRITICAL: UI source of truth

**Every page's UI already exists as a .dc.html file in Claude Design.**
Claude Code must NOT invent, redesign, or guess any UI.

The workflow is:
1. Read the current `.tsx` file to understand what's already wired
2. Open the .dc.html file listed for the page
3. Update only what changed — translate new HTML/CSS/React into the existing `.tsx`
4. Keep all data fetching, auth, and API wiring intact
5. Push — do not change layout, copy, colors, or components without a new .dc.html version

If a UI detail is unclear → ask Claude Design to clarify in the .dc.html. Never guess.

**THE SOURCE OF TRUTH IS `design/design-system-8/` — iHYPE Design System 8
("Bulletin", vendored 2026-08-09, DESIGN_SYNC row 274). It is the ONLY design
source.** It absorbed both earlier bundles, and on 2026-08-11 those bundles were
**deleted** — `design/design-system-app-shell/` and `design/handoff-music-map-me/`
are gone from the repository, not archived. They had been "kept as history", and
history on disk gets read: code comments cited them as their source, and every
few sessions something got rebuilt from the older of the two. If a question
seems to need them, it is answered in DS8 or it is not a design question.

**AMENDED 2026-08-20 — there are now THREE copies of the design system on disk
and the live one is not on disk at all.** In order of staleness:

| Copy | Last updated | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| **Claude Design, project `0a104bf9-bc92-45f4-aa81-48b29a6b9a93`** | live | **The actual source.** Reach it with the `DesignSync` tool (`list_files` / `get_file`), not by reading a directory. |
| `design/handoff-console-2026-08-21/` | 2026-08-21 | **The current pull, and the only on-disk copy of the console direction as the design system states it** — README, ADHERENCE, MOBILE, the route map, eight token files, six console templates, `_ds_bundle.js` and `component-source/`. The seven components under it are the source `src/components/ds/` is generated from; see the row in the wiring table below before trusting a value in it. |
| `design/design-system-8/` | 2026-08-14 | The vendored snapshot the audits scan. Predates the console. |
| `.claude/skills/ihype-design/` | 2026-08-10 | A *second* vendored copy, behind the first, loaded by `/ihype-design`. |

**The 2026-08-21 bundle is the freshest on disk and is still not simply
correct — it disagrees with production, and with itself, in four measured
ways.** Its `tokens/colors.css` re-lightens the four role hues and every one
**fails AA on cream** (3.03–4.22:1 against the shipped 4.50–6.36:1), so the
shipped tokens are ahead and that file must not be adopted. Its components write
type as inline `px`, 21 sizes of it below the 15px content floor the same bundle
publishes. Its components hardcode `#ff5029` rather than reading `--accent`. And
its README replaces the arc nav with a rotary knob while its own ADHERENCE rule 6
still names the logo trigger and arc as the shell's only chrome. Freshest is not
the same as authoritative: measure, then decide which side is right.

This matters because **the two on-disk copies disagree with production and with
each other**, and a session that reads either one and applies it faithfully will
repaint the app in DS8 navy. The skill copy is the worse trap: its `readme.md`
still lists a **DJ role**, promoter as a role, Syne/DM Sans, and `--bg-base:
#0a0805` — a ground two conversions old.

**The live project is NOT stale — it is ahead of both.** Its `tokens/console.css`
was generated by reading production at `86cb473` and every value matches
`globals.css` exactly (verified token-by-token, 2026-08-20). It also carries
`RETRO_HIFI_DIRECTION.md`, `tokens/v9-retro.css`, a `components/shell/` set and
**`PORT_TO_APP.md`**, a real change list against this repo that nothing on disk
contains.

So: **pull before you trust a template.** The 53 findings from `audit:design` are
measured against the 08-14 snapshot, not the live project, and `PORT_TO_APP.md`
states the DJ role has since been removed from the design system — so that number
is a floor on history, not a current count. Re-vendor and re-run before quoting it.

**SUPERSEDED IN PART, 2026-08-19 — the console direction.** The owner
directed the app to be rebuilt to the hi-fi console prototype ("I want the app
to look like that mockup"). That is a live instruction and it outranks the two
lines below that say the overhaul required "nothing structural" and that the
shell geometry is frozen. The chrome IS being restructured: the trigger and
the player sit on a walnut cabinet, and a tuner dial occupies the band between
them (`design/console-2026-08/`, and the amendment at the top of
`SHELL_LOCK_2026-08-08.md`).

What survives from those two rules, and what matters more than either: the
shell's figures are load-bearing on each other. Both bugs that came out of
adding the dial were a dependent nobody re-derived — the pill hung 181px past
the cabinet at 1100px, the dial overlapped the mini player at 393px, and
neither was visible in the source. **Re-derive downstream, then measure at
393px and at a desktop width.** Do not read "superseded" as "free to nudge".

Read these three files, in this order, before touching any surface:

1. `design/design-system-8/SYNC_AUDIT_2026-08-07-overhaul.md` — the drift table
   and, in "What the code should change", the *complete* list of what this
   overhaul asked of the codebase. Its last line is the important one:
   **"Nothing structural. The shell's structure was already correct — this
   overhaul brought the design system up to the code, not the reverse."** If
   you find yourself restructuring a component to satisfy DS8, re-read it.
2. `design/design-system-8/SHELL_LOCK_2026-08-08.md` — the app shell's geometry,
   **signed off by the user as final**. Every figure is load-bearing on the
   others and several were arrived at by fixing a specific bug. Change one and
   re-derive the rest; do not nudge one in isolation.
3. `design/design-system-8/ROUTE_TEMPLATE_MAP.md` — which template governs which
   route. This replaces guessing from the page map further down.
4. `design/design-system-8/MOBILE.md` — added 2026-08-11. **iOS and Android are
   not a separate build**: `templates/simplified-app/` IS the app, Capacitor
   wraps that exact page, so a phone bug is a CSS bug here (the same point
   `mobile-fit.css` makes from the code side). Design at **375px** first — 327px
   of content after the pane padding. **One breakpoint, 620px**; do not invent a
   second, and note it is NOT `ARC_NARROW_MAX_WIDTH`, which is 720 in
   `ArcNav.jsx` and governs the arc table alone. **44×44 minimum on every
   control, desktop included** — grow vertical padding, never font size. **`dvh`,
   never `vh`.** Every native capability needs three designed states (primer →
   OS prompt → denied fallback that still works), asked at the moment of use and
   never on launch. The four `guidelines/mobile-*.card.html` specimens carry the
   same rules visually.

**What DS8 changed in this codebase (all of it, 2026-08-09):** the ground moved
from warm near-black to ink navy (`--bg` `#0a0805` → `#0b1220`) and the ink from
warm cream to cool off-white (`--ink` `#f0ebe5` → `#eef1f6`); display type moved
Syne → **Bricolage Grotesque** and body DM Sans → **Work Sans**; four token
names were added (`--radius-card`, `--radius-trigger`, `--opsz-*`,
`--leading-*`) and the shadows were re-anchored to the navy ground. **Every
token NAME is unchanged**, which is why this was a value-only edit and why
`mmm.css` needed nothing: it names no hex.

**SUPERSEDED, 2026-08-19/20 — DS8's navy ground is no longer what ships.** The
console conversion moved `--bg` to warm cream `#f0dfb8` and `--ink` to `#1c1408`,
and re-anchored the four role hues for a light ground (see Brand constants
below). The paragraph above is kept because its *method* still holds — a
ground move is a value-only edit precisely because every token NAME stayed put,
and that is why the console conversion was also a value-only edit. What does
not hold is its last sentence, which used to say the role hues were "unchanged —
that is what the brand carries across the overhaul". Only `--accent` `#ff5029`
carried; the role hues moved, because a hue chosen to sit on navy fails on cream.

Three rules from DS8 that the code follows and you must not undo: **no emoji
anywhere** (Unicode glyphs only), **promoter is not a role** (never put it in a
role picker), and **there is no DJ role** (deleted 2026-08-06).

**The Music · Map · Me shell is built and live at `/app`** (DESIGN_SYNC row 268):
`/app/map`, `/app/music/[tab]`, `/app/me`, `/app/me/[panel]`. `MmmShell` is
mounted in the **`/app` layout** on purpose — the map is the base layer and must
survive navigation between modules, and a layout is the only place the App Router
guarantees that. `AppShell` stands aside for `/app/*`, and since 2026-08-11 that is the ONLY
surface it stands aside for. **The cutover is done.** `/listen`'s six-module
deck is retired (`/listen` is a forward into `/app/music/discover`), and so is
the phone swipe shell that kept `/listen`, `/shows` and `/pages` permanently
mounted behind a quick grid. Three shells for one signed-in experience is what
DS8's route map and MOBILE.md between them rule out: there is no `/listen`, and
"there is no separate mobile build" because `templates/simplified-app/` IS the
app at every width. Two shells remain and they do not overlap — MMM at `/app/*`,
the legacy app shell everywhere else a signed-in member goes.

**SUPERSEDED 2026-09-05 — THE DEFAULT LOOK IS APPLE MUSIC iOS, and the console is a THEME (owner: "Make design like Apple Music iOS style. Keep all the same functionality"; DESIGN_SYNC row 346).** Read the paragraph below for what the bar carries and where its geometry lives, both of which are unchanged; read it as history for what the bar is MADE of. By default it is iOS's translucent tab bar over a white ground with SF Pro and Apple Music red — no walnut, no brass, no grain. Every material is a token (`--dock-*`, `--plate-*`, `--pill-*`, `--*-texture`, `--map-*`) that resolves to `none` on the default and to the console PNGs on `street`/`metal`/`flowery`/`classical`, so the cabinet still exists for anyone who picks it. `dark` is Apple Music dark. **Do not paint a material into `mmm.css` again — set the token in globals.css and let the themes decide.** The console paragraph:

**The chrome inside `/app/*` is ONE walnut dock and nothing else (2026-08-22,
DESIGN_SYNC row 289, owner: "I don't want any previous design, retire it …
Bottom hifi nav system is the only thing I want").** That instruction is still
live and the bar still obeys it — one bottom bar on walnut, and no other
persistent chrome anywhere in the shell. **What it carries changed on 2026-09-04
(the MIDDLE ROAD, row 341): four labelled tabs and a mini player, not a knob, a
dial and a joystick.** This line used to name `RotaryNav` · `TunerDial` ·
`JoystickTransport` as mounted from `src/components/ds/`; nothing has mounted
those three since 2026-08-24 (the dc.html superseded them) and now nothing draws
their shapes either. The logo trigger, the radial arc, the nav hint, the scrim,
the player pill, the phone mini-player and the artist highlight are **deleted
files**, and `guard:design` asserts they stay deleted — a retired control comes
back one piece at a time, and each piece looks reasonable on its own. The same
now goes for the hardware: `e2e/mmm-shell.spec.ts` asserts `.mmm-knob`,
`.mmm-hifi-dial`, `.mmm-stick`, `.mmm-gate` and `.mmm-dock-badge` render zero
nodes. Anything in this file that describes the arc, the trigger or the pill is
history; so is anything describing a knob or a dial. The paragraph below about
"MMM's arc nav already carries MAP" is kept only because the invariant it states
(no LISTEN destination may resolve to `/listen`) is still live — **the Listen tab
carries it now, and there is a fourth destination, TICKETS**.

**What IS cut over, since row 273: the way in and the way around.** `/` and
sign-in resolve to `WORKBENCH_PATH = '/app/map'`, and the four LISTEN
destinations in `app-nav.ts` plus `MobileBottomNav`'s Listen tab point at
`/app/music/*`. That closed a trap worth understanding before touching either
shell: MMM has no Events or Pages module, so it links out to `/tickets`,
`/pages` and `/shows/[slug]`, which render in the LEGACY shell — and while every
nav destination still pointed back at `/listen?tab=…`, one tap on a ticket
dropped a member into the six-module deck (now retired) **with no route back for
the rest of the session**. The shell was live, was the landing surface, and still nobody saw
it; it was reported as "I still see older versions". The invariants: no LISTEN
destination may resolve to `/listen` — it is a redirect and is out of the route
registry entirely (asserted in `app-nav.test.ts`) — and any
new MMM→legacy bridge needs a legacy→MMM counterpart or it re-opens the door the
other way. **Do not add a MAP row to the drawer to solve this** — the drawer's
structure is the app-shell handoff's and is asserted as such; MMM's arc nav
already carries MAP. Events, Pages, advertise and Account keep legacy routes on
purpose (no MMM equivalent), and `/app/me/settings` is a bridge MENU of links
rather than the settings UI, so it is not the Account destination.

**The three token scales that still disagree under the same name.**
`--radius-lg`, `--ease` and `--duration-slow` mean different things in the
design system and in `globals.css`, and DS8 **deliberately did not reconcile
them** — its own note says renumbering them "would add drift rather than remove
it", because 19 live call sites depend on `--radius-lg` being 18px here against
the system's 10px. Assuming a `--radius-*` name means the same thing in both
places is silent, not an error. This is exactly why DS8 added `--radius-card`
as a NEW name rather than moving the ramp: reach for `--radius-card` and
`--radius-pill` in new work and leave the sm/md/lg/xl ramp to the surfaces
already built on it. **`--radius-card` is `3px`, not the 18px this line used to
state** — the console conversion made new work near-square, because a machined
panel has a cut edge and an 18px round-rect reads as a phone app icon. The
sm/md/lg/xl ramp is still 18px at `--radius-lg` and is still deliberately
unreconciled, so the trap this paragraph describes is unchanged.

**One follow-up DS8 names and this codebase does not yet do:** the 46
re-anchored templates got colour and type, not layout. The Bulletin direction's
card radius, whitespace and editorial hierarchy are applied in the app shell;
the remaining surfaces still carry their old layout. That is a real backlog
item, not an oversight — do not treat a marketing page's spacing as drift.

## Infrastructure (already configured — do not reconfigure)

- **Database:** Supabase Postgres at `db.bjkabtzvgfshsrmjhrkx.supabase.co` — 99 migrations in `prisma/migrations`, all tables exist.
- **The database is backed up by us, not by Supabase — there is no PITR and no vendor daily backup on this plan.** `.github/workflows/backup-database.yml` writes an encrypted `pg_dump` to R2 on a 6-hourly schedule that GitHub delivers in **8 hours or less, measured** (`npm run check:backup-cadence`, nightly), and the production deploy takes another one immediately before any pending migration. Those objects are the only copy outside the live cluster; see the `scripts/backup-database.mjs` row in the wiring table above, and `docs/runbooks/backup-restore-drill.md` for the drill that proves they restore. **Do not add a restore procedure that starts at the Supabase dashboard** — that is what this replaced, and the button it named does not exist without paying for it.
- **A single failed migration stops ALL production deploys — this happened, and went unnoticed for a day.** On 2026-07-27 a session applied `20260726120000_add_advertiser_category_pitch` out of band via the Supabase MCP (see DESIGN_SYNC row 250), which is exactly what the `apply_migration` de-allowlisting below exists to prevent. CI later re-ran the same file through `prisma migrate deploy`; `CREATE TYPE "AdvertiserCategory"` hit the type that already existed and failed with SQLSTATE **42710** (duplicate_object), leaving a `finished_at IS NULL` row in `_prisma_migrations`. From then on every deploy failed with **P3009** at the migrate step — and because the Cloudflare deploy, cron deploy, smoke test and cache purge all come *after* it in the job, they were skipped. **Production shipped nothing for a day** while three PRs merged green. Recovery: confirm the DDL really is in the database, then run the **"Resolve a failed migration"** workflow (Actions tab, `workflow_dispatch`) with the migration name — it calls `scripts/resolve-failed-migration.mjs`, which refuses to act unless `prisma migrate status` itself reports that migration as failed. The deploy workflow now detects P3009 and fails immediately with that instruction instead of retrying a permanent error three times behind a generic message.
- **Every migration in `prisma/migrations/` is applied to production automatically** (as long as the migrate step is passing — see the row above)**.** `.github/workflows/deploy-production.yml` runs `prisma migrate deploy` on every push to `main`. There is no manual apply step and never was. A "DO NOT APPLY BLIND" comment in a migration header does **not** gate anything on its own — three migrations carried such comments and all three shipped on merge, including two that drop data (`20260714020000_drop_ad_submission`, `20260714050000_drop_ad_image_url`) and `20260727120000_enable_rls_all_tables`. Earlier revisions of this file described the first two as unapplied; that was wrong.
- **There is a second, vendor-managed schema in this database: `stripe`.** It is the Supabase **Stripe Sync Engine** (~25 synced tables — `charges`, `invoices`, `payment_intents`, … — plus its own `stripe._migrations`), installed outside this repo and not referenced by any application code: iHYPE talks to Stripe through the Node SDK (`src/lib/stripe.ts`), never through these tables. Do not add it to `schema.prisma` and do not assume changes to it survive — the sync engine runs its own migrations and may `CREATE OR REPLACE` its functions at any upgrade.
- **Supabase advisor findings are worth reading twice before acting.** The 2026-07-28 run reported `public.rls_auto_enable()` as executable by `anon` "via `/rest/v1/rpc/rls_auto_enable`". The grants were real, but the function `RETURNS event_trigger`, and PostgreSQL refuses to invoke those outside the event-trigger manager — verified against production: `ERROR: 0A000: trigger functions can only be called as triggers`. So it was a wrong grant, not a reachable path. It was revoked anyway (`20260728210000_harden_function_grants_and_search_path`), along with pinning `search_path` on the three `stripe` trigger helpers. The revoke does not stop the `ensure_rls` event trigger firing: EXECUTE on a trigger function is checked when the trigger is created, not each time it fires — verified on a local Postgres fixture before shipping.
- **A migration that touches objects it may not own must fail soft.** `ALTER FUNCTION`/`REVOKE` need ownership, and the four functions above are owned by `postgres` while migrations run as whatever `DIRECT_URL` authenticates as. A permission error would leave a failed row in `_prisma_migrations` and **block every subsequent production deploy** — far worse than the warning it was clearing. That migration therefore wraps each statement in an exception handler that degrades to `RAISE WARNING`. The cost is that a green deploy no longer proves it applied: re-run the Supabase advisor to find out.
- **First real use of the gating workflow:** `prisma/migrations-pending/20260728201000_drop_ad_clicks` (drops `Ad.clicks`, dead since the banner-ad system was retired — an audio spot has no click-through, and `/admin/ads` was rendering a permanent `0 clicks` beside a real impression count). The field is already out of `schema.prisma` and out of the UI, so the *code* half is deployed; the column drop waited for someone to confirm `SELECT count(*) FROM "Ad" WHERE "clicks" <> 0` is 0 and then `git mv` it across. **That happened — `migrations-pending/` no longer holds it, and the workflow has now run end to end twice.** The second was `20260906000000_drop_feature_flag_table_and_dead_columns` (row 353): parked with six numbered checks, the counts read from production in one query on 2026-09-06 (all zero), then moved across in its own commit. **Two things that pass make the round trip worth copying.** The counts are written INTO the migration header rather than into a chat message, because the next reader cannot re-run a check against the database as it was that night — a verdict alone ("verified, all zero") is unfalsifiable a week later. And the header **cannot name the gate marker**: `check-gated-migrations.mjs` matches the header text case-insensitively, so writing the marker while recording that it was cleared re-gates the file and fails CI, which is exactly what happened on the first attempt. Say "the gate marker", never the marker.
- **To gate a migration, park it in `prisma/migrations-pending/`** — Prisma never reads that directory. `npm run guard:migrations` (`scripts/check-gated-migrations.mjs`, wired into CI and into the production deploy immediately before the migrate step) fails the build if a migration marked `@gated` sits in `prisma/migrations/`. Applying one is a deliberate `git mv` back, in its own commit. See `prisma/migrations-pending/README.md`. **AND THE STEP BETWEEN HAS ITS OWN GUARD AS OF 2026-09-15 (DESIGN_SYNC row 490): `src/lib/__tests__/drop-migration-counts.test.ts` fails when a migration carrying a `RUN <date> against production` block drops a table or column that block never names.** The header states the exact query; whoever runs it can run a DIFFERENT one, and that is what happened here — the eight-count query in `20260914180000_drop_columns_nothing_reads` was hand-rewritten to seven and came back all zero, with `SocialPost.postedAt` never asked about. **A seven-of-eight answer reads precisely like an eight-of-eight one.** So: run the query the header states, verbatim, and record the NUMBERS under the real identifiers — the recorded block is matched against the SQL's own `DROP` statements, and query aliases (`with_password_hash`) match nothing. The guard reads comment lines and statement lines SEPARATELY and judges only from the recorded-run marker onward: its first version searched the whole header, found the example query it quotes, and passed the exact omission it was written for — the scanner-reads-its-own-prose trap for the sixth time, and the reason `mask-comments.mjs` exists. Both failure modes were driven before the guard was believed.
- **A CAPABILITY IS A MEASUREMENT, NOT A NOTICE — CALL THE TOOL BEFORE REPORTING IT UNAVAILABLE (2026-09-15, owner: "ensure these kinds of misses don't keep happening").** A session-start reminder said the Supabase connector needed re-authorising; it was relayed to the owner as fact, twice, and it was wrong — one `list_projects` call returned the iHYPE project immediately. Org-level "Connected" and a session's own notice are two different readings, and the notice is the one that goes stale. The same rule in its second form is the migration-count defect above: **run the query that was specified, verbatim; do not reconstruct it from memory.** A paraphrase that drops a column, a check that reports a condition it never tested, and a capability declared dead without a call are one defect — a claim asserted from a secondary source where a primary one was available in a single step. This file is full of the consequences (`user.ip` attributing a caller it cannot see; a "still needs the operator" line sending the owner to redo finished work; a named guard that did not exist for a day while the sentence made the next reader stop looking). Verify against the thing itself, then report.
- **The Prisma MCP server is REMOVED — do not re-add it (2026-08-06).** `.mcp.json` used to register `prisma` (stdio, `npx prisma mcp`). It exposed exactly three tools and earned nothing: `Prisma-Studio` is a local UI and useless in a remote session, `migrate-status` was the only one allowlisted, and **`migrate-dev` is actively dangerous** — it writes a new migration straight into `prisma/migrations/`, the directory that auto-deploys on every push to `main`, and can reset or drop the database it points at, bypassing the `migrations-pending/` gating workflow entirely. Carrying a server whose one useful tool is a status read, and whose second tool can drop the production database, is a bad trade; the whole entry is gone. **Write migrations by hand into `prisma/migrations-pending/`, and read data through the Supabase MCP** — the Prisma one never had a query tool at all, which is why it could not answer a single question anyone actually asked of it. Note this removes only the MCP *server*: the Prisma ORM and Prisma Migrate are load-bearing (309 files import `@/lib/db`; 109 migrations deploy on every push) and are not going anywhere.
- **Do not add Prisma's *remote* MCP server (`https://mcp.prisma.io/mcp`) either — it is a different product.** Prisma ships two MCP servers; the local one is removed per the bullet above, and this is the other. The remote one is the management API for **Prisma Postgres**, Prisma's own hosted database product, and authenticates via OAuth to a Prisma Console workspace on first use. Its tools (`CreateDatabase`/`DeleteDatabase`/`ListDatabases`, `CreateBackup`/`ListBackups`/`CreateRecovery`, `Create`/`Delete`/`ListConnectionStrings`, `ExecuteSqlQuery`, `IntrospectSchema`, `search_prisma_documentation`) act on databases inside that workspace. iHYPE's database is Supabase Postgres behind Cloudflare Hyperdrive — there is no Prisma Postgres instance for any of those tools to reach, so the only thing connecting it would add is an OAuth-gated, write-capable database surface (`ExecuteSqlQuery`, `DeleteDatabase`) sitting outside both the `read_only=true` pin below and the `migrations-pending/` gating workflow above. Note also that adding it the documented way — claude.ai → Connect apps → **+ Add integration** — installs it as a per-account connector, so it would **not** appear in `.mcp.json` and this repo would have no record of it; that is why the decision is written down here. If iHYPE ever actually moves onto Prisma Postgres, revisit this bullet.
- **REVISITED 2026-08-06 — a Prisma Postgres instance now exists, and the bullet above is out of date on that one point.** A **`Prisma Compute Deploy`** check started appearing on pull requests at 12:22 UTC that day (first seen on PR #648; nothing in this repo was changed to cause it). It runs `prisma migrate deploy` against a Prisma Postgres database at **`db.prisma.io:5432`**, replaying every migration in `prisma/migrations/` — 109 of them at the time of writing — into a Prisma Console workspace (`console.prisma.io/cmmmd9o5u04g2yye9iy9mkv4k`). **Nothing in this repository references it**: not `prisma.config.ts`, not `.mcp.json`, not any workflow. It is an account-level GitHub integration, which is precisely the "this repo would have no record of it" failure mode the bullet above predicted. Consequences worth knowing: (1) there is now a **second database** receiving this schema, so "the database" is ambiguous — production is still Supabase behind Hyperdrive, and `src/lib/stripe.ts`-style app code never talks to Prisma Postgres; (2) anything moved out of `prisma/migrations-pending/` into `prisma/migrations/` will be applied **there** as well as in production, so the gating workflow now gates two targets; (3) its failures are **not** production deploy failures — the first one (PR #650) was `P1002`, a 10-second advisory-lock timeout, i.e. a transient concurrency fault on their side, and it had passed twice before. **Do not treat a red `Prisma Compute Deploy` as a P3009-class blocker** — check `_prisma_migrations` in Supabase before concluding anything about production. If this integration is not wanted, it is removed from the Prisma Console workspace or the repo's GitHub App settings, not from this codebase.
- **The Supabase MCP server is pinned to `read_only=true`** in `.mcp.json`, which makes Supabase run every query as a read-only Postgres user — enforced at the database, not just by withholding tools. `mcp__supabase__apply_migration` was **removed from the `.claude/settings.json` allowlist** for the same reason `migrate-dev` was never added: it applies DDL straight to the production database, bypassing the `migrations-pending/` gating workflow and version control entirely. Schema changes belong in `prisma/migrations/` in git, deployed by CI — never through an MCP tool call. Do not drop `read_only=true` or re-add `apply_migration` to make a write "just work"; write the migration file instead. **`read_only` is scoped to SQL only** — Supabase documents it as "execute all queries as a read-only Postgres user", and says nothing about the non-SQL tools. That is why the URL also pins `features=database,debugging,development,docs`: the omitted groups (`account`, `branching`, `functions`) carry write operations — project management, branch create/delete, `deploy_edge_function` — that run through the Management API rather than Postgres, so the read-only role cannot be assumed to cover them. The four kept groups supply every allowlisted tool (`execute_sql`/`list_tables` from `database`, `get_advisors` from `debugging`). Widening `features=` re-opens a write path that `read_only` does not close.
- **Cloudflare Worker:** `ihype` — deployed, secrets already set
- **Hyperdrive:** `03f39c51f80a45d3bb6792a9676e292e` — pooled Postgres connection
- **KV namespace:** `b6330641874a4420b240d3a82760a9aa` — runtime flags
- **Storage budgets scale from KV, not from a deploy (2026-09-02, owner: "Make storage expansions quick to scale").** `src/lib/media-limits.ts`: defaults in code (60 MB a file, 1 GB and 100 tracks a profile), overrides under `limits:media_file_mb` / `limits:media_profile_gb` / `limits:media_profile_tracks` in the runtime KV namespace, read on every upload. `npx wrangler kv key put --remote --namespace-id b6330641874a4420b240d3a82760a9aa limits:media_profile_gb 5` raises every profile to 5 GB on the next upload; deleting the key restores the default. **The per-file cap has a ceiling the override cannot lift (60 MB)** — the upload route buffers the whole file in the Worker's 128 MB isolate to sniff, measure and scan it; past that the fix is a streaming route, not a bigger number. **What it costs (R2 pricing as read from Cloudflare's docs the same day):** storage $0.015 per GB-month, writes $4.50 per million, reads $0.36 per million, egress free, with 10 GB-month, 1 M writes and 10 M reads free each month; the Worker is on the $5 Paid plan (`cpu_ms = 1500` needs it), which includes 10 M requests. So a 60 MB lossless upload costs about a tenth of a cent a month to hold and half a thousandth of a cent to write; a profile's whole 1 GB budget full is 1.5 cents a month; a hundred full profiles are about $1.35 a month after the free 10 GB; a terabyte is about $15. A play is one read plus one Worker request, both inside the free and included tiers until the site is far past alpha. Storage is not where the money goes. **Project Galileo (approved 2026-09-02):** puts the ihype.org zone on the Business plan at no charge. Its page lists WAF, rate-limiting rules, unmetered DDoS mitigation, CDN, Zero Trust application access and Gateway, and names Workers; it does not name R2, so the R2 figures above stand until the account's billing page says otherwise, and whether the $5 Workers Paid subscription is still charged is a dashboard question this file cannot answer. Two things it makes worth doing are now CODE rather than dashboard clicks: `scripts/cloudflare-edge-guards.mjs`, run by the **"Cloudflare edge guards"** workflow (`workflow_dispatch`, dry run unless `apply` is ticked, idempotent), upserts two WAF rate-limiting rules (Business allows five) — 120 requests a minute per IP on `/api/auth/*` + `/api/register` and 600 on `/api/analytics/track`, both sized as a FLOOD backstop above the app's own buckets so a venue's shared wifi is never locked out of signup — and a Cloudflare Access application on `ihype.org/admin` (pages only, never `/api/admin/*`, which the pages fetch with the app session) allowing `admin@ihype.org` by one-time PIN, as a second gate ahead of `isAdminSession()`. **Whether the edge carries them is decided by the workflow's last run, not by this file**: the sandbox has no Cloudflare credential, so the first apply is the operator's click, the deploy token may need Zone WAF:Edit and the two Access permissions the script names on failure, and the Access half exits 3 until the account has a Zero Trust organization (a one-time team-name choice the script refuses to make). Record the first successful apply here. **APPLIED 2026-09-02 22:14 UTC** (workflow run 33688827005, attempt 2, after the deploy token gained Zone WAF:Edit + the two Access permissions and `CLOUDFLARE_ZONE_ID` was added to the `Production – ihype` environment; the first attempt answered `403 10000 Authentication error` on the ruleset read, which is what a token without the zone's WAF scope looks like): the ruleset was created with both rules, the Zero Trust organization is `ihype.cloudflareaccess.com` with the One-time PIN provider, and the Access application "iHYPE admin" (`852d0c58-ec2f-4743-9dba-5d7098299bf2`) fronts `ihype.org/admin` for `admin@ihype.org` with a 24h session. A dry run a minute later reported every item unchanged, so the script is idempotent against the real account. Opening `/admin` now shows Cloudflare's PIN page before the app's own sign-in. **The second apply, on 2026-09-03 after `staff@ihype.org` became the second administrator, FAILED and the script was wrong**: it POSTed a new allow policy beside the existing one and Cloudflare refused with `12130 access.api.error.invalid_request: policy precedences must be unique` — both carried precedence 1. It now **rewrites the policy it owns in place** (matched by name, `PUT` at the precedence it already holds) and only creates when there is none, at one past the highest in use. Two allow policies on one application would have been the wrong shape even had the API taken it: the single-address policy would have survived beside the pair, and removing an administrator later would mean deleting a policy rather than editing one. A policy someone wrote by hand is still never touched. **RE-APPLIED 2026-09-03 02:37 UTC** (workflow run 33708506692, from `main` once the fix merged in #805): both WAF rules unchanged, and `✓ rewrote the allow policy` — the Access application now admits **admin@ihype.org and staff@ihype.org**. A dry run a minute later (run 33708554238) reported `= an allow policy already admits admin@ihype.org, staff@ihype.org`, so the rewrite path is idempotent against the real account too. The edge gate and the app gate now name the same two people.
- **R2 bucket:** `ihype-media` — media storage, reached through the Worker's **`R2` binding** (`wrangler.toml`'s `[[r2_buckets]]`). **There are no `R2_*` credentials and none are needed** — `wrangler secret list` shows 22 secrets on the live Worker and not one is an R2 credential, which is why `src/lib/object-storage.ts`'s old S3/SigV4 path was dead from the day it was written: profile images, cover art and verification documents were being base64'd into Postgres, and ad-audio upload answered 503. It uses the binding as of 2026-08-31 (DESIGN_SYNC row 322), the same way `src/lib/r2.ts` always has. Objects are served by **`src/app/cdn/[...key]/route.ts`**, which allows **public prefixes only** (`profile/`, `artist-media/`, `ads/`) — a key is a bearer token, so that list is a decision about who can read what. **Verification documents deliberately never enter R2**: they are identity/ownership proofs, and an edge rule for `/cdn/*` may exist in the Cloudflare dashboard where this project's routes live, so the allowlist alone is not a sufficient guarantee. Do not reintroduce the credential path. **Media written before that fix is still base64 in Postgres**; `GET /api/cron?job=media-backfill` measures it and `&apply=1&limit=N` moves it (`src/lib/media-backfill.ts`, DESIGN_SYNC row 323) — dry-run by default, idempotent, never dropping an inline copy before the R2 write lands, and cascading the denormalized `FanPlaylistItem`/`FanFavoriteMedia` copies by `mediaId` rather than re-uploading. It is a cron job rather than a script **because the binding only exists inside a Worker**. It skips `verificationProofUrl` permanently.
- **There is no minimum number of uploads for alpha (owner, 2026-09-02).** `ALPHA_CONTENT_TARGETS` used to require ten playable tracks and `/api/health` blocked on it; the count is still REPORTED under `alphaReadiness.content.playableTracks` so an empty station is visible, but it is information, not a gate. The checklist line changed with it. Do not put it back on the reasoning that the station needs music — the owner's call is that the doors do not wait for uploads.
- **Launch seed:** Already ran June 23 — demo accounts exist
- **Admin accounts:** `admin@ihype.org` (the ONLY email this product uses for contact, sending, or bootstrap) and, **as of 2026-09-03 by owner instruction ("staff@ihype.org is the 2nd"), `staff@ihype.org`** — the second administrator the alpha readiness gate and the release checklist require. `src/lib/admin-allowlist.ts`'s `DEFAULT_ADMIN_EMAILS` names both, and both locks read it: the jwt clamp in `auth.ts` and `isAdminSession()` in `permissions.ts` (which deliberately ignores the `ADMIN_ALLOWED_EMAILS` override, so a code change was the only way to add a second admin). Eligibility is not the role: the `staff@ihype.org` User row still has to carry `role = 'ADMIN'` (no route grants it — `POST /api/admin/setup` bootstraps `admin@ihype.org` only, and this sandbox's Supabase connector is read-only — so the owner sets it in the Supabase SQL editor after the account registers) and the account has to register an admin device through `/admin/device-register`, reached by the link `POST /api/admin/device-reissue` emails to a signed-in administrator. The Cloudflare Access application in front of `/admin` names the same two addresses (`ADMIN_EMAILS_DEFAULT` in `scripts/cloudflare-edge-guards.mjs`, which now takes a comma-separated `EDGE_GUARD_ADMIN_EMAIL`); the edge gate and the app gate must always name the same people, or an administrator the app accepts is stopped at the PIN page — re-run the edge-guards workflow with `apply` after this merges so the policy admits both. The line that follows about the personal Gmail address is history and still holds. Until 2026-08-13 this line named a personal Gmail address instead; there was no such ADMIN row, so anything keyed on it silently matched nothing. The personal address was removed from the repo entirely on 2026-08-14 (tests now use a reserved `example.com` fixture) — do not reintroduce it, in code, tests, fixtures or docs. `admin@ihype.org` and `staff@ihype.org` are the only addresses `src/lib/admin-allowlist.ts` accepts.
- **`npm audit` reads 0 vulnerabilities as of 2026-09-06, and three `overrides` are what keep it there — read `docs/dependency-advisories.md` before touching them.** All three long-standing findings (`deepmerge-ts < 8` and `mysql2 <= 3.23.0` via the Prisma CLI, `uuid < 11.1.1` via `@capacitor/cli` → `xcode`) were closed by pinning the vulnerable transitive dependency FORWARD, because both parents pin theirs to an exact version and npm's only offered fix was a **major downgrade of a working build tool** — Prisma 7 back to 6 against 127 migrations, Capacitor 8.5 back to 8.4 against the `cap sync` every mobile CI job runs. **`npm audit fix --force` is still the wrong command here.** **This file and that document both used to argue against exactly these pins, and the reasoning was wrong in a way worth keeping**: the objection to `uuid@^11` was that a v7→v11 major jump into a CommonJS consumer would surface as a failed mobile build. That was a PREDICTION where a measurement was available — uuid 11 still ships a CJS entry and still exports `v4`, `xcode` calls `uuid.v4()` and nothing else, and `npx cap sync` on both platforms settles it in seconds. Each pin was driven rather than argued: `xcode.generateUuid()` against the real `project.pbxproj`, and `prisma migrate deploy` applying all 127 migrations to a scratch Postgres 17 — the command whose failure blocks every later deploy with P3009. **An `overrides` entry is a standing liability**: it holds a dependency at a version its parent never tested against, silently, after the reason is gone. Remove each one — do not bump it — when the parent ships the fix itself; the document names the release to wait for. **CI still audits production dependencies only** (`npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high`) and that is deliberate: a gate over the dev set goes red the next time a build tool picks up an advisory whose only fix is a downgrade, and a check nobody working here can clear is a wall in front of the instruments.
- **Dependencies are swept weekly and the sweep is written down: `docs/dependency-upkeep.md`.** A Routine fires Mondays 15:00 UTC in a fresh session and walks npm, the six claude.ai connectors, plugins, the SHA-pinned GitHub Actions and Dependabot. **It REPORTS; it does not push** — an unattended dependency commit is how a Monday morning starts red. Three things from that document are worth carrying here because each has already cost someone. **(1) A caret next to an exact pin of the same product is a drift generator**: `next` is pinned exactly while `@next/bundle-analyzer` carries a caret, and `@sentry/cloudflare` is pinned while `@sentry/nextjs` carries one — so a bare `npm update` lands 16.3.3 beside 16.3.4 and Sentry 10.72 beside 10.73. Check for the sibling before moving either. **(2) npm's `Latest` column is a DOWNGRADE for two packages here** and taking it would be the bug: `next-auth` reads "latest 4.24.15" against the 5.0.0-beta.32 this product runs, and `@types/bcryptjs` reads "latest 2.4.6" against 3.0.0. Never treat that column as a target without comparing it to what is installed. **(3) `maplibre-gl` is the bump with teeth** — the build copies two files out of its `dist/` into `public/vendor/`, and a rename there is the map going blank for every member with nothing in CI able to see it (row 350). Re-run the vendor step from an empty directory, confirm the copied worker is JavaScript and not an HTML error page, and find it in `.open-next/assets/` before believing a green build. Majors are never part of a sweep: `@simplewebauthn` 13→14 sits on the only way into an account, `vitest` 4→5 under 1,179 tests, and `prisma` 8 is a release candidate. Also note `cap sync` and `cf:build` each regenerate tracked files (three native project files; `public/sw.js`'s commit-stamped cache version) — revert them, CI regenerates its own.

---

## Brand constants (never change these in code)

- **Contact:** admin@ihype.org (only email)
- **Site:** ihype.org (only domain)
- **Founded:** Portland, ME · 2026
- **Split:** **75% artist / 25% venue / 0% iHYPE, of the face value AFTER Stripe's card fee comes off the top** (owner, 2026-09-25: "Take fee off the top for sure. It should be a split POST fee between artist/venue", choosing 75/25 and "No sales until onboarded"; DESIGN_SYNC row 520). It replaced 70/20/10. **There is no promoter share any more**: a HYPE link still records who referred a sale and earns nothing, and promoter was never an account type either. The buyer pays face value plus tax and nothing else — no processing line, no protection reserve. **The venue is the merchant of record on every sale** (a direct charge on its own Stripe account: its name on the statement, its disputes, its refunds, its tax to remit), and the purchase route refuses a sale with 409 `VENUE_NOT_PAYMENT_READY` until the venue can take a charge; the destination-charge and platform-settled modes are deleted for new sales, and orders sold under them still refund and pay out by their recorded `settlementMode`. iHYPE claims only the artist's 75% as the application fee and pays it out after the show. The fee is a standard-rate ESTIMATE (`stripeCutOf`) fixed when the session is created, so an Amex or international card leaves the venue a few cents under 25%. The constants are `ARTIST_SHARE_PERCENT`/`VENUE_SHARE_PERCENT` in `src/lib/ticketing.ts`; every surface computes from them (never from `Show.*PayoutPercent`, which a migration moved to 75/25/0). Do not reintroduce a promoter share, a buyer-paid processing line, or a non-venue merchant without the owner.
- **Money terms are disclosed and ACKNOWLEDGED before an artist or venue can be paid** (owner, 2026-09-25: "TOTAL transparency … We simply don't have the staff to do this"; DESIGN_SYNC row 521). `MoneyTermsDisclosure` states every fee, refund and cancellation cost, chargeback, tax duty and payout timing, with a worked ticket computed from the purchase route's own arithmetic; it renders open on the recruiting pages, the create-page card, both wizards and every Connect control. `POST /api/stripe/connect/onboard` refuses an artist or venue profile without the current `MONEY_TERMS_VERSION` (400 `MONEY_TERMS_REQUIRED`) and audits `money_terms_accepted`. **Change a sentence's meaning → bump the version**, so everyone is asked again; a new control that starts payouts must render the disclosure and send the version (`money-terms-callers.test.ts` finds every caller).
- **The only age rule is 13+ at signup** (owner, 2026-09-25: "remove the 18+ requirement from the entire site"; DESIGN_SYNC row 522). Nothing asks for 18+ — not purchase, resale, HYPE links or referral credit — and `audit:retired-claims` refuses the copy. A show's own 18+/21+ door policy is the venue's and stays. The `User.isEighteenOrOlder` drop is parked in `prisma/migrations-pending/`.
- **Ticket tax** — the VENUE's jurisdiction, never the buyer's: admission is taxed where the show happens and the venue remits it as merchant. `calculateTicketTaxes()` charges the venue's US state sales tax rate plus that state's population-weighted AVERAGE local rate, from the Tax Foundation's "State and Local Sales Tax Rates" as of 2026-01-01 — the workbook is committed at `data/tax/`, `npm run tax:import` derives `src/lib/tax/us-sales-tax-rates.json` from it (integer parts per million, a negative local average clamped to 0), and a unit test fails when the two disagree. No federal line (the US has none) and no tax outside the US. It is an ESTIMATE on a receipt: it does not know a state's admissions exemption, a separate amusement tax or a city's own admissions tax, and the money terms say the venue confirms the rate. To move to a new year, replace the workbook and re-run the import; never edit the JSON by hand. **A venue can set its own rate (2026-09-25, owner: "Build per venue rate override"; DESIGN_SYNC row 524)**: `Profile.ticketTaxRatePpm`, set in the profile editor's Event Info section, REPLACES the table entirely — the whole amount on `stateCents`, `localCents` 0, `rateSource: 'venue'` — and works outside the US. `0` is a confirmed exemption, `null` means use the table, and anything outside 0–25% (`MAX_VENUE_TAX_RATE_PPM`) is ignored by the calculation as well as refused by the editor schema and a DB CHECK constraint, so a bad value degrades to the table rather than charging it.
- **No video** — iHYPE does not host video, live streams, or recorded video. Audio only.
- **There are no radio shows** — the feature is deleted (2026-09-11, owner: "remove all references to radio shows (this was the old feature that we've deleted)"). This line used to read "DJs can go live (audio-only) and shows auto-save for on-demand replay" and was the single most load-bearing stale claim in this file: it is in BRAND CONSTANTS, which is the section a session treats as settled, so every pass that met a radio label read this and left it. Listening is the **always-on station** (`radioStation.ts`) and the **computed stations** (`stations.ts`). `Show.isRadioShow`, `RadioShowTrack` and `NotificationPreference.radioLive` are out of `schema.prisma`; the column drop is **APPLIED** — it moved across in #909 (`31c4bb13`) once the three counts were read from production and every one came back zero. **That sentence used to end "and `prisma/migrations-pending/` now holds nothing but its README", which stopped being true on 2026-09-14** — three gated drops are parked there now (passwords/MFA, the aux queue and four other writer-less tables, eight columns nothing reads), each carrying the counts to run. The directory is the one place this file may not describe by memory: `audit:doc-paths` excludes it by design, because it empties and refills, so **read `ls prisma/migrations-pending/` before repeating any claim about what is in it.** **This line said the drop was parked with its counts unrun until 2026-09-13, four days after it shipped** (found by a full audit run, when `prisma migrate deploy` against a scratch database applied it). A parked-migration line is a claim with an expiry date and nothing in CI re-tests it: check `prisma/migrations/` before repeating one. `npm run audit:retired-claims` refuses the vocabulary.
- **Ground:** **white `#ffffff` · ink `#0a0a0a` · accent Apple Music red `#e0263e` with WHITE ink on it — the Apple Music iOS look is the DEFAULT as of 2026-09-05 (owner: "Make design like Apple Music iOS style. Keep all the same functionality"; DESIGN_SYNC row 346).** The line that follows describes the console cream ground it replaced; that ground now survives only as the tint the four character themes lay over their own textures. Warm cream `#f0dfb8` · ink `#1c1408` (the console conversion, 2026-08-19/20) WAS the default ground. **SUPERSEDED IN PART, 2026-08-24 — member-selectable themes are back by owner instruction** ("Can you add different themes to the app? Like dark, flowery, street, metal, classical?"): five `[data-theme]` token blocks in `globals.css` (dark/flowery/street/metal/classical), picked in ME → Settings → Accessibility, persisted through `AccessibilityProvider` + the pre-paint bootstrap in `layout.tsx`. The line this replaces said "no theme switcher, no `[data-theme]` block" — that rule's REASON survives as the mechanism: every value in a theme block is measured (`npm run audit:contrast` walks every block), the ink/bg RGB triplets and the whole `--ink-a*` ramp are restated per block, the three dark themes take the DS8 role hues (console hues are dark-on-dark there), and — until 2026-08-26 — **the walnut dock, brass and parchment chart did not theme**, on the reasoning that they were "the machine, not the room". **THAT IS REVERSED** by owner instruction ("Themes should adjust console graphics, map graphic, buttons, and fonts as well (for each of the theme options)"): every theme now carries its own cabinet (`--walnut`/`-2`/`-3`, `--brass`, `--brass-deep`, `--lamp`), its own chart (`--map-filter`, `--map-rule`, `--map-vignette`, `--map-blend`, tokenised in `mmm.css` so the filter ORDER — darken before sepia, or a near-white tile clips to white — lives in ONE place rather than five), its own `--radius-panel`, and its own display/body type pairing. **The four photographed textures are re-TINTED, never replaced**: the same walnut, brushed, brass and grain PNGs take each theme's colour through the tokens layered over them, which is why theming the machine cost no new assets — five swapped texture sets would have been 20 files against a budgeted Worker bundle. **Type is drawn only from the four faces already loaded** (Bricolage, Work Sans, JetBrains Mono, Instrument Serif) in five distinct pairings; a sixth family would cost a webfont download on every page to serve one theme. **Each cabinet's `--ink-on-walnut` ramp is COMPUTED against that cabinet** at the base ramp's own ratios (10.87 / 7.61 / 5.51), and `audit:contrast` now measures the ramp in EVERY block rather than only `:root` — it read the cabinet from `:root` alone, which was right only while the cabinet did not theme, and left alone would have gone on passing while five themed cabinets carried any ink at all. Because the blocks are pre-merged over `:root`, a theme that restates the cabinet and forgets its ink resolves to base cream on steel and fails; inheriting is not a defence. 'console' is the absence of the attribute; `html.high-contrast` is still **not a theme** and beats any theme on specificity.
  - This line named ink navy `#0b1220`/`#eef1f6` and "light theme `#f4f6fa`/`#101725`" until 2026-08-20. That was DS8, and DS8's ground is gone. Kept as a correction because a session that trusts it paints a navy surface on a cream board.
- **Colors:** accent `#ff5029` (unchanged, and the one thing that did carry across) · venue `#0f6b62` · **promoter `#a3175f`** · fan `#5b3d8f` · advertiser `#8a5a06`. **The role hues DID move in the console conversion, and this line said the opposite.** Every one was darkened to stay legible on a light ground; measured on cream they are venue 4.83:1, promoter 5.61:1, fan 6.36:1, advertiser 4.50:1 — all clear AA, which the DS8 hues did not.
  - The retired DS8 values were venue `#22e5d4`, promoter `#ff3e9a`, fan `#b983ff`, advertiser `#ffb84a`. Two of those hexes are still live under **different** tokens — `--success` is `#22e5d4` and `--warning` is `#ffb84a`, both as FILLS — so finding the hex in the codebase does not mean you have found a role.
  - Promoter colours the 10% slice in split bars only. It is **not an account type**. `--role-dj` is gone from `globals.css` entirely; this file described it as a live deprecated alias, and it is not one.
- **Fonts:** Bricolage Grotesque (display/headlines) · the SYSTEM face (body and UI: `-apple-system`/SF Pro Text/Segoe UI/Roboto) · JetBrains Mono (labels/mono) · Instrument Serif (editorial). **The display/body SPLIT is the decision, and this line named Work Sans as the body face for weeks after it stopped being one** — the iOS skin moved body to the system stack in row 346 and took display with it by accident, so from then until 2026-09-18 the default theme had NO Bricolage anywhere and Work Sans survived only inside three `[data-theme]` blocks. Body is the system face ON PURPOSE (it is what makes a row and a control read as the platform's own); display is Bricolage ON PURPOSE (it is where the product sounds like itself). Work Sans is still loaded and is still the body face of the street, flowery and classical themes. Bricolage is loaded as the **variable** face with its optical-size axis (`axes: ['opsz']` in `layout.tsx`) — pinning a static instance throws away the axis the family was chosen for. Syne and DM Sans are retired.

Its promise cards restated the charter and its funding section restated the transparency panel; the story timeline and team blurb had no equivalent and were dropped with the page (`git show 49f2ecff^:src/app/about/page.tsx`). |

Three sections lived only on the standalone page and moved across: "How iHYPE makes money" (as `legalPage.charter.processingFeeBody`), "Promoters and the 10%", "What actually makes this real" — plus the split bar, the callout treatment and the contact footer. The count-up animation was **not** carried across: it ran off an IntersectionObserver, and inside a tab panel that starts hidden a missed observer leaves the split reading `0% artist · 0% venue · 0% promoters`. `CharterAnimated.tsx` was deleted. |
| Legal.dc.html | /info | src/components/InfoTabs.tsx (rendered by `src/app/info/page.tsx`) — **the single public-information hub (2026-07-29)**: Trust & Safety, Transparency, Privacy, Terms, Charter, DMCA. `/legal`, `/privacy`, `/terms`, `/transparency`, `/audit`, `/about` and `/charter` are all thin redirects into the matching `?tab=`, kept because those URLs appear in signup consent copy, the cookie banner, emails and app-store listings. **`/legal` forwards its `?tab=`** — it used to drop it, so the cookie banner's "privacy policy" link and the homepage's "read the full charter" link both landed on Terms of Service. **InfoTabs syncs its tab from `searchParams` via an effect**: arriving at `/info?tab=x` from elsewhere on `/info` is a soft nav, so the `useState` initialiser never re-runs and the tab silently stayed put. `/info` stays a **server** component so the two data-driven panels keep running their Prisma aggregates; only the tab strip is client-side and receives them as rendered slots (same pattern as the `/payouts` hub). |
| Privacy.dc.html | /info?tab=privacy | **No file** — `/privacy` is a `redirects()` entry in `next.config.mjs`. The policy renders inside `InfoTabs`. |
| Terms.dc.html | /info?tab=terms | **No file** — `/terms` is a `redirects()` entry. The signed-in copy is `src/components/mmm/MmmLegal.tsx` at `/app/me/info/terms`; see DESIGN_SYNC row 407 for why there are two texts and which question that leaves open. |

**Reversed (2026-07-19):** the per-role dashboard pattern that Home.dc.html/Studio.dc.html were retired for (see below) is back, by explicit design/product decision — Claude Design's new `templates/` tree (distinct from the legacy `uploads/iHYPE/*.dc.html` set) ships a real `artist-dashboard`/`dj-dashboard`/`venue-dashboard`/`fan-dashboard` template per role. These are NOT a revival of the old role-switching Home.dc.html single mockup — each is its own route, reachable as a "Dashboard" link from that profile's Pages toolkit (`PageRoleModules.tsx`) rather than being the post-login landing page; `/app/map` is the default landing surface (`WORKBENCH_PATH`). Built 2026-07-19: `src/app/artists/[slug]/dashboard`, `src/app/promoters/[slug]/dashboard` (DJ), `src/app/venues/[slug]/dashboard`, `src/app/me/dashboard` (fan, any authenticated user) — all owner-gated except the fan one, all real Prisma aggregates (no fabricated stats), no schema changes. `src/lib/artist-dashboard.ts`/`src/lib/venue-dashboard.ts` hold the month/week-scoped aggregate helpers that `getProfileInsights` didn't already cover.

**Retired:** Studio.dc.html (the generic creator workbench) is gone — `/studio` is now a bare `redirect('/app/map')` with no auth gate, no `StudioDashboard` component, and nothing in the app links to it. Its former responsibilities live in MMM's MUSIC module (discovery/radio), EventCreator (event creation), and `/radio` (the always-on station).

**Retired:** Home.dc.html (the role-switching Fan/Artist/Venue/DJ single-dashboard mockup) has no live route — the Workbench/Home dashboard it depicted was superseded by the Listen/Events/Pages tab architecture; `/home` is now just a `redirect('/app/map')` alias for old bookmarks/links. Its Fan section's responsibilities live in MMM's MUSIC module; its Artist/Venue/DJ sections live on Pages' role toolkit (`PageRoleModules.tsx`) and each role's own profile page. Its role-switching *pattern* is back (see "Reversed" note above) but as separate per-role routes, not a single mockup page.

**Retired (2026-07-13):** Beta.dc.html was deleted from Claude Design — no design source exists for a standalone `/beta` marketing page anymore. `/beta` is now a bare `redirect('/register')`, matching the Studio.dc.html precedent; its former invite-code CTA content is fully covered by `/register` itself.
